===== Boston College =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 83 | ACC PREDICTION: 16 | 2025 RECORD: 2-10, 1-7 ACC

OFFENSE Boston College will look to reverse a horrific 2025 season in
which head coach Bill O’Brien’s squad went 2-10. While the team got hit

with an avalanche of major injuries, there were problems up and down the
roster.

The Eagles lost two of their key weapons with running back Turbo
Richard (Indiana) and wide receiver Reed Harris (Arizona State) leaving
via the portal. BC added Great Lakes Intercollegiate Athletic Confer-
ence Player of the Year QB Mason McKenzie out of Div. II Saginaw
Valley State; he is the presumed starting quarterback. Running back Evan
Dickens arrives from Liberty, and a half-dozen offensive linemen, many
of whom are in position to start, have transferred in.

Tight end Kaelan Chudzinski (24 receptions, 313 yards, four IDs) was

a revelation last season, and if he continues to develop, he could become

one of the best tight ends in the ACC.
DEFENSE On defense, KP Price, BC’s leading defensive player from ’25

returns, giving BC a hard-hitting and smart safety who can focus on his
position instead of being moved around because of injuries.

The Eagles also did a very solid job rebuilding their edge rusher po-
sition in the portal, adding Kris Jones (Georgia) and Demetrius Ballard
(Buffalo). O’Brien also turned to the portal to fill his linebacker spots,
adding three LBs who figure to get major snaps this year: Bodie Kahoun
(Notre Dame), Anthony Palano (Washington State) and Justin Medlock
(SMU).

SPECIALISTS ‘The special teams unit returns arguably BC’s best player in
2025 — placekicker Luca Lombardo, who connected on 16-of-17 field-
goal attempts. FCS Towson transfer Bryce LaFollette is likely to be the
starting punter, while starting defensive backs TJ Green and Isaiah Farris
are slated for return duties.

FINALANALYSIS O’Brien’s coaching staff is almost completely revamped,
with veteran coach Ted Roof taking the defensive reins and O’Brien and
some of his assistants handling offensive play-calling. In all, O’Brien
replaced 75% of his positional staff during the offseason.

BC also saw the most active transfer portal period in school history, as
30 players departed, and 28 transfers arrived.

This year could have a host of different outcomes, depending on how
the transfers perform. If McKenzie can be a mobile game-changer like
Ole Miss star Trinidad Chambliss, to whom O’Brien has compared him,
BC could be a surprise team. But if McKenzie struggles, and a new often-
sive line does not jell, this could be another long season in Chestnut Hill.

CAL GOLDEN BEARS


===== Cal =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 47 | ACC PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 4-4 ACC

OFFENSE New Cal head coach Tosh Lupoi left his introductory news con-

ference, flew to Hawai'i, convinced star quarterback Jaron-Keawe Sagapo-
lutele to return and then flew back, without changing clothes. Lupoi has a
potential centerpiece in JKS, and he’s not taking him for granted.

The Bears are building around Sagapolutele with gifted wide receivers,
tight ends and running backs while working to upgrade the offensive line.
Cal added second-team All-MAC wideout Chase Hendricks (Ohio),
productive Rutgers receiver [an Strong and New Mexico’s Dorian ‘Thom-
as, a first-team All-Mountain West selection who ranked among the
nation’s top-10 tight ends for receptions (56) and receiving yards (560).

Cal has depth at tight end with returning starter Mason Mini, Purdue
transfer Rico Walker and others. Washington transfer Adam Mohammed,
who rushed for 105 yards against Lupoi’s Oregon defense in 2025, head-
lines a transfer-heavy running back room.

The line returns experience in the interior with Tyson Ruffins and Sio-
ape Vatikani, and Cal has supplemented with transfers, as Jimothy Lewis

Jr. (Mississippi State), Kahlee Tafai (Minnesota) and others arrive.

DEFENSE Lupoi and the defensive staff have work ahead to transform body
types, including adding length up front. Veterans Nate Burrell and Derek
Wilkins return, and tackle Jericho Johnson is among those who joined
Lupoi from Oregon. Cal also is banking on potential with edge transfers

Solomon Williams (Texas A&M) and Emmanuel Okoye (Tennessee).

Linebacker is a question as first-team All-ACC selection Cade Uluave
and Luke Ferrelli, the ACC Defensive Freshman of the Year, both trans-
ferred. Aaron Hampton and Jayden Wayne are back, but Cal will need to
fill out the depth chart with transfers like Texas A&cM’s Tristan Jernigan.

Cal’s secondary has a new look, but “we upgraded dramatically there, as
far as size-speed combo,” Lupoi says. The length shows up at cornerback
with 6-foot-3 Ole Miss transfer Ricky Fletcher, 6-foot-3 Georgia transfer
Daniel Harris and others. Nickel Cam Sidney returns from 2025.

SPECIALISTS Chase Meyer is back after connecting on 10-of-13 field goals,
including 7-of-7 inside 40 yards. Cal added punter Angus Davies from

Tulsa in the portal. Jacob De Jesus will be missed on returns, and special
teams coordinator Zach Tinker likely will lean on Hendricks and others.

FINAL ANALYSIS Lupoi and general manager Ron Rivera bring a ped-
al-down personnel approach that Cal needs. If Sagapolutele gets better

protection, he could lead one of the ACC’s better passing attacks. Cal lost
key back-end players on defense and must generally get better along both
lines of scrimmage, but the future looks bright.

DUKE BLUE DEVILS


===== Duke =====
@ | NATIONAL FORECAST: 54 | ACC PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 9-5, 6-2 ACC

OFFENSE The ACC’s top scoring offense (34.6 ppg) propelled Duke to its
first ACC championship since 1989. A quarterback change necessitated

by Darian Mensah’s controversial last-minute portal move to Miami
(Fla.) and Walker Eget’s arrival from San José State could make that
level of production more difficult to attain. Eget completed 59% of his
passes last season, throwing for 3,051 yards with 17 touchdowns and nine
interceptions. [hose numbers don’t quite match Mensah’s. But given the
circumstances, the Blue Devils are in decent shape there.

Duke retained running back Nate Sheppard, who turned in a fabulous
freshman season with 1,132 rushing yards and 11 touchdowns. He'll run
behind an offensive line that returns two starters — left guard Jordan
Larsen and center Matt Craycraft. Right tackle Micah Sahakian is back
after missing last season with an injury. Coastal Carolina transfer Nick
Del Grande, a two-time All-Sun Belt pick, slots in at left tackle.

Duke will count on FCS Penn transfer Jared Richardson, a 1,000-yard

receiver last season, to start.

DEFENSE ‘Though head coach Manny Diaz is known for his defensive

prowess, the Blue Devils won the ACC despite finishing in the bottom
half of the league in scoring defense (29.4 ppg, 14th) and total defense
(424.2 ypg, 15th). Duke needs a host of reserves to step into starting roles.

Up front, David Anderson, Preston Watson and Tyshod Reed will get
opportunities. Senior linebacker Nick Morris Jr. is returning from a torn
ACL, and Duke needs more contributions from Luke Mergott, who
stepped in and made big plays last season.

‘The secondary needs transfers Dylan Flowers (WKU), Evan Smith
(Northwestern) and Patrick Smith-Young (North Texas) to jell quickly
with returners Landon Callahan and DaShawn Stone.

SPECIALISTS Cosme Salas, Duke’s kickoff specialist as a freshman last sea-

son, steps into full placekicking duties as a sophomore. His leg is strong,
but he needs to prove he’s accurate as well. Duke has one of the ACC’s
better punters in Kade Reynoldson, who averaged 44.6 yards per attempt
as a sophomore. Sheppard could bring explosiveness as a punt returner,
and the same goes for wide receiver Jayden Moore on kickoffs.

FINAL ANALYSIS Duke reached an impressive milestone with its ACC

championship. Still, the Blue Devils lost five regular-season games, mak-
ing the title game as a result of a series of tiebreakers. It looks unlikely
that the offense will match last season's production, which means the
defense needs to improve. So many new faces could make that difficult.

FLORIDA STATE SEMINOLES


===== Florida State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 48 | ACC PREDICTION: 11 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 2-6 ACC

OFFENSE Florida State is breaking in a new starting quarterback for the
third consecutive season and is tasked with replacing its entire offensive
line for the second straight year. Transfer quarterback Ashton Daniels has
been inconsistent throughout his college career, but he had some promis-
ing flashes in limited action at Auburn in 2025.

The Seminoles do bring some promising playmakers to the table. Duce
Robinson and Micahi Danzy could blossom into one of the best wide
receiver duos in the country. Robinson is coming off a 1,000-yard season,
while Danzy’s explosiveness leaves opposing defenses in the dust.

FSU has a three-headed monster at running back with Texas transfer
Quintrevion Wisner joining Ousmane Kromah and Samuel Singleton Jr.
It’s a group that will give opposing defenses fits.

DEFENSE ‘The Seminoles revamped their defense over the offseason, bring-
ing in multiple transfers to fortify the unit. The added talent in the middle
will help shore up a weakness from a year ago. Southern Miss transfer
Chris Jones was among the top-10 tacklers in the FBS in 2025, and
North Carolina transfer Mikai Gbayor has previous experience playing
under defensive coordinator Tony White when both were at Nebraska.
Florida State has questions in the trenches, although the team did
retain twin defensive linemen Darryll and Desir Mandrell. Daniel Lyons
has grown into a leader in the middle. Rylan Kennedy (Texas A&M) was
the only defensive end the Seminoles added through the transfer portal.
On the back end, cornerback Ja’ Bril Rawls and rover Ashlynd Barker

are two crucial pieces to the success of the unit.

SPECIALISTS ‘This unit is relatively unknown, as former Oklahoma State

kicker Gabe Panikowski and former New Mexico punter Daniel Hughes
transferred to Tallahassee, along with North Dakota State long-snapper
Caleb Bowers. ‘The options in the return game are intriguing, with dy-
namic threats such as Singleton, Danzy, Jasen Lopez and Devin Carter.

FINAL ANALYSIS Head coach Mike Norvell enters his seventh season at
Florida State firmly on the hot seat.’ [he Seminoles have missed the post-

season four times in the past six years and are just 7-18 overall since being
infamously snubbed from the College Football Playoff after the 2023
regular season. [he schedule does Norvell no favors. FSU faces Alabama
in Tuscaloosa after hosting SMU on Labor Day. There is also a pair of
two-game road trips to grapple with, and it’s been more than two years
since the program won outside of Doak Campbell Stadium.

In the end, Florida State’s lack of urgency to invest in a top quarterback
transfer will likely come back to bite Norvell, who might be gearing up
for his final chance to right the ship.

GEORGIA TECH YELLOW JACKETS


===== Georgia Tech =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 41 | ACC PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 ACC

OFFENSE Georgia Tech will have a new offensive coordinator, former
Jackets quarterback and veteran NFL assistant George Godsey, and a new
quarterback, with Haynes King graduating after three years as the starter.
The Jackets will likely return to a more run-heavy, ball-control type of
system, as head coach Brent Key wants a tough, physical brand of football.

Filling King’s shoes will be a tough assignment, but transfer Alberto
Mendoza is expected to get the first shot following a year spent backing
up his brother, 2025 Heisman Trophy winner Fernando, at Indiana.

Tech made its biggest portal splash at running back with the acquisi-
tion of Justice Haynes, who has shown dynamic playmaking ability during
stints at Alabama and Michigan. He will pair with Malachi Hosley, who
led Jackets RBs last year with 697 rushing yards.

Attrition hit the wide receiver and tight end positions hard. Speedy
Jordan Allen returns with higher expectations after a solid true freshman
campaign at WR, and FCS Elon transfer Isaiah Fuhrmann is a big-
ger-bodied target. At tight end, several transfers will fill key roles in an
offense that uses the position in a variety of ways.

‘The offensive line will have three new starters along with returnees
Ethan Mackenny at left tackle and Malachi Carney, who is expected to
move to guard after starting at right tackle in 2025.

DEFENSE New defensive coordinator Jason Semore will emphasize ageres-
siveness and takeaways after the unit struggled to force turnovers (nine)
and get to the quarterback (13 sacks) in 2025.

The line lost several key contributors, but the coaching staff made it a
priority to build depth and emphasize size in the portal. Linebackers Kyle
Efford and E.J. Lightsey return as the two top tacklers from 2025, but in
a new system, the pair will likely have to earn their spots again.

Question marks fill the back end following the loss to graduation of
two starters at corner and two starters at safety. Transfers Jaylen Mbakwe
and Jonas Duclona should step in at corner, while returning safeties Tae
Harris and Fenix Felton look to take their games to a new level.

SPECIALISTS ‘The Jackets return one of the best placekickers in the country
in Aidan Birr, who had multiple clutch field goals in 2025. The punting
situation will be a battle between an incoming transfer, a freshman and a
walk-on. There are multiple talented playmakers in the return game.

FINAL ANALYSIS ‘The roster is much younger, the Jackets have new coordi-
nators and multiple new assistants and the schedule appears to be tougher.
Key and his staff have done a good job filling holes in the roster, but how

it all comes together is the million-dollar question.

MIAMI HURRICANES


===== Miami (Fla.) =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 7 | ACC PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 13-3, 6-2 ACC

UFFENSE Miami has explosive pieces on offense, led by former Duke
standout quarterback Darian Mensah, who threw for 34 touchdowns and
only six interceptions in leading the Blue Devils to the ACC title in 2025.
The Hurricanes also picked up his favorite target at Duke — Cooper Bar-
kate (1,106 yards, seven TDs last season). Now add in the return of one
of the nation’s most explosive slot threats in Malachi Toney (1,211 yards,
10 TDs), as well as running back Mark Fletcher Jr. (1,192 yards, 12 TDs),
and the Canes have no shortage of weapons.

‘The big question is an offensive line that loses four starters and is rely-
ing on oft-injured center Ryan Rodriguez, true freshman tackle Jackson
Cantwell and former 5-star recruit Samson Okunlola. The lone returner

up front is Matthew McCoy, who could play guard or tackle.

JEFENSE Despite some key losses — including star ends Rueben Bain Jr.
and Akheem Mesidor — this should once again be a playmaking group.

Miami added fearsome Missouri pass rusher Damon Wilson II (nine

sacks in 2025), and the team returns Marquise Lightfoot (2.5 sacks), DE/
DT Armondo Blount (2.5 sacks), starting tackles Ahmad Moten Sr. (4.5
sacks) and Justin Scott (6.5 tackles for a loss).

The top four cornerbacks return, led by Romanas “OJ” Frederique Jr.
(seven pass breakups) and Xavier Lucas (45 tackles, INT). Starting safety
Zechariah Poyser (68 tackles) is joined by nickel/S Bryce Fitzgerald (six
INTs) and Boston College transfer Omar ‘Thornton (82 tackles).

An area of concern depth-wise is at linebacker. Mohamed ‘Toure (84
tackles) is back, but the position has question marks, as guys like Chase
Smith and former NC State transfer Kamal Bonner need to step up.

SPECIALISTS Northwestern transfer Jack Olsen (19-of-21 on field goals,
with a long of 41 yards) will handle field goals with Florida State transfer
Jake Weinberg handling kickoffs. Punter Dylan Joyce is one of the ACC's

best, averaging 44.4 yards per punt last season. The return game should be
explosive behind the speedy Toney (13.0 yards per punt return).

rINALANALYSIS Fresh off a run to the national title game, this year’s Hurri-
canes might even be better. The offense has a pair of 1,000-yard receivers
(Barkate, Toney), a 1,000-yard rusher (Fletcher) and one of the ACC’s
top returning QBs (Mensah). The defense added a massive pass-rush
threat in Wilson and returns six starters. Head coach Mario Cristobal’s
roster-building has positioned Miami as an ACC favorite and legitimate
national title threat.

===== PAGE 60 =====

===== North Carolina =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 56 | ACC PREDICTION: 14 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-6 ACC

The success of North Carolina’s offense will likely hinge on the
quarterback decision and how quickly a rebuilt offensive line stabilizes. If
Wisconsin transfer Billy Edwards Jr. wins the QB job, expect a controlled
version of new offensive coordinator Bobby Petrino’s scheme — quick-
game concepts and selective deep shots. Edwards brings experience (520
career attempts at the Power 4 level) but lacks elite arm strength and
experience in systems that align with Petrino’s preferences. If'Texas A&M
transfer Miles O'Neill is pushed into the role, the ceiling rises, but so does
the risk behind an unproven offensive line replacing all five starters.

Center Christo Kelly is the key piece for UNC’s O-line after returning
from injury, bringing more than 20 career starts from his time at FCS
Holy Cross and leadership to a unit otherwise short on Power 4 experi-
ence. [he tackles — long, physically imposing options like Jordan Hall
— fit the prototype but remain unproven in pass protection, a critical
variable in a Petrino system that prefers leaving tackles on an island.

Skill talent is intriguing but largely undefined; this offense may lean on
efficiency over explosiveness early while searching for identity.

There is more clarity on this side of the ball. Coordinator Steve
Belichick inherits a defensive front anchored by Melkart Abou Jaoude,
who posted 47 tackles and finished second in the ACC with 10.5 sacks
last season. His return provides both production and leadership for a unit
that brings back multiple contributors along the line.

The broader question is consistency. UNC struggled with fundamentals
in 2025 — tackling, alignment, communication — and Belichick’s system
prioritizes those details. The linebacker room is being rebuilt, and the
secondary features a mix of veterans and transfers who must quickly jell in
a matchup-driven scheme. If the front generates pressure, the defense can
take a step forward; if not, the lack of depth at linebacker could be exposed.

The kicking game is relatively stable, led by transfer place-
kicker Aeron Burrell and punter Jacob Horvath. Return production is less
certain, with multiple options competing to replace departed contributors.
Overall, this unit projects as steady but unspectacular.

The offense has upside within Petrino’s system, but ear-
ly-season matchups against top defenses could mask progress while the
offensive line and quarterback position settle. Defensively, improvement is
more about execution, with Abou Jaoude providing a reliable anchor.

If the offensive line develops quickly and the quarterback situation sta-
bilizes, UNC has the coaching to outperform last year’s 4-8 mark. If not,
inconsistency — particularly early — could again define the season.

===== PAGE 62 =====
Pitt PITT PANTHERS


===== Pitt =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 40 | ACC PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 6-2 ACC

OFFENSE ‘The quarterback position has been more Pitt stop than perma-
nent in recent seasons. Now, along comes Mason Heintschel, a midseason
revelation who fueled an 8-5 finish in 2025 with an “m Him” mentality.
The lightly recruited freshman was summoned by head coach Pat Nar-
duzzi after a 2-2 start and promptly went 5-0, putting the Panthers in the
College Football Playoff conversation before late-season losses to Notre
Dame and Miami (Fla.). He went 6-3 overall, a run that featured four
consecutive road victories, four 300-yard passing games and two upsets of
ranked opponents. Ihe 6-foot-2 Ohio native threw for 2,354 yards with
16 touchdowns and a 63.6% completion rate.

Coordinator Kade Bell oversaw a unit that ranked 20th nationally in
scoring (33.7 ppg) and passing (273.3 ypg), though the running game
ranked 115th (117.2 ypg). Tailback JaKyrian Turner emerged in the final
five games with 127 rushing yards at Stanford, 201 at Georgia Tech and
93 vs. East Carolina in the Military Bowl. The true freshman had a team-
high 745 rushing yards (5.3 per carry) with seven scores. Returners Keith
Gouveia, Ryan Baer and BJ Williams lead the O-line.

Other than returning starter Cataurus Hicks (24 receptions, 422 yards,
long of 84), the receiving corps is light on experience. FCS Western
Carolina transfer Malik Knight, freshman Dylan Wester and sophomores
Tony Kinsler and Bryce Yates must be quick studies.

DEFENSE Braylan Lovelace aims to become Pitt’s next iconic linebacker.
He posted a career-best 80 tackles and returned an interception 100 yards
for a touchdown in a win over Georgia Tech. The senior middle lineback-
er could pair nicely with rising star Cameron Lindsey, a former backup
who posted 32 tackles, five for a loss, two sacks, a pass breakup and a
forced fumble. Purdue transfer Alex Sanford Jr. will play extensively.

The line features returners Isaiah Neal, Nick James, Sean FitzSimmons
and Jimmy Scott — a quartet that enabled Pitt to rank No. 8 nationally
vs. the run. Hard-hitting safety Cruce Brookins (53 tackles, two intercep-
tions) and corner Shawn Lee Jr. (four pass breakups) lead the secondary.

SPECIALISTS FCS transfers Sam Hunsaker (15-of-22 on field-goal at-
tempts in two seasons at Northern Arizona) and Antonio Chadha (28-of-
38 in two seasons at Western IIlinois) will vie for placekicking duties. FCS
Idaho State transfer Gabe Russo averaged 46.3 yards on 38 punts in’25.

FINALANALYSIS Much is expected of the 19-year-old Heintschel after he
led Pitt to a second-place ACC finish and its most victories since ’22. If
the Panthers can take advantage of a four-game homestand to start the
season, the momentum could once again lead to eight (or more) victories.

SMU MUSTANGS


===== SMU =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 22 | ACC PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 ACC

OFFENSE Kevin Jennings returns at quarterback, but his group of tight
ends, wide receivers and running backs is almost completely different.
Jennings was injured in 2025, hampering his ability to run, but he took a
step forward as a passer, ranking in the top 10 nationally for passing yards.

Alabama transfer Jalen Hale and East Carolina transfer Yannick Smith
arrive to bolster the wide receiver room, while Jalen Cooper and Isaiah
Robertson look to assert themselves in their second season at SMU. Yamir
Knight is a dynamic slot receiver for Jennings. Cal transfer running back
Kendrick Raphael is expected to pair with second-year back Dramekco
Green to provide plenty of backfield production.

‘The offensive line is a strong unit, with three returning starters, includ-

ing PJ Williams, who figures to be a high NFL Draft pick.

DEFENSE Last year, the SMU defense was all over the place, with moments
of impressive play followed by huge game-changing lapses.

SMU brings back Marcellus Barnes Jr. and William “Speedy” Nettles at
corner and Tyren Polley Jr. at safety, but the rest of the secondary has been
revamped with transfers. UTSA safety Jimmy Wyrick, USF nickel Jarvis
Lee and ‘Texas A&M transfer cornerback Jayvon Thomas all are expected
to make a major impact.

SMU returns an impressive trio of linebackers in Brandon Booker,
Alexander Kilgore and Brandon Miyazono. Up front, SMU reworked the
defensive line with Louisiana Tech transfer Christian Davis and Kansas
State transfer Malcolm Alcorn-Crowder, and 2024 standout Jonathan
Jefferson returns after missing all of the 2025 season due to injury.

Finding a pass rush from the likes of Aakil Washington, Ira Singleton
IV, Tavion Atkinson, Marques White, Jahkai Lang and others will be

critical.

SPECIALISTS Placekicking was an adventure for SMU last season, so the

Mustangs brought in FCS New Hampshire All-American Nick Reed.
Punter Wade McSparron and long-snapper Morgan ‘Tribett return for the

Mustangs. Knight is locked in as the punt returner, while kickoff returns
are up for grabs a bit with Link Rhodes and Daylon Singleton both back.

FINALANALYSIS With Jennings back, SMU should be among the favor-
ites to win the ACC championship. Head coach Rhett Lashlee’s team
has been among the nation’s most consistent programs for the past few
seasons, and expectations are as high now as they've ever been. Lashlee
and his staff seemed to hit on transfers who could push the team over the

top. If so, SMU could find itself back in Charlotte playing in the ACC
Championship Game. It’s College Football Playoff or bust for this team.

STANFORD CARDINAL


===== Stanford =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 84 | ACC PREDICTION: 17 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 ACC

OFFENSE ‘The Stanford offense will center around quarterback Davis War-
ren, running back Micah Ford, wide receiver Caden High and tight end

Benji Blackburn. A veteran with starting experience at Michigan, Warren
brings a good presence in the pocket, an ability to make plays with his
legs and a knack for clutch throws. He threw for 1,199 yards, seven
touchdowns and nine interceptions for the Wolverines in 2024.

Ford rushed for 643 yards and four touchdowns on 145 carries last
season and could flirt with 1,000 rushing yards this season. High had 37
receptions for 414 yards and one touchdown last season, while Blackburn
had nine receptions for 109 yards and one touchdown. Both guys should
see plenty of targets and could make a real impact. It'll be interesting to
see what new head coach ‘Tavita Pritchard brings in terms of scheme.

Up front, Niki Prongos, Fisher Anderson and Simione Pale will anchor
the offensive line. Their play will be critical in the Cardinal’s quest for
offensive balance.

DEFENSE Middle linebacker Matt Rose is the heart and soul of the Cardi-

nal defense. He is coming off a season in which he had 106 total tackles
(49 solo) and eight tackles for a loss. He should once again wreak havoc
everywhere on the field.

The safety room is also strong with Jay Green, Scotty Edwards, Charlie
Eckhardt and Darrius Davis. Cornerback Brandon Nicholson looks to
take his game up a notch from last year.

On the defensive line, Braden Marceau-Olayinka and Zach Rowell
should both be forces after making significant strides last season. The
Cardinal played top-25-level defense in the red zone in 2025 and will

look to keep that momentum going under new defensive coordinator Kris

Richard.
SPECIALISTS Punter Aidan Flintoft and placekicker Emmet Kenney are

both veteran guys with a lot of experience. [he real question on special
teams is whether or not the Cardinal can make some noise in the return

game. High will look to do his part.

FINAL ANALYSIS Coming off a 4-8 season in which it defeated Cal to win
the Stanford Axe, Stanford is hoping to build on what it did last year and
take more steps forward in 2026. Given the Cardinal’s schedule, they'll be
hard pressed to win five-plus games, but the belief in the program’s tra-

jectory is there, spearheaded by Stanford alumni Andrew Luck (general

manager) and Pritchard.

RETURNING STARTERS IN BOLD = *2026 TRANSFER

SYRACUSE ORANGE


===== Syracuse =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 71 | ACC PREDICTION: 15 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 1-7 ACC

UFFENSE Syracuse was 3-1 when quarterback Steve Angeli was ruled out
for the 2025 season due to a torn Achilles tendon. The Orange finished
3-9, failing to score more than 18 points in any of the eight losses that
followed. If the Fran Brown era is to be rejuvenated in 2026, it will almost
certainly be on the back of his star quarterback. Angeli, who started his
career at Notre Dame (2023-24) and was leading the nation in passing
yards (1,317) at the time of his injury, was a limited participant during
spring practice but is expected to be ready for fall camp.

Around Angeli will be a committee of skill-position players, including
a bevy of transfers. SU doesn't return anyone who recorded more than 130

rushing or receiving yards last year. And it doesn’t help that 5-star fresh-
man WR Calvin Russell II] suffered a torn Achilles of his own in March.

Former LSU running back JuJuan Johnson and Miami (Ohio) wide

receiver Cole Weaver will be among the newcomers getting significant
early opportunities.

Up front, the Orange feature an experienced offensive line bolstered by
the addition of Houston transfer David Ndukwe.

JEFENSE A step forward on defense starts in the trenches for Syracuse,
and a trio of transfers in Dillan Fontus (Maryland), Keyshawn Johnson
(FCS UT-Martin) and Tunmise Adeleye (UNLV ) is expected to provide
an immediate boost. Ihe Orange allowed 5.3 yards per carry last season
— ranking 132nd of 136 teams in the FBS.

But under new defensive coordinator Vince Kehres, the reloaded defen-
sive line room should have the personnel to fit into what's expected to be
a variable scheme featuring 4-3 and 3-4 bases.

‘The question is whether the Orange can execute in run fits and avoid
miscommunications on the back end in Year 1 of the system. Interior
linebackers like Chris D’Appolonia and Antoine Deslauriers will be
tasked with leading communication, while SU’s corners project to take on
heavy man-coverage assignments.

SPECIALISTS ‘Tripp Woody returns as placekicker after making 12-of-14

field goals last season, including all 10 of his attempts inside 40 yards.
But SU must replace standout punter Jack Stonehouse, likely hoping that
highly touted prospect Jimmy Gregg can hit the ground running.

rINALANALYSIS Brown's dazzling 10-3 debut campaign is fading into the

rearview faster by the day, and the reality is that winning in Syracuse over
the past quarter-century has hinged on QB play. Keeping Angeli upright
is priority No. 1, while preparing transfer quarterback additions Amari

Odom (Kennesaw State) and Malachi Nelson (UTEP) is right behind.

===== PAGE 66 =====
\W/_| VIRGINIA CAVALIERS

OFFENSE Virginia’s offseason mission prioritized the retention of its veter-
an offensive line, and that unit is the Cavaliers’ backbone heading into the
fall. Projected starters McKale Boley, Noah Josey, Drake Metcalf, Makilan
Thomas and Monroe Mills have combined for 164 career starts, making
UVa's O-line the most experienced in the nation. The Hoos want to run
behind that group. Boley and Josey were All-ACC performers in 2025,
helping UVa rush for 2,502 yards, the program’s most since 2004.

UVa is deep at running back even though they’re replacing J’Mari Tay-
lor, a first-team All-ACC performer. Tennessee transfer Peyton Lewis has
the most elite combination of size and speed for a running back that the
Cavaliers have had in fifth-year head coach Tony Elliott’s tenure. Middle
Tennessee transfer Jekail Middlebrook will get plenty of touches, and
Xavier Brown and UAB transfer Solomon Beebe are capable contributors.

Quarterback Beau Pribula, a Missouri transfer, will factor into the
running game, too. But Pribula must progress as a passer. He was third in
the SEC in completion rate (67.4%) last fall, but his touchdown-to-inter-
ception ratio was 11-to-9. He must reduce his turnovers.

Some new receivers must emerge after the Cavaliers lost six of their top
seven receptions leaders from last fall.


===== Virginia =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 39 | ACC PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 11-3, 7-1 ACC

DEFENSE UVa is strongest up the middle, where the headliner is linebacker
Kam Robinson. Last season, he became the first FBS player in 20 years to
block a punt, return an interception for a touchdown and recover a fumble
in the same season, although he missed six games due to a broken collar-
bone and torn ACL. UVa needs him healthy. Maddox Marcellus filled in
admirably for Robinson last year, and now they'll be paired as starters.
Defensive tackles Jason Hammond and Anthony Britton both enter
their fourth seasons, and the Cavaliers expect Michigan transfer Bran-
dyn Hillman to be the squad's next productive safety. At UVa, he'll step
into the role previously occupied by Devin Neal Jr. last year and by Jonas
Sanker in 2024. Both former UVa safeties led the team in tackles in those
seasons. Fisher Camac (6-7, 253) is the Cavaliers’ best pass-rusher.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Will Bettridge and punter Daniel Sparks give

UVa trusted fifth-year starters at those spots. Beebe and Jacquon Gibson
are slated to handle returns.

FINAL ANALYSIS UVa set a school record with 11 wins last season, and EI-

liott’s bunch will be a factor again because of its excellent O-line and deep
backfield and because defensive coordinator John Rudzinski can mold his
unit quickly. What could hurt the Hoos is if their wide receivers don't pan
out and the passing game flops.

’ VIRGINIA TECH HOKIES


===== Virginia Tech =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 30 | ACC PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 2-6 ACC

OFFENSE New head coach James Franklin has a familiar face leading his

offense, bringing quarterback Ethan Grunkemeyer with him from Penn
State. Though Grunkemeyer didn’t ascend to a starting spot until after
Franklin's ouster, he acquitted himself well, throwing for 777 yards, six
touchdowns and no picks in the Nittany Lions’ final four games.

He'll have some intriguing weapons, with Ayden Greene back as the
top wideout and the shifty Que’Sean Brown (846 receiving yards, five
TDs last year) transferring from Duke. Running back Marcellous Haw-
kins quietly ranked fifth in the ACC at 6.35 yards per carry last year, and
Penn State transfer tight end Luke Reynolds is a former 5-star recruit.

As usual, it could come down to the offensive line, which struggled to
protect the quarterback last year but should be better under the instruc-
tion of second-year position coach Matt Moore. Center Kyle Altuner
locks down the middle, but every other spot feels up for grabs.

DEFENSE Meet the new boss, same as the old boss. Brent Pry is back with
the school that fired him last September as head coach. He’s reunited with
Franklin in a coordinator role, a partnership that served both of them well
in Happy Valley.

Weight room stud Kemari Copeland (7.5 tackles for a loss, 4.5 sacks in
2025) gives the Hokies a building block on the interior line, and the sec-
ondary is beefed up. Quentin Reddish returns from a shoulder injury to
headline the safeties room, while Tech restocked at corner, adding Jaquez

White (Troy) and Kenny Woseley Jr. (Penn State) as transfers and getting
Joshua Clarke back from a torn ACL. Expect the pass defense to improve

upon its national ranking of 81st nationally in yards allowed.
There are question marks at linebacker and on the front. Sophomore
Noah Chambers has hard-hitting upside in the middle, but there are few

proven playmakers other than Copeland in the front seven.

SPECIALISTS John Love returns as one of the nation’s top placekickers, a
two-time All-ACC honorable mention selection who has made 84.4% of
his career field-goal attempts and has booted five from 50-plus in the past
two years. Brown could kickstart a stagnant punt return.

FINALANALYSIS It’s been 10 years since Virginia Tech has played for an

ACC championship and 16 years since it has won one — far too long for
a program of the Hokies’ caliber. By fortuitous timing, a recruiting-sawvy,
proven winner from the mid-Atlantic happened to be available on the
coaching market last fall. It might take Franklin more than a year to make
the Hokies a contender, but with his history and ‘Tech’s recent cash infu-
sion to athletics, the days of going 3-9 feel over.

===== PAGE 68 =====

===== Wake Forest =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 53 | ACC PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 4-4 ACC

OFFENSE Gone is a quarterback duo that could be erratic; in steps a
quarterback who was erratic for a different ACC team. Gio Lopez is set
to reunite with Rob Ezell, his offensive coordinator at South Alabama in
2024 and current coordinator at Wake Forest. The 2024 version of Lopez
was responsible for more than 3,000 yards of offense and 25 touchdowns.
And if Lopez can recapture that form under Ezell’s tutelage, his rough
one-season stint at North Carolina will be forgotten.

Wake Forest has to solve the puzzle of who's blocking in front of him.
Clinton Richard is a good building block, and there’s no shortage of op-
tions up front — but there’s work to be done in figuring out the top five.

The Deacons also have to replace plenty of lost production at wide re-
ceiver, where only Carlos Hernandez returns, and at running back, where
Demond Claiborne is a significant loss. But there’s plenty of speed and
potential with transfer wide receivers Ny Carr, Chase Tyler, Wondame

Davis Jr. and Kam Shanks.

DEFENSE ‘This unit was the strength of last year’s team, and that’s probably
the case again. Wake Forest returns both starting ends, Gabe Kirschke
and Langston Hardy, who combined for 12 sacks and 26 tackles for a loss
last year. Ihere’s also plenty of experience returning to the defensive tackle
and linebacker spots.

On the back end, Davaughn Patterson is a budding star at nickel.
Replacing two cornerbacks will be tough, but there are plenty of options,
both with returning players and transfers. Getting Rushaun Tongue back
from injury will help offset the loss of program pillar Nick Andersen.

SPECIALISTS Punting was a glaring issue last year. Ethan Hyams, from
Australia, will get a crack at solidifying the position. Connor Calvert

was excellent as a redshirt freshman kicker. He made 18-of-22 field-goal
attempts, including a walk-off winner against SMU. Wake Forest was dy-
namic in the return game and should have no shortage of options despite
losing Chris Barnes.

FINAL ANALYSIS Wake Forest won more games in head coach Jake Dick-

ert’s first season (nine) than it had in the previous two seasons combined
(four in each). Following up an impressive first season with an equal or
better year will be tough — and might be impossible if Lopez is the same
rattled QB he was for most of last season.

Unlike many Wake teams of old, though, the Deacons can rely on their
defense. On paper, this team checks all the boxes — experience, depth,
size, speed — and should be able to grind out some close wins.

CHARLOTTE 49ers


===== Charlotte =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 127 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 14 | 2025 RECORD: 1-11, 0-8 AMERICAN

OFFENSE Charlotte posted its worst offensive output in program history
last season, scoring only 14.3 points per game in the first year under head

coach Tim Albin and offensive coordinator Todd Fitch. The staff has

prioritized beefing up the offensive line and identifying reliable running
backs, headlined by the return of Jariel Cobb and Henry Rutledge.

With starting quarterback Conner Harrell missing much of last season
and being held out of team periods this spring due to a knee injury, the
QB battle has reopened. Transfer Cole Gonzales, formerly at Pitt and
FCS Western Carolina, saw plenty of reps in the spring, with the battle
for the starting spot coming down to Harrell’s health and how fast Gon-
zales can pick up Fitch’s system.

The offense can only be better, and it all begins up front, where transfers

J’Ven Williams, Nic Cruji and Reginhard Pierre-Nau are slated to start.

DEFENSE Charlotte’s defense returns plenty of familiar faces in the front
seven but will see a completely new crop of starters in the secondary.
Linebacker Kadin Schmitz is the leading returning tackler with 66, along
with two interceptions and two forced fumbles last season. Schmitz and
Mike linebacker Gavin Willis lead the defense, with the latter wearing the
helmet mic and communicating with defensive coordinator Nate Faanes.

The return of edges DJ Burgess and Curtis Simpson is huge for Faanes,
as the group struggled to rush the passer last season due to injuries. Collin
Gill and Dy’Lon Womack are expected to take on significantly bigger
roles in the secondary, an area that derailed the defense a season ago.

The talent level on the defense is undoubtedly a step above last season’s
group, but Albin and Faanes will need a good stroke of injury luck for the
unit to reach its full potential.

SPECIALISTS Charlotte has only one kicker on the roster — redshirt

freshman Aiden Bui. [he expectation is that the team will add at least one
more ahead of the season. Bronson Long is expected to serve as the punt-
er, with Pitt transfer Henry Searcy manning the long-snapper position.
The return game gets a huge jolt with Rutledge returning for his final
year, plus the addition of Jaden Barnes, an Appalachian State transfer.

FINALANALYSIS Albin needs a turnaround in Year 2 — which he has been
known for in stops at North Dakota State and Ohio. There’s some added

pressure with new athletic director Kevin White joining the fold follow-
ing the firing of Mike Hill, who hired Albin.

Albin focused on depth in the portal, but the roster itself doesn't appear
much more talented than it did in 2025. If Fitch’s offense continues to be
stagnant, expect a change there at the conclusion of the season.

===== PAGE 74 =====
EAST CAROLINA PIRATES


===== East Carolina =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 75 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 AMERICAN

OFFENSE ‘Two veteran quarterbacks battled all spring, and head coach

Blake Harrell likely won't declare a winner until the opener. Texas Tech

transfer Mitch Griffis has slightly more experience, but ex-Miami Hurri-
cane Emory Williams, at 6-foot-5, 225, is from QB central casting.

New offensive coordinator Jordan Davis lit up scoreboards at North
Texas, and he has a deep wide receiver unit. Returning starter Brock
Spalding is back alongside North Texas transfer Landon Sides, who had
88 career catches for 1,092 yards in Davis’ offense. FCS Furman import
Ja Keith Hamilton had 40 grabs and five scores while averaging 13.6 yards
per reception last year, and there’s former Miami (Fla.) slot speedster Ray
Ray Joseph, who projects in the return game, too.

Running back TJ Engleman Jr. returns after averaging 5.2 yards per
carry last season. Transfers Ashton Gray (North Texas) and Michael
Allen (Marshall) should hit the ground running, too. Gray had 152 yards
and two scores in the New Mexico Bowl.

All-Conference USA performer Brandon Best, a Kennesaw State
transfer, will step right into a depleted line. Southern Miss transfer Hayes
Creel is solid, and Ethan Kramer brings 29 starts from FCS Northern

Arizona. Experienced returnee Bryce Weaver impressed in the spring.

DEFENSE New defensive coordinator Jordon Hankins must replace a lot of

splash plays up front. ECU was fourth with 7.8 tackles for a loss per game
in 2025 and 26th nationally in rushing defense (120.3 ypg). Jasiyah Rob-

inson is the only starter back up front. He had five sacks and has fellow
veteran leaders Rion Roseborough and Preston Carr alongside him.

Tackling machine DJ Johnson Jr. anchors a linebacking crew with expe-
rience and potential. Crews Law, a transfer from Memphis who arrived
with Hankins, should help, too. On the backline, nickel Kevon Merrell
returns. FU transfer Ashton Levells-Mitchell and talented Appalachian
State addition Zyeir Gamble plug right in to keep the secondary tip-top.

SPECIALISTS Senior Andrew Conrad, the primary placekicker in 2023-24,
takes over again this fall. He has hit 36-of-51 career field-goal attempts
and tied an NCAA record with five successful field goals of 40-plus yards
in a 2023 game. The punting unit depends on the solid foot of Australian
Tomas O’Halloran, who averaged 42.1 yards on his 21 boots.

FINALANALYSIS The portal swept away lots of star power off another ECU
bowl team, but don’t be fooled. ‘The Pirates are sneaky good. Guys who

have played significant time without necessarily racking up starts are all
over the roster. Harrell is 14-5 since taking the helm, and he’s the archi-
tect of a sack-happy defense and great team culture in Greenville.

FLORIDA ATLANTIC

FLORIDA ATLANTIC OWLS


===== Florida Atlantic =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 96 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 9 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 AMERICAN

OFFENSE Importing the quarterback-wide receiver tandem of Caden
Veltkamp and Easton Messer from WKU bore fruit in head coach Zach
Kittley’s first season in Boca Raton. KAU flirted with a 30 points per
game average and bested North Texas in passing yards per game. But the
week-to-week production was inconsistent. Veltkamp threw for a pro-
gram-record 494 yards in a shootout loss to UConn. One week later, FAU
managed just a field goal in a blowout loss to East Carolina. Turnovers
were a persistent issue, as Veltkamp paced the FBS in interceptions (17),
and the Owls led all teams in giveaways (29).

Familiarity is one thing in KAU’s favor — five starters are back on
offense, including two on the line. Year 2 in Kittley’s offense could yield
further fireworks. And perhaps the pivot to Kaden Shields-Dutton in the
backfield will produce a more reliable run game to diversify one of the
most pass-happy offenses in college football.

DEFENSE For better or for worse, the Owls bring back more than half of

their starters from the group that allowed the most points per game (36.3)
in program history. FAU was one of 10 teams that gave up more than 200
yards per game both rushing and passing.

Top tackler Leon Hart Jr. and leading sack-getter CJ Doggette Jr. are
decorated returnees. Linebacker Joseph Sipp Jr. (Kansas) and defensive
tackle Blake Burris (SMU) arrived via the portal to bolster a group that
was gashed on the ground. ‘Though the offense put the defense in a bad
position with turnovers, the defense was almost as bad at forcing take-
aways. KAU’s eight turnovers forced were the fifth fewest in the FBS.

The secondary underwent a considerable overhaul, but some of the new
blood could help stop the bleeding over the top. Division II All-American
and national champion Ahlston Ware transferred from Ferris State to

provide some playmaking help at safety.

SPECIALISTS Newcomers at punter and kicker could provide an upgrade at
both positions. Reed Chandley comes to FAU from Georgia State, where
he led the Sun Belt in punting average (45.8). And placekicker Collin
Rogers transferred in from SMU, where he’s the all-time leading scorer.
Wide receiver Easton Messer will continue returning punts after leading
the conference in return average (20.8). Fellow wideout Kelby Valsin
could take over kickoff return duties.

FINAL ANALYSIS It will take considerable improvement on defense for FAU
to even dream of its first bowl bid since 2020. If that materializes, and
Veltkamp cuts down on the turnovers, there’s a path to improvement —
and maybe even six wins — for the Owls despite a difficult early schedule.

===== Memphis =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 66 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 4-4 AMERICAN

OFFENSE New offensive coordinator Kevin Decker will bring a fast, spread-
style attack that was successful at Old Dominion, although his quarter-
backs are unproven at the F'BS level. Marcus Stokes, a finalist for the Div.
I] Harlon Hill Trophy in 2025 while at West Florida, is expected to land
the starting job but will get competition from South Carolina transfer Air
Noland, who appeared in only two games for the Gamecocks in 2025.

Transfer running back Jaylin Carter (Southern Miss) followed first-year
head coach Charlie Huff from Hattiesburg to Memphis. Carter, Manny
Covey (Cincinnati) and Dallan Hayden (Colorado) should get the bulk
of the carries. Hayden, a Memphis native, is a powerful back who had 70
carries for 326 yards last season.

Receivers will line up wider in Decker’s scheme. Speedy Alante Brown,
a Michigan State transfer granted a seventh year of eligibility, is a big-
play candidate, as is Southern Miss transfer Tychaun Chapman.

DEFENSE An abundance of Southern Miss transfers will pace the defense.
Linebacker Mike Montgomery and safety Ian Foster, both arriving from
Southern Miss, will be the leaders of the unit under the direction of coor-
dinator Lance Guidry, who will implement a 4-2-5 scheme. Guidry spent
last season as an analyst at LSU. Foster, expected to play nickel corner in
the 4-2-5, is a punishing tackler who can effectively blitz off the edge as
well as play smothering man-to-man defense.

J’Mond Tapp, another ex-Southern Miss standout, will anchor the
defensive line. Guidry needs to find complements for Montgomery; the
rest of the linebackers have amassed only 23 career tackles.

SPECIALISTS Foster, Brown and Covey are experienced return special-

ists. As a freshman at Marshall in 2024, Foster led the nation in kickoff
returns of 50 or more yards (three) and 40 or more yards (five). Brown
returned four kicks for a 53.5-yard average in 2025. Covey averaged near-
ly 10 yards per punt return last season at Cincinnati. Harrison Tuck is a
former Australian Rules Football player who spent the past three seasons

punting at FCS Alabama State. He averaged 41.8 yards per punt in 2025.
FINALANALYSIS ‘There will be many new pieces for the Tigers, including a

new head coach and coordinators, new quarterbacks and a roster com-
posed mostly of transfers. It’s a challenge for Huff, a Nick Saban disciple
with a successful track record. He’s brought with him a group of talented
players from Southern Miss. If Stokes can quickly adapt to Decker’s
offense and the speed of FBS defenses, the Tigers’ defense should hold
its own behind Foster and Montgomery. Huft’s goal is to lead the Tigers
back to the American Championship Game for the first time since 2019.

\
UNT

OFFENSE North Texas boasted one of the top offenses in college football a
year ago, averaging an FBS-leading 512.4 yards and 45.1 points per game
en route to a 12-win season and American Conference Championship
Game appearance. Head coach Eric Morris, the mastermind behind it all,
is now at Oklahoma State. So are quarterback Drew Mestemaker, wide
receiver Wyatt Young and running back Caleb Hawkins.

Former Troy and West Virginia head coach Neal Brown takes over
the program after serving on the Texas staff last season and inherits an

NORTH TEXAS MEAN GREEN


===== North Texas =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 109 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 11 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 7-1 AMERICAN

offense with no returning starters.

Quarterback Chris Jimerson Jr. appeared in four games as a true fresh-
man. Transfers will be critically important at the skill positions. Wide
receiver [J Chukwurah was a Division I] All-American at Black Hills

State, and ex-West Virginia running back Jahiem White was a Freshman
All-American under Brown for the Mountaineers in 2023.

Much of the offensive line that paved the way for the most rushing
TDs in the country is gone, but Georgia Southern transfer Chandler
Strong has 30 career starts and will fill one vacancy.

DEFENSE UNT struggled at times on defense, allowing 381.9 yards and

26.5 points per game. Ihe Mean Green ranked 130th nationally in run
defense, giving up 215.7 rushing yards per game. Conversely, pass defense
was a strong point, limiting teams to just 166.2 yards (eighth in the FBS).
First-year defensive coordinator Matt Powledge, who held that position
at Baylor the past three years, will have his work cut out for him.

Terrell Washington has the most experience among returning defensive
linemen. [he additions of transfers Aaron Alexander (Arkansas State) and

Zakye Baker (SMU) should bolster the linebacking corps.
Twin defensive backs Caden and Cameren Jenkins both followed

Powledge from Baylor to Denton and should fill starting roles.
SPECIALISTS Placekicker Kali Nguma made 92.3% of his field-goal at-

tempts last season before transferring to Colorado State. Former Appa-

lachian State kicker Dominic De Freitas (20-of-25 FGs, 33-of-33 PATs)
will replace him. The Mean Green ranked fifth in kick returns and 14th in

punt returns in the American last season but must find replacements after
losing Simeon Evans and Miles Coleman.

FINALANALYSIS Running the ball has been a staple of Brown-coached of-

fenses, so expect the Mean Green to lean heavily on the run game. Several

new faces on defense bring a lot of questions with them, too, but change
could be good after the unit’s inconsistent showing the past two years.

RICE OWLS


===== Rice =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 120 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 5-8, 2-6 AMERICAN

OFFENSE The Owls became run-heavy and predictable at times last season.
Head coach Scott Abell, who is entering his second season at the helm
after a 5-8 debut, implemented his own version of the option offense last
season, and the Owls had success running it, but there will be a new quar-
terback under center for a second straight season. After Chase Jenkins
left via the transfer portal, Abell brought in transfers Jacurri Brown, who
had stops at Miami (Fla.) and UCF, and Gael Ochoa (UNLV) to run the
offense.

Whoever is under center will be tasked with improving a unit that
averaged 19.1 points per game last season and had most of its success on
the ground, averaging nearly 205 rushing yards per game. Leading rusher
Quentin Jackson returns, but the Owls lost most of their skill position
players to graduation or the portal.

DEFENSE ‘The Owls’ defense lost most of its starters and the top seven
tacklers to graduation and the transfer portal, so it might take a few
games for Rice to find its groove on that side of the ball.

Viper Jo Chavez and defensive end Joseph Mutombo will be among
the returning players who will lead a unit that surrendered nearly 33
points and 404 yards per contest. [he Owls need more takeaways after
forcing only seven last season, and they also must do a better job of
stopping teams in the red zone after opposing offenses scored on all 41
red-zone trips last season, including 30 touchdowns.

SPECIALISTS Enock Gota returns as the primary placekicker. Gota was
10-of-13 on field goals last season, but he has yet to hit from beyond 50
yards. Gota is solid on kickoffs with 37 touchbacks each of the past two
seasons. Punter Charlie Durkin comes to Rice after a good season with
Kent State, where he averaged 41.4 yards per punt, including six over 50
yards, and placed 14 inside the 20-yard line.

FINAL ANALYSIS The Owls were improved in Abell’s first season, appearing

in a bowl game despite their losing record thanks to having the highest
Academic Progress Rate among five-win teams. Rice will have a chance to
compete for a bowl game again this season, but the Owls were forced to
retool through the portal after losing more than 50 players to graduation
and transfers.

The Owls have to find a way to score after surpassing the 20-point
mark in only five games last season and in only two of their eight losses.
The defense was on the field too much due to the inability of the offense
to sustain drives, so the success of the Owls this season could come down
simply to moving the sticks.

, TEMPLE OWLS


===== Temple =====
es © NATIONAL FORECAST: 90 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 3-5 AMERICAN

OFFENSE ‘Temple’s offense enters the 2026 season with cautious optimism
after showing clear signs of progress a year ago under offensive coordina-
tor Tyler Walker. The biggest question for the Owls will be under center,
as they look to replace quarterback Evan Simon. As it stands, the quarter-
back battle is a two-horse race between Penn State transfer Jaxon Smolik
and Washington State transfer Ajani Sheppard, with Smolik appearing to
have the edge coming out of spring ball.

Temple returns several key weapons in the passing game, headlined by
standout tight end Peter Clarke and veteran receivers Colin Chase and
JoJo Bermudez. ‘The ground game will feature a new primary option in
Sam Brown V,a Rutgers transfer who impressed throughout the spring
as a physical, downhill runner. Perhaps most importantly, the Owls bring
back four starters along the offensive line, providing valuable continuity.

DEFENSE The Owls look to pair returning leadership with an influx of
new contributors. The strength of the unit lies at linebacker, where Curly
Ordonez and Eric Stuart lead a group that brings both production and
experience. Ordonez, an All-American Conference honorable mention
pick in 2025, is working back from offseason surgery, but once healthy, he
and Stuart should provide a steady presence in the middle.

Up front, the Owls will rely on a plethora of new faces. Though unprov-
en, the group has a chance to earn roles early and improve on last season's
struggles, when Temple ranked among the nation’s worst in rushing de-
fense (197.3 yards per game) and totaled just 16 sacks. Greater consisten-
cy and physicality will be key.

Cornerback remains the biggest question mark due to limited experience
and depth, while safety stands out as arguably the deepest position group.
Illinois transfer Saboor Karriem impressed during the spring with his
toughness and athleticism and looks ready to make an immediate impact.

SPECIALISTS Senior punter Dante Atton returns to anchor the unit, while
Carl Hardin is set for his second season as the placekicker after convert-
ing 11-of-14 field goals and all 40 PATs last year. In the return game,
Bermudez flashed elite potential on punt returns a season ago. Kickoff
return duties are more of an open competition, but veteran running back
Hunter Smith is a strong candidate thanks to his explosiveness in space.

FINALANALYSIS Year 2 of the K.C. Keeler era brings a mix of optimism
and unfinished business. Despite a disappointing finish that saw the Owls

drop their final four games and miss bowl eligibility at 5-7, Temple still
exceeded many expectations in 2025. ‘There is real optimism surrounding
the program as Temple aims to claim its first winning season since 2019.

TULANE GREEN WAVE


===== Tulane =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 79 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 11-3, 7-1 AMERICAN

-

5
D).

OFFENSE Retaining budding star running back Jamauri McClure gives new
head coach Will Hall a dynamic playmaker to build around in a loaded
backfield. McClure, who was fifth on the depth chart early last season,
averaged 98 rushing yards and 6.5 yards per carry in Tulane’s final five
games while showing an ability to run both inside and outside. Tulane
also has Maurice Turner, who won the starting job last preseason before
sustaining a high ankle sprain in the opener, and Florida State transfer
Jaylin Lucas. Both are versatile runners/receivers who fit Hall’s system.

Dual-threat Zeon Chriss-Gremillion, a Houston transfer, and Kadin
Semonza, who put up good numbers at Ball State in 2024 but was third
string last year at Tulane, will compete for the starting quarterback job in
preseason camp. Anthony Brown-Stephens (41 catches, 523 yards, two
TDs) and Zycarl Lewis Jr. (24 catches, 330 yards) are the top returning
receivers for a group that lost leading wideout Shazz Preston to Indiana
but added speedy LSU transfer Destyn Hill.

The lone returning starting lineman, Reese Baker, has moved inside to
guard from tackle.

DEFENSE Six of the top 10 tacklers return, a testament to the continuity on
a staff that retained key assistants from former head coach Jon Sumrall's
tenure. Safety Jack Ichienchou (83 tackles) was the defensive MVP of the
American Conference Championship Game. Linebacker Dickson Agu
was leading the team in tackles before tearing an ACL in the fifth game.
Linebacker Chris Rodgers (80 tackles) returned an interception for a TD
in the championship game. Safety Kevin Adams II] was fourth on the
team with 53 tackles despite never starting.

Although the line lost five stalwarts to Power 4 programs, the Green
Wave were incredibly deep up front and should not have a significant
drop-off there. Sumrall said several times last season that some of his
backups who rarely played were good enough to start elsewhere.

SPECIALISTS Sumrall took both specialists with him to Florida, but
transfer Jackson Courville was 26-of-34 on field goals in two years at Ball
State (2023-24) and hit a pair from 50-plus yards in 2024. Punting is a

concern. [he coaches love Lucas’ potential on returns.

FINALANALYSIS As an assistant, Hall was part of a winning culture at
Tulane under Willie Fritz and Sumrall. He put together a top-notch
staff and kept far more players than his conference brethren that also lost
coaches. If the quarterback play is good enough — a big if — the Green

Wave can reach their fifth consecutive league title game.

TULSA GOLDEN HURRICANE


===== Tulsa =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 108 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 1-7 AMERICAN

OFFENSE Second-year head coach Tre Lamb is trusting his offense to
baby-faced sophomore quarterback Baylor Hayes. Coaches rave about
Hayes’ “It Factor,” something he showed off in Tulsa’s 19-12 upset win at
Oklahoma State in 2025.

Hayes made nine starts a year ago, including the last six. He threw for
2,158 yards and 12 touchdowns, but it’s his running threat (376 yards
before sacks) that makes him the right fit for Lamb’s offense.

Hayes figures to benefit from a relatively experienced offensive line that
features returning starter Cam East at right tackle and six others who
combined to appear in 32 games a year ago. But TU must replace its three
leading rushers and receivers.

The Hurricane did get a late boost in the portal by signing running

back ‘Irequan Jones from Old Dominion. Pro Football Focus had Jones as
the third-highest graded transfer RB.

DEFENSE TU’s defense was greatly improved in 2025, but an epidemic of
big plays plagued the unit as the season progressed. Six players who start-
ed last year’s season finale against UAB return, led by cornerback Elijah
Green, who led the American Conference with five interceptions, and
safety Zach Williams, who had 73 tackles and two forced fumbles.

But the Hurricane must fill a huge hole in the middle, as leading
tackler Ray Coney (an American-leading 129 stops) moved on to Texas
A&M. Linebacker Chris Thompson Jr., a 2025 team captain who missed

the season after breaking his ankle during fall camp, returns.

SPECIALISTS TU spent the spring holding auditions at placekicker and
punter. Marlon Hauck emerged at kicker after transferring from Missis-
sippi State, where he attempted two extra points and no field goals over
four seasons. [he Hurricane are hoping to again tap into the Australian
punter pipeline after Torquay native Angus Davies started the last three

seasons. FCS Bethune-Cookman transfer Max Tulen of Melbourne takes
over after averaging 44.6 yards on 44 attempts in 2025.

FINALANALYSIS TU earned rave reviews for its “Portal House,” an upscale

rental home the school used to host staff and recruits during the January
transfer window. Ihe team was in need of a feel-good story after a 4-8
record in Lamb’s first season — one marked by only marginal progress
from 2024. In the end, the real measure of success for the “Portal House”
will be the product TU puts on the field. Lamb is confident that he’s the
right man to take advantage of the American's coaching upheaval. And if
a few of those social media stars from the “Portal House” live up to their

billing on the field, TU could be a pleasant surprise.

UAB BLAZERS


===== UAB =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 121 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-6 AMERICAN

OFFENSE Quarterback Ryder Burton enters his fourth season of college
football but his first as the clear-cut starter. [he 6-foot-2, 200-pound
Utah native threw for 251 yards and three touchdowns while engineering
the memorable upset win over Memphis in his first career start. His only
other start came in a loss to Rice when he threw for 213 yards and a pair
of touchdowns. Look for Burton to take a huge leap this year.

It is helpful that a loaded backfield that includes 6-foot-1, 235-pound
Georgia transfer Rod Robinson II can fuel the offense if needed. Injuries
slowed Robinson's progression at Georgia, but if he remains healthy, the
San Diego native can be a great power runner. Coastal Carolina transfer
Ja Vin Simpkins and ULM transfer Bam McReynolds rushed for a com-
bined 1,228 yards and five touchdowns last year.

Wide receiver depth could be dicey if Kaleb Brown can’t come back
from a spring ankle injury, but there is some talent there. Tight end Anto-
nio Ferguson is poised for a breakout season.

Depth should be a strength for an offensive line led by returnees Adam
Lepkowski and Calib Perez.

DEFENSE Veteran defensive coordinator Todd Grantham has put his stamp
on a defense that was quickly rebuilt through the transfer portal. The unit
should be strong up the middle with defensive tackles Cam Cunningham,
Chris Spencer and Nigel ‘Tate, inside linebackers Muaaz Byard and Ike
Esonwune and safeties Jeremiah Jordan and Jotavian Pierce.

Esonwune played in Grantham’s system at Oklahoma State last season
and contributed 31 tackles. The 6-foot-3, 330-pound Tate was slated to
anchor the defense last season but suffered a torn Achilles in fall camp.
Delvon Gulley returns at cornerback after an up-and-down season. Mar-
shall transfer Darrell Sweeting could be the best coverage cornerback.

Defensive end Marlin Dean, who began his college career at Georgia
and is on his fourth team, is a 6-foot-5, 240-pound presence on the edge.
Opposite Dean, Blanche Gold is a bit undersized but is a twitchy, intrigu-
ing prospect from FCS Morgan State. Grantham and his defensive staff
have also managed to build some depth.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Jonah Delange has made 31-of-38 field goals and

all 60 extra-point attempts in two seasons as the team’s starter. ‘The only
other returning specialist is Owen Simpson, who handles kickoffs.

FINALANALYSIS ‘There is a great unknown in Alex Mortensen’s first full
season as the Blazers’ head coach. The program fell into a ditch in Trent

Dilfer’s three-year tenure. If his team can stay healthy, then Mortensen
just might be the coach to get them back on the road.

Mekhi Anderson (So.)
David Amador II (Jr.)


===== UTSA =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 73 | AMERICAN PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 4-4 AMERICAN

OFFENSE New offensive coordinator Rick Bowie has designs on pushing
UTSA’s tempo and unleashing more downfield shots. Quarterback Owen
McCown was limited during spring practice as he recovered from surgery
to repair a sports hernia, but he enters his third year as a starter with the
potential to emerge as one of the top signal-callers in the Group of 6 if he
can avoid the injuries that nagged him last season.

Speedy running back Will Henderson III broke out with 866 yards and
six touchdowns last season, and the Roadrunners also bring back stand-
out receivers David Amador II and AJ Wilson. Running back Brandon
High Jr. (Cal) and receivers T'J West (Texas Tech) and James Madison II

(Missouri) are expected to be impact transfers. Despite new faces at tight

end and left guard, expectations are high for UTSA’s offense.

DEFENSE ‘The Roadrunners lost the bulk of their defense to graduation or
the transfer portal and spent the spring looking to reload. Inside lineback-
er Brandon Tucker, a former FCS All-America selection and Southland
Conference Defense Player of the Year at East Texas A&M, seems most
likely to step in as the leader of the unit after taking a rotational role last
season. Veteran Owen Pewee is projected to join him on the inside, mak-

ing the shift from nickel for his final season.
The Roadrunners have long taken pride in a strong, deep defensive line,

and ends ‘Tai Leonard and Johnny Bowens III and nose Jameian Buxton
are expected to anchor the unit. UTSA will rely on Camron Cooper — still
recovering from a knee injury in last year’s opener — to provide a pass-rush
threat at outside linebacker alongside UTEP transfer Elijah Baldwin.

FCS Marist transfer Nate Robinson Jr.,a San Antonio native, could be
a Day 1 starter at safety, while the cornerback position might be manned
by a pair of brothers: sophomore Ahamad Chapman and incoming ju-
nior-college transfer Asaad Chapman.

SPECIALISTS UTSA returns all of last year’s primary options on special
teams. Michael Petro connected on 17-of-20 field goals in 2025, while

Caile Hogan averaged 45.7 yards per punt. Amador was the leading punt
returner, while fellow wideout Mekhi Anderson handled kickofts, but
both spots will likely be open for competition.

FINAL ANALYSIS In the transient world of college football, having a
seventh-year head coach in Jeff Traylor and a third-year quarterback in

McCown makes UTSA an outlier that will face high expectations. ‘The

Roadrunners have posted winning records in every season of [raylor’s ten-
ure, and though keeping up with the most well-resourced programs in the
American Conference will be daunting, UTSA should remain in the hunt.

ARIZONA WILDCATS


===== Arizona =====
© NATIONAL FORECAST: 32 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-3 BIG 12

OFFENSE Arizona’s offense returns the reigning first-team All-Big 12 quar-
terback in Noah Fifita, who became the first UA quarterback to receive
first-team all-conference honors in 50 years. Fifita needs 829 yards to pass
Nick Foles for the most career passing yards by an Arizona quarterback.
Fifita already broke Foles’ career passing touchdowns record last season
and will likely go down as the statistical leader in every passing category
at the UA when it’s all said and done.

For the first time since becoming the starter, Fifita will have the same
offensive coordinator as the previous season. Another year with rising
play-caller Seth Doege could take Fifita — and the Arizona offense — to
another gear. Arizona also returns a nucleus of wide receivers in [re Spiv-
ey, veteran Chris Hunter and Giovanni Richardson, among others.

DEFENSE Defensive coordinator Danny Gonzales signed a contract
extension this offseason and was named a Broyles Award semifinalist for
the best assistant coach in college football. In the unit’s first season under
Gonzales, Arizona finished seventh nationally in passing defense and
helped the team post a plus-18 turnover margin. Ihe Wildcats had more
takeaways (31) than touchdowns allowed (28) and led the Big 12 in inter-
ceptions (22), which tied for second nationally. They led college football
in takeaways per game (2.4).

Arizona will have to replace a good chunk of the production, especially
in the secondary following the loss of defensive backs ‘Ireydan Stukes,
Genesis Smith and Dalton Johnson, who combined for 6,700 defensive
snaps in their Arizona careers. Ihe Wildcats signed eight defensive backs
in the transfer portal in 2026, but replacing the heartbeat of Arizona's
defensive secondary will be a tall task this fall.

SPECIALISTS Arizona placekicker Michael Salgado-Medina finished 19-

for-31 on field-goal attempts in 2025, the most misses in a season for the
UA since 1999, although snapping inconsistencies were a factor. Salga-

do-Medina was 5-for-12 between 40-49 yards but displayed leg strength

ARIZONA STATE SUN DEVILS


===== Arizona State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 35 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 6-3 BIG 12

OFFENSE The Sun Devils will lean heavily on the No. 15 transfer portal

class (per 247Sports) to overcome the departures of most of their starters
to graduation or transfer, including quarterback Sam Leavitt's move to
LSU. Cutter Boley, who started for Kentucky as a redshirt freshman, will
likely be the triggerman of an offense that added the nation’s top-ranked
wide receiver transfer class, headlined by Omarion Miller (Colorado) and
Reed Harris (Boston College) and one that also includes Raiden Vines-
Bright (Washington). All three look like future NFL Draft picks.

Center [ana Alo-Tupuola, who helped Georgia Tech rank as one of the
top rushing attacks in the F'BS last season, will help stabilize an offensive
line that also added transfer Luke Baklenko — a former starter at Stan-
ford who played at Oklahoma last season — at right tackle.

ASU’s backfield is deep and talented, with 2025 opening-game starter
Kyson Brown back at full strength after a season-ending injury and
newcomer Marquis Gillis, who rushed for more than 1,100 yards at FCS

Delaware State last season, a potent addition.

DEFENSE ASU should again be very stout, led by all-conference candidate
C.J. Fite at nose tackle and transfer ends Jalen Thompson (Michigan
State) and Emar’rion Winston (Baylor). Owen Long, the FBS’ leading
tackler last season at Colorado State, will help replace second-team All-
Big 12 linebackers Jordan Crook and Keyshaun Elliott, with sixth-year
returner Zyrus Fiaseu and Martell Hughes also starter-level players.

‘The secondary could be ASU’s biggest question, especially the all-im-
portant nickel position, where Adrian “Boogie” Wilson could start in a
move from free safety. Ashton Stamps, a two-year starter at LSU, will help
replace Keith Abney II and Javan Robinson at cornerback, with sopho-
more Rodney Bimage Jr. ready to shine. Senior free safety Lyrik Rawls was
a key addition after leading Kansas with 57 solo tackles last season.

SPECIALISTS Big-legged Australian punter Nick McLarty, an Ohio State
transfer, should help ASU improve from second-worst in the FBS in net

punting in ’25. Austin Peay transfer placekicker Carson Smith earned
Specialist of the Year honors in the FCS United Athletic Conference.

FINALANALYSIS Although they look deeper and more talented than head

coach Kenny Dillingham’s prior three teams, the Sun Devils will need to
prove that they didn't regress too much at quarterback with their move
from Leavitt to Boley. The Sun Devils are likely to be pegged to finish in
the middle of the Big 12, but they look better than that. A brutal schedule

wont help them show it in the win column, though.

BAYLOR BEARS


===== Baylor =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 62 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 3-6 BIG 12

J

OFFENSE Quarterback DJ Lagway holds the keys to the Baylor offense this

season. [he former 5-star prospect comes to Baylor — where his dad was
a running back 25 years ago — after two up-and-down years at Florida.
“Every day | wake up, it’s a blessing, because I’m living six-year-old me’s
dream,” Lagway says.

Lagway is one of many new faces expected to fill a lot of holes on of-
fense, as the Bears replace their top four receivers, tight end, running back,
quarterback and four of five starters on the offensive line.

After redshirting last season, wide receiver Louis Brown IV is expected
to play a key role on the outside, while Jadon Porter is back for his third
season in Waco and hopes to have a larger role. Dre’lon Miller did a little
bit of everything at Colorado and will have a similarly large role at Baylor.

Dawson Pendergrass will lead the backfield after missing last season
with a foot injury, but he has three promising underclassmen behind him.

DEFENSE After two years of calling plays on defense, head coach Dave
Aranda has handed those duties to first-year defensive coordinator Joe
Klanderman, who spent the past six seasons at Kansas State. Klander-
man’s scheme is predicated on creating havoc and getting pressure on the
quarterback, something Baylor struggled with mightily last season. “One
of the things we’re talking about now is havoc rate,” Klanderman says.
“We're trying to create that by letting those defensive linemen rumble.
We're more of an attacking style than we are a reacting style.”

The Bears focused on adding pressure-inducing game-wreckers in
the front seven, including Hosea Wheeler from Indiana, Jamaal Whyce
Jr. from Marshall, Ryan Davis from Kansas State, Garrick Ponder from
Southern Miss and Kedrick Walker from Georgia State.

Cornerback LeVar Thornton Jr. had a strong season last year and
returns to anchor the back end, while Devin ‘Turner, a former All-Big Ten
safety at Northwestern, returns after missing last year with a torn ACL.

SPECIALISTS Palmer Williams was a finalist for the Ray Guy award after

averaging 46.9 yards per punt. Ihe Bears are replacing their starting
kicker, Connor Hawkins, who was a late exit via the transfer portal, with
sophomore Rhett Armstrong, who was highly rated coming out of high
school and handled kickoff duties last year, although he hasn't attempted

a field goal in a game. [he return spots remain an open competition.

FINALANALYSIS ‘This is a do-or-die season for Aranda. The school decided

to bring him back for a seventh season just to keep things stable when
former athletic director Mack Rhoades left in a lurch near the end of last
year. [here is no magic number that will save his job, but Aranda knows
that the pressure is on to win, and win now.

BYU COUGARS


===== BYU =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 16 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 8-1 BIG 12

OFFENSE ‘The Bear is back. Quarterback Bear Bachmeier flourished as a

freshman in 2025 after not even arriving on campus until June. After
participating in spring drills at Stanford last year, Bachmeier stunned
everyone by beating out two seasoned backups and then led the Cougars
to the Big 12 Championship Game, where they lost to Texas Tech for the
second time in a month. Bachmeier started in all 14 games for the 12-2
Cougars, completing 65% of his passes for 3,033 yards and 15 touch-
downs, with only seven interceptions. Even bigger things are expected of
the Southern California native in 2026.

Another notable returnee is running back LJ Martin, the 2025 Big 12
Offensive Player of the Year. The senior ran for 1,305 yards last year and
can become one of the most prolific running backs in school history —
top three, at least — with another big year.

DEFENSE Utah head coach Kyle Whittingham’s departure for Michigan
left a dent at rival BYU as well, as three-year Cougars defensive coordina-
tor Jay Hill followed his former coach to Ann Arbor. Hill’s defense in his
third season at BYU was among the Big 12’s best — allowing just 332.4
yards and 19.1 points per game. Head coach Kalani Sitake decided on
special teams coordinator Kelly Poppinga to fill that role, and Poppinga
has a fairly full cupboard to work with.

Utah transfer Keanu ‘Tanuvasa will be the heart of the front seven,
aided by former Cal linebacker Cade Uluave, a gem from the transfer por-
tal. Junior safety Faletau Satuala will help the new secondary coaches get
settled in, but there are question marks, especially with defensive passing

game guru Jernaro Gilford joining Hill and Whittingham at Michigan.
SPECIALISTS Gone are kicker Will Ferrin, punter Sam Vander Haar and

long-snapper Garrison Grimes. Senior kicker Matthias Dunn has been
in the program for several years and will finally get his chance to shine —
at least until former five-star recruit Brody Laga returns from a church
mission. When Vander Haar didn't get a waiver for another season, BYU
turned to freshman Fuller Shurtz in spring camp, and the results so far
have been mostly positive.

FINAL ANALYSIS BYU just missed out on the College Football Playoff each
of the past two seasons, going 11-2 in 2024 and 12-2 in’25. So expecta-
tions are soaring in Provo for Sitake in his 11th season, especially after
the school broke the bank to keep the coach from going to Penn State
last December. BYU won't be sneaking up on anybody this year, but the
Cougars should have enough firepower and enough defense to contend

for another berth in the Big 12 Championship Game.
