CINCINNATI BEARCATS


===== Cincinnati =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 63 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-4 BIG 12

OFFENSE Cincinnati is replacing virtually every skill player from last sea-
son, starting with quarterback Brendan Sorsby, who committed to Texas
Tech via the portal as one of the highest-paid players in the country. To
replace him, the Bearcats landed on Georgia Southern’s JC French IV.
‘The wide receiver room has to be rebuilt to a large extent. Isaiah John-
son will have a big role thanks to his lightning speed. Additions like JV
Gibson, Larenzo Fenner, and Cade Wolford fill out the rest of the room.
The Bearcats boast a great offensive line that returns three starters —
All-American Evan Tengesdahl, Taran Tyo and sought-after left tackle
Joe Cotton. Expect the offense to lean heavily on the ground game with
running backs Zion Johnson, Gi’Bran Payne and Zylan Perry, and then

use the play-action pass, an approach that French is familiar with.

DEFENSE First-year defensive coordinator Nate Woody is implementing
a pressure-based 3-3-5 scheme, but as with the offense, the Bearcats are
replacing some studs from the 2025 season. Jake Golday and Dontay
Corleone will both play in the NFL, while defensive backs Christian
Harrison and Jiquan Sanks both transferred after the season.

Cincinnati added support at defensive tackle in Josh Hough and
Chidera Otutu, while solidifying the defensive backfield with safety ‘Ty
Goodwill and cornerback Kenny Worthy. Defensive end Marquaze Park-
er has been consistent since his true freshman season and projects to play
a massive role as the Bearcats’ best pass-rusher opposite outside lineback-
ers Brian Simms III and Thomas Johnson. Northern Illinois transfer Filip
Maciorowski will play the Golday role as a high-volume tackler capable
of getting after the quarterback.

Expect the scheme and heightened investment on the defensive side of
the ball to lead to much more chaos, with a big uptick in forced turnovers.

SPECIALISTS Cincinnati is returning placekicker Stephen Rusnak and

punter Max Fletcher, both of whom had great first seasons as starters and
should take another step forward. Isaiah Johnson makes the most sense as
the kickoff and punt returner — he’s the team’s fastest and shiftiest player.

FINALANALYSIS It will be hard for Cincinnati to have a better season in

2026 than it did in 2025, simply because of all the departed talent. French
should be the deciding factor. While he isn’t as explosive as Sorsby,
Cincinnati should have a better running game to make French’s job easier.
‘The defense played well enough last season to keep UC in games, but the

turnover numbers were not where they needed to be. ‘The new scheme

should help with that in 2026.

HOUSTON COUGARS


===== Houston =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 19 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 10-3, 6-3 BIG 12

OFFENSE Quarterback Conner Weigman is coming off a career-best season
(2,705 passing yards, 25 touchdowns, 700 rushing yards, 11 rushing TDs)
and an MVP performance in the Texas Bowl. Weigman was healthy this
offseason and will have the same offensive coordinator (Slade Nagle) in
consecutive seasons for the first time. He will also mentor 5-star freshman
Keisean Henderson, who should play some with an eye on 2027.

Weigman has plenty of weapons at his disposal with All-Big 12 wide
receiver Amare [homas, who had 12 touchdown catches and just missed
a 1,000-yard season; running back Makhi Hughes, who amassed nearly
3,000 rushing yards in two seasons at Tulane but saw limited action at
Oregon in 2025; and Patrick Overmyer, an NFL-caliber tight end trans-
fer from UTSA who must replace Tanner Koziol, one of the nation’s most
productive players at the position the past two seasons.

A retooled line added three starters from the portal — left guard
Shadre Hurst, center Anthony Boswell and right tackle Drew Terrill.

DEFENSE Graduation, not the transfer portal, left several big holes for
second-year defensive coordinator Austin Armstrong.

The secondary is the unit’s strength, with All-Big 12 cornerback Will
James, free safety Kentrell Webb and strong safety Jordan Allen all starting
13 games last season. Jalen Mayo, who had four interceptions at FCS
Stephen F. Austin, will slide into a corner spot, while fellow transfer Javion
White (Tulane) is expected to start at nickel. True freshman Paris Melvin
Jr. could see action in the secondary, at receiver and as a kick returner.

Up front, the Cougars must replace Carlos Allen, the team’s leading
tackler and interior force, and sacks leader Eddie Walls III. Off the edge,
the Cougars return Brandon Mack II (five sacks) and Latreveon Mc-
Cutchin and add Ashton Porter, a regular contributor for Oregon. The
interior boasts depth, led by Khalil Laufau, Oklahoma State transfer
De’Marion Thomas, Myles Parker and Travis Buhake.

Sione Fotu (12 starts) is back at middle linebacker, while Carmycah
Glass and Jaden Yates (Ole Miss) will handle the weakside duties.

SPECIALISTS Houston will have a new placekicker, and Zac Yoakam is the
only candidate with college experience. Evan Noel, the No. 1 kicking

prospect in the class of 2025, and Jonathan Dimas will also compete.
Punter Liam Dougherty is coming off a career-best 44.1-yard average.

FINALANALYSIS Expectations are high in head coach Willie Fritz’s third

season. If Weigman has another big year, and a rebuilt defense plays up to
potential, the Cougars could find themselves in the Big 12 Championship

Game.

IOWA STATE CYCLONES


===== Iowa State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 70 | BIG12 PREDICTION: 16 | 2025 RECORD: 8-4, 5-4 BIG 12

OFFENSE ‘Three-year starting quarterback Rocco Becht followed former
head coach Matt Campbell to Penn State, leaving Arkansas State transfer
Jaylen Raynor to take over. Raynor threw for 3,361 yards and 19 touch-
downs in 2025 and brings experience to a new system led by a former
Campbell staffer — offensive coordinator Tyler Roehl, who returns to
Ames after a stint with the Detroit Lions. Roehl’s scheme is expected to
lean more on downhill rushing and play-action passing.

The skill positions feature a mix of returnees and intriguing additions.
Aiden Flora is back at running back and will get help from Bowling
Green transfer Cameron Pettaway, the 2025 MAC Freshman of the Year.
At receiver, Dominic Overby returns, while Tulane transfer Omari Hayes
and FCS Tarleton State’s Cody Jackson add needed explosiveness.

The offensive line will be almost entirely rebuilt. Oklahoma transfer
Jake Taylor, Montana’s Colin Amick and Tarleton State’s Braden Smith
—an FCS All-American as a redshirt freshman — headline a group that
must come together quickly for this offense to fully click.

DEFENSE Iowa State suffered a crippling injury in the spring when Toledo
transfer safety Braden Awls went down with a torn ACL. The only good
news is that the staff will have all summer to develop talent to replace him.
Despite heavy turnover, lowa State retained several key contributors,
including defensive linemen Zaimir Hawk and Jace J. Gilbert, linebacker
Carson Willich and defensive backs David Coftey and Drew Surges.
New head coach Jimmy Rogers and defensive coordinator Jesse Bobbit
also brought familiarity with them from Washington State. Linebacker
Sullivan Schlimgen, defensive linemen Max Baloun, Isaac Terrell (7.0

sacks in 2025) and Malaki Ta’ase, along with defensive backs Duhron

Goodman and Trillion Sorrell, should play significant roles in the rotation.

SPECIALISTS Junior placekicker Kyle Konrardy is one of the best in the

country and gives lowa State a reliable scoring option. Pettaway, who led
the nation with a 33.0-yard kickoff return average, adds explosiveness to
the return game, while Flora averaged 26.1 yards per punt return with a
touchdown. Australian Ethan Gurney will compete for the punting job.

FINAL ANALYSIS More than 50 Cyclones entered the transfer portal,
including 16 starters and a staggering total of 24 players who followed

Campbell to Happy Valley. Rogers wasted no time reshaping the roster.
More than 45 transfers arrived in Ames, giving the Cyclones one of the
most transformed teams in the country. With that level of turnover, 2026
represents a full reset, but there’s enough incoming talent to remain com-

petitive in the always-changing Big 12.

KANSAS JAYHAWKS


===== Kansas =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 52 | BIG12 PREDICTION: 11 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 3-6 BIG 12

OFFENSE Andy Kotelnicki is back as Kansas’ play-caller, but he must re-
place prolific quarterback Jalon Daniels. Kansas will turn to Cole Ballard,

Daniels’ backup in recent years, or dual-threat sophomore Isaiah Marshall.
The new QB will share a backfield with Dylan Edwards, a former top-
150 recruit and Kansas native who joins his third program after stops at
Colorado and Kansas State. “Andy’s really going to enjoy finding different
ways to be creative with him,” head coach Lance Leipold says of Edwards.

Kansas has depth at running back with transfers Yasin Willis and Jalen
Dupree. Cam Pickett, who tied for the team receptions lead last fall with
45, headlines the wide receiver group along with transfers Nik McMillan
and Nahzae Cox. Sophomore tight end Carson Bruhn returns and will
team with Old Dominion transfer Jailen Butler.

The O-line returns established starters at left tackle (Calvin Clements)
and left guard (Amir Herring) and welcomes a transfer group that in-

cludes tackle Nick Morrow (Cal) and Kasen Carpenter (Oklahoma State).

DEFENSE Kansas will try to upgrade a unit that struggled against the run
and didn’t generate enough takeaways in 2025. Leipold likes the depth

at defensive end with top pass-rusher Leroy Harris III, Alex Bray and
Dakyus Brinkley joining Michigan State transfer David Santiago. Blake
Herold, who had five tackles for a loss in 2025, leads the defensive tackles.

The defense has a centerpiece in linebacker Trey Lathan, who had a
team-high 86 tackles, seven for a loss, an interception, a forced fumble
and two fumble recoveries. Leipold expects transfers Bam Crouch (Bos-
ton College) and Jibreel Al-Amin (Marshall) to help on the outside.

In the secondary, Austin Alexander, Jalen ‘Todd, Taylor Davis and Syeed
Gibbs return, and Kansas added several transfers, including Corey Gordon
(Louisville), Roman Pearson (Ball State), Khijohnn Cummings-Coleman
(Iowa State) and Jaden Harris (Georgia). “The depth is going to give us
opportunities to play more guys and be a fresher defense,” Leipold says.

SPECIALISTS Edwards’ arrival and the return of wide receiver Tate Nagy
give Kansas intriguing options in the return game. Ihe team added place-
kicker Martin Connington from Michigan State, who connected on 12-
of-16 field-goal attempts last year, and Australian punter Matthew Gill.

FINALANALYSIS Kansas has some urgency in Leipold’s sixth season. Kotel-
nicki’s management of the quarterback candidates, Edwards and a poten-
tially deeper offensive line will be key, and Kansas also could have greater
depth at all three levels on defense. An opening stretch that features
Missouri at home and Arizona State in London could shape the season.

ey KANSAS STATE WILDCATS
(GS

OFFENSE Avery Johnson, the single-season leader and career co-leader

for touchdown passes at Kansas State, is back for his senior season at
quarterback. Once he got more usage in the running game last season, the
offense performed measurably better. But 2025 was still an underwhelm-
ing year for him, likely the product of working with his third offensive
coordinator in three years and missing key offensive weapons through
most of the season. Reuniting with new head coach and former K-State
offensive coordinator Collin Klein should provide a big boost to his game.

Joe Jackson started slowly at running back last season, but he came on
strong, rushing for a school-record 293 yards against Utah. Rodney Fields
Jr. transferred from Oklahoma State to make a reliable top two.

Jaron Tibbs was a surprisingly strong WR2 in 2025 and now takes over
the top spot. Upside transfers like Josh Manning and Izaiah Williams will
play significant roles as well. Former 5-star recruit Linkon Cure could be
the X-factor with his involvement either as a tight end or spread out wide.

The offensive line will be the key in 2026 after the Wildcats lost most

of their starters and were forced to turn to the portal for reinforcements.


===== Kansas State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 31 | BIG12 PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 6-6, 5-4 BIG 12

DEFENSE K-State had to focus its portal attention on virtually every posi-
tion after losing nearly all of its defensive starters from 2025. ‘The defen-
sive end and safety spots got the most attention, including the addition of
Wendell Gregory from Oklahoma State, the sixth-ranked edge defender
in the portal. His performance, along with that of prolific pressure-applier
Elijah Hill, will be one of two critically important positions that must
excel for the defense to have a strong season. [he other is at corner, where
the Wildcats will return Zashon Rich and Donovan McIntosh.

The Wildcats have seemingly addressed the pass rush and pass defense,
so stopping the run will be the biggest question mark for the new-look
defense.

SPECIALISTS Special teams have taken a big step back over the last few
seasons at K-State. Bryce Noernberg’s burst as a kick returner is a good
foundation, but he had some turnover problems in 2025. Placekicking
shouldn't be an issue, but winning field position with punting will be the
biggest factor on special teams. In each of the past three seasons, K-State

has finished in the bottom half of the Big 12 in net punting.

FINALANALYSIS Klein, a Heisman Trophy-contending quarterback for the
Wildcats during his playing days, returns to Manhattan as the favorite

son, but fans should be patient. The offense should be Big 12-caliber, but
crucial holes in the defense may prevent K-State from competing for a
league title in Klein's first season.

===== PAGE 98 =====
OSU

U The core of the Oklahoma State offense followed new head
coach Eric Morris from North Texas. Quarterback Drew Mestemaker led
the nation in passing yards for the Mean Green last year at 4,379 yards
and will try to replicate that production with the Cowboys.

Running back Caleb Hawkins set the FBS record for touchdowns by
a freshman with 29 at North Texas last season, to go with 1,434 rushing
yards. Another North Texas transfer, receiver Wyatt Young, was Meste-
maker's favorite target, finishing with 1,264 receiving yards last season.
Transfers Justin Bowick from Illinois and Chris Barnes from Wake Forest
arrive with high expectations of fitting well into a pass-happy offense.

While the offensive line has some veterans, their development and
chemistry will be critical. Incoming transfers like Jacob Sexton from
Oklahoma, Tyler Mercer from Kansas, Joseph Hanson from Coastal Car-
olina and a trio of players from North Texas will be among those tasked
with protecting Mestemaker.


===== Oklahoma State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 36 | BIG12 PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 1-11, 0-9 BIG 12

Less is known about the direction the defense will take under

Skyler Cassity, who at 32 is one of the youngest defensive coordinators
in the nation. In his one season at North Texas, he trimmed the Mean
Green's points allowed per game by nearly 10 from the previous year.

Cassity uses multiple fronts and often likes to put five defensive backs
on the field. His front will feature a talented group of edge rushers, in-
cluding Jaleel Johnson and James Williams, as well as proven, productive
linebackers like Ethan Wesloski, Tate Romney and Isaiah Chisom.

‘The secondary has a blend of veterans like cornerback Mo Horn and
safety Quinton Hammonds, and emerging young players such as corner-
back LaDainian Fields and true freshman safety Braeden Presley.

iLislo Morris brought in an entirely new group of specialists,
beginning with placekicker Sam Keltner from SMU, who will be asked to
increase his consistency after going 14-of-20 on field goals as a redshirt
freshman last season. Australian punter Lachie Pozzobon showed a knack
for accuracy and hangtime while averaging 41.8 yards per punt as a fresh-
man at FCS Stephen F. Austin. Barnes will be the biggest threat in the
return game, where he posted some highlight-reel plays at Wake Forest.

Morris’ track record for offensive success was a big reason
for his hiring and the offense will be expected to produce from the start.
Morris brought in 18 players from North Texas, so there could be grow-
ing pains, but this is Morris’ third stop as a head coach, so he understands
the challenges of building a program. Ihe Cowboys went winless in Big
12 play the past two seasons, so the bar for improvement remains low.

===== PAGE 100 =====
TEXAS TECH RED RAIDERS


===== Texas Tech =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 12 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 8-1 BIG 12

TM

OFFENSE With the uncertainty around star quarterback Brendan Sorsby’s
eligibility, Tulsa transfer Kirk Francis appears to be first in line for the
job. Francis started 10 games at Tulsa and racked up 3,045 yards and 18
touchdowns in his 18 total appearances. Redshirt freshman Lloyd Jones
III is a backup option who tore his ACL his senior year of high school
and appeared in one game in 2025. Will Hammond, last season’s backup,
is recovering from his own torn ACL, though he is projected to be back
sometime early in the season.

The Red Raiders have a fully stocked arsenal around the QB, whoever
he is. Running backs Cameron Dickey and J’Koby Williams return fol-
lowing phenomenal sophomore seasons. Ihe tandem combined for nearly
2,000 rushing yards last season and scored 25 total touchdowns.

Texas Tech returns three starters on the offensive line — tackles How-
ard Sampson and Jacob Ponton and center Sheridan Wilson.

Sorsby has several weapons at his disposal this season, including sec-
ond-team All-Big 12 tight end Terrance Carter Jr. and Pitt transfer wide
receiver Kenny Johnson.

DEFENSE Replacing several NFL Draft picks on the defensive side of the
ball is the toughest task ‘Texas Tech is facing this season.

Following in the footsteps of the nation’s 2025 sack leader David Bailey
are transfers [rey White and Adam Trick. Their production is enticing.
White tallied 19.5 sacks the last two seasons at San Diego State, while
Trick finished with 12.5 tackles for a loss last season at Miami (Ohio).

The Red Raiders recruited Kansas State linebacker Austin Romaine
out of the transfer portal to pair up next to Big 12 Championship Game
Most Outstanding Player Ben Roberts. The most important returner on
defense, however, is cornerback Brice Pollock. He finished with five inter-
ceptions last season and was named first-team All-Big 12.

SPECIALISTS Lou Groza Award semifinalist and All-Big 12 first-teamer

Stone Harrington returns for his senior season as the incumbent place-
kicker. Harrington won the job last season with a big leg that helped him
nail a career-long 58-yard field goal against Utah. [he Red Raiders must

replace their punter after the graduation of starter Jack Burgess.

FINAL ANALYSIS ‘The schedule presents intriguing measuring sticks with
Houston, Arizona and Oklahoma State. It’s likely that the Red Raiders
will be marginally worse defensively, purely because of the personnel they're
losing to the NFL. Still, even without the services of its top signal-caller,
Texas Tech’s roster is top-to-bottom the most talented in the Big 12.

===== UCF =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 65 | BIG12 PREDICTION: 15 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 2-7 BIG 12

- After a season of quarterback uncertainty and injuries, UCF is
banking on James Madison transfer Alonza Barnett III to stabilize the
offense. Barnett was the 2025 Sun Belt Player of the Year after throwing
for 2,806 yards, rushing for 589 and accounting for 38 total touchdowns
while leading JMU to a College Football Playoff appearance. ‘That kind of
proven production is badly needed after UCF averaged just 24.3 points per
game in 2025 and scored touchdowns on only 55% of its red-zone trips.

‘The run game also gets a major reset. Louisville transfer Duke Watson
brings explosiveness after averaging 8.9 yards per carry as a freshman in
2024, while FCS Central Arkansas transfer Landen Chambers arrives af-
ter a 1,273-yard season. UCF also hopes former top recruit Taevion Swint
can make an impact after redshirting in 2025.

There are solid returning pieces in the passing game. Duane Thomas Jr.
led UCF with 53 catches for 528 yards; tight end Dylan Wade added 43
receptions for 523 yards and five touchdowns; and Waden Charles contrib-
uted 26 catches for 290 yards as a true freshman. FCS Monmouth transfer
Josh Derry has the résumé to become the go-to target on the outside.

Up front, right tackle Preston Cushman returns, but the line will again
lean heavily on portal additions, including Buffalo transfer Henry Tabansi.

U Et ‘The strength should be the secondary, which returns almost in-
tact. Cornerback Jayden Bellamy had eight breakups and one interception,
Antione Jackson added five breakups, Braeden Marshall picked off two
passes, and Demari Henderson finished with 61 tackles.

Linebacker Lewis Carter headlines the front seven after leading the
team with 92 tackles, while tackles RJ Jackson Jr. and Horace Lockett give
UCE experience inside.

The biggest question is edge rusher. UCF posted 27 sacks in 2025, but
10 came from departed veterans Nyjalik Kelly and Malachi Lawrence.
Returners Isaiah Nixon and Sincere Edwards should step into larger roles,
and UCF will count on Akron transfer Bruno Dall immediately.

- ‘This is a full reset. Texas transfer Will Stone has a big leg
and handled kickoffs for the Longhorns, but he has never attempted a
field goal in a college game. Wisconsin transfer Atticus Bertrams brings
experience at punter and should help steady the unit.

) 19 This is an important season for head coach Scott Frost to
start showing that his second stint at UCF can look more like his first
than his tenure at Nebraska. Barnett gives UCF a proven winner at the
game's most important position. If he can steady the offense, bowl eligi-
bility should be a realistic expectation. If not, the questions about Frost’s
long-term future in Orlando will get louder.

===== PAGE 102 =====
UTAH UTES


===== Utah =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 20 | BIG 12 PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 11-2, 7-2 BIG 12

OFFENSE ‘The Utes offense is transitioning from former offensive coordi-
nator Jason Beck to veteran play-caller Kevin McGiven, who arrives from
Utah State and will look to maintain a strong run game while attempting
to establish a more productive passing attack. Utah welcomes an infusion
of proven talent from the portal, including Braden Pegan from Utah State
(60 receptions, 926 yards, five touchdowns last season) and Kyri Shoels
from San José State, who will complement up-and-coming returners from
last season.

Dynamic quarterbacks Devon Dampier and Byrd Ficklin, who return
in a “tag-team’ effort, highlight the offense, along with leading rusher
Wayshawn Parker. But they'll have to get the job done behind an offen-
sive line that replaces five starters. Ihere’s optimism that the line can still
be effective, but the success of the offense will largely depend on how well
that line functions this season.

DEFENSE ‘The defense lost several key players from last season’s team to
graduation or the portal, but the unit was able to reload in areas of need.
A big area of focus — adding more talent to the defensive line after the
Utes were gashed in the run game in the latter part of the season. Utah
addressed that need in the portal by adding proven players Jireh Moe
(San José State) and Lucas Samsula (Wyoming), among others, and the
line looks poised for improvement.

Utah added size and physicality to its defensive backs room, while the
linebackers provide more versatility than in previous seasons. As a whole,
Utah has a veteran defense, one that can provide a bit more versatility for
new head coach Morgan Scalley and new defensive coordinator Colton
Swan to stress opposing offenses.

SPECIALISTS Utah didn’t need its special teams unit to do too much last
year, but Dillon Curtis returns as the team’s placekicker and Orion Phil-
lips as the team’s punter. Ihe two provide consistency and experience to a
part of the game that is expected to improve overall. Additionally, Mana
Carvalho looks to be a game-changer in the return game with a year of
experience under his belt.

FINALANALYSIS Utah underwent a major change when Kyle Whittingham
stepped away after 21 seasons and accepted the head-coaching job at

Michigan. But Scalley has been groomed for the top spot and looks to
provide consistency and continuity after several years with the program.
He’ll have to do it with a new staff and 18 new faces on the roster in
his first season, but his experience with the program should allow for a
smooth transition as the Utes pursue a Big 12 title.

===== PAGE 106 =====
ILLINOIS FIGHTING ILLINI


===== Illinois =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 33 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 9 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 5-4 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Head coach Bret Bielema found a replacement for three-year
starting quarterback Luke Altmyer at Kast Carolina, where Katin Houser
had two strong seasons after starting his career at Michigan State. Houser
made a strong impression in the spring, winning over his new teammates
with his talent and leadership.

Houser will be operating behind a mostly new offensive line. Guard
Brandon Henderson is the lone returning starter. Line coach Bart Miller,
who has been at Illinois since Bielema took over in Champaign, spent the
spring trying to fit the pieces together. One big addition: former Wiscon-
sin center Jake Renfro, the son of former Illinois offensive lineman Rick.

Tailback is set with the return of Aidan Laughery and Ca’Lil Valen-
tine, who combined for 996 rushing yards and seven scores. [he coaches
felt good enough about the position to move Kaden Feagin to tight end,
where he could have a major impact.

Hudson Clement and Collin Dixon, who caught 36 and 35 passes,
respectively, in 2025, figure to be Houser’s top targets.

DEFENSE When defensive coordinator Aaron Henry left for Notre Dame
after the season, Bielema decided to take the unit in a totally different di-

rection, hiring former FCS Montana head coach Bobby Hauck to install
a 3-3-5 defensive scheme and show the Big Ten something new. Bielema
is counting on the element of surprise to give his team a boost.

‘There’s experience in the secondary, where safeties Matthew Bailey and
Xavier Scott return. Bailey led the team with 76 tackles in 2025. Senior
cornerback Juice Clarke should be an honors candidate.

Up front, Ilinois is counting on veteran Pat Farrell, who learned from
star pass-rusher Gabe Jacas last season. James Kreutz and Daniel Brown
are the top returnees at linebacker.

SPECIALISTS David Olano won the Music City Bowl for Ilinois with a

late field goal against Tennessee, then got an offer he couldn’t refuse and
transferred to Texas A&M. Ethan Moczulski returned to Illinois after a
season at Washington and will handle placekicking duties. [linois added

punter Lars Rau from Texas State.

FINALANALYSIS The 2024 and’25 Illinois football teams set the school
record for wins in a two-year stretch with 19. What Bielema and his
staff really want, though, is a spot in the College Football Playoff. The
schedule includes a pair of preseason top-5 teams — Ohio State and
Oregon — but every other game is winnable. Still, [linois is one of the
least-experienced teams in the country a year after being one of the most
experienced. Another nine-win season is the likely best-case scenario.

INDIANA HOOSIERS


===== Indiana =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 5 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 16-0, 9-0 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Head coach Curt Cignetti follows the maxim of “production
over potential” when it comes to transfers. Josh Hoover, the former TCU
quarterback who'll replace Heisman ‘Trophy winner Fernando Mendoza,
is that adage come to life. Hoover is the most productive returning QB
in the FBS with 9,629 passing yards and 71 touchdowns across 36 games
(31 starts). He has a turnover issue that needs fixing and doesn’t have the
same stature as Mendoza — he’s very generously listed at 6-foot-2 — but
Hoover is the kind of heady player who will thrive in Cignetti’s scheme.
He will also benefit from the tremendous amount of experience on the
offensive line. Ihe group features three returning starters, including reign-
ing Big Ten Offensive Lineman of the Year Carter Smith at left tackle.
Wide receiver Charlie Becker closed out 2025 looking like one of
the country’s top playmakers and made a series of spectacular grabs that
helped [U win the national title. He will line up next to Michigan State’s
former top receiver Nick Marsh to give IU a potent passing attack.
Sophomore running back Khobie Martin is one to watch as well. The
reserve has waited his turn and looks primed to have a breakout season.

DEFENSE Indiana added one of the top defensive ends (Tobi Osunsanmi)
and cornerbacks (AJ Harris) out of the portal while bringing back six

starters, including a pair of starting defensive tackles (Tyrique Tucker and
Mario Landino) and linebackers (Rolijah Hardy and Isaiah Jones).

It’s an impressive mix of talent that will enable defensive coordinator
Bryant Haines to create the same kind of havoc as he did last year. The
Hoosiers had the second-most sacks in the FBS (46) and led the country
in tackles for a loss (129) by mixing in stunts and twists up front, along
with simulated pressures and disguises in the back end.

SPECIALISTS Indiana special teams units were the best in the country. Re-
turning placekicker Nico Radicic has been a sneaky weapon, having gone
28-of-30 on field-goal attempts since 2024. 1U has plenty of talented
athletes — receivers Tyler Morris and Shazz Preston along with running
back Lee Beebe Jr. — capable of making an impact in the return game.

FINALANALYSIS Cignetti’s impeccable track record in Bloomington makes
it easy to envision a path back to the postseason. While Indiana needs to
replace its first-ever Heisman winner and a handful of James Madison
transfers (Aiden Fisher, Elijah Sarratt and D’Angelo Ponds) who became
household names in the Big Ten, Cignetti has been the best talent eval-
uator in college football in recent years. Behind the scenes, there’s a high
degree of confidence that IU has built a lasting winner.

===== PAGE 108 =====
IOWA HAWKEYES


===== Iowa =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 25 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-3 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Iowa added a program-record 16 players from the transfer portal,
but none of them play quarterback. Head coach Kirk Ferentz seems to

be saying that he believes he already has a capable successor to Mark
Gronowski on the roster. Sophomore Jeremy Hecklinski and junior Hank
Brown were in a hotly contested battle in the spring, with Hecklinski
appearing to have a slight edge after having moved ahead of Brown on
the depth chart about midway through the 2025 season. Neither played
enough in 2025 to know how they would handle being the starter.

Iowa also has to replace three starters on the offensive line, all of whom
made first-team All-Big Ten last season, including center Logan Jones,
the 2025 Rimington Trophy winner as the nation’s top center.

Three receivers arrived via the transfer portal, and Iowa has a rising star
at tight end in sophomore and former walk-on DJ Vonnahme, who led
the team with 434 receiving yards and three touchdown catches in 2025.

Kamari Moulton returns at running back after rushing for 878 yards
and scoring five touchdowns last season.

DEFENSE Veteran defensive coordinator Phil Parker has eight starters to
replace on defense, including all four on the defensive line. Parker is wide-
ly regarded as one of the top defensive coordinators in college football,
and he will certainly have a chance to show why this fall.

Iowa loaded up in the transfer portal, adding four linemen and four
defensive backs, including safety Tyler Brown from James Madison.

Junior Zach Lutmer is also poised to be lowa’s next great defensive
back after earning second-team All-Big Ten honors last season. He has
played safety, cornerback and the cash position, which is a cross between a

linebacker and safety.

SPECIALISTS Iowa’s placekicker, punter and All-America return specialist
Kaden Wetjen all departed. The Hawkeyes also have a new special teams
coordinator, with Chris Polizzi replacing the highly successful Le Var
Woods, who is now the assistant head coach and special teams coor-
dinator for Michigan State. lowa turned to the transfer portal, landing
placekicker Eli Ozick and two punters — Australia native Boston Everitt
and ‘Tanner Philpott. Replacing Wetjen could require a group effort.

FINALANALYSIS Iowa has typically been built to win anywhere from seven
to 10 games under Ferentz, and this season appears to be no exception
despite the loss of so many starters. If the quarterback position rises to the
challenge, Parker continues to work his magic on defense and if the special
teams do their part, lowa could make some noise, but those are big “ifs.”

===== PAGE 110 =====
MICHIGAN WOLVERINES


===== Michigan =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 13 | BIGTEN PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 7-2 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Michigan’s 68th-ranked scoring offense gets a boost from a new
face running the show and plenty of returning production. Offensive
coordinator Jason Beck follows new head coach Kyle Whittingham from
Utah, and Beck hopes to unlock quarterback Bryce Underwood after a
disappointing freshman season. Look for the Wolverines to run more
spread and to use Underwood more frequently in the run game.

Running back Jordan Marshall (932 yards, 10 TDs) returns and has
some help in 5-star freshman Savion Hiter. Freshman star receiver
Andrew Marsh (651 receiving yards, four TDs) elected to return, and
Michigan dipped into the transfer portal to land JJ Buchanan (Utah) and
Jaime F french (Texas) for more passing targets.

With four starters back and more depth than last year, Michigan's
offensive line is primed to make a jump as well.

DEFENSE Jay Hill from BYU takes over as defensive coordinator and
wants to replicate the 2023 Michigan national title unit. That means

varied looks, simulated blitzes, a mix of zone and man-coverage — all in
the name of confusing opponents and forcing turnovers.

All-America defensive end John Henry Daley (11.5 sacks) follows the
staff from Utah and will be an anchor up front, while Michigan is bullish
on defensive tackles Trey Pierce and Enow Etta.

Linebacker is thin, but the Wolverines’ secondary is seasoned and full
of playmakers. Starting cornerbacks Jyaire Hill and Zeke Berry are back,
along with a healthy Rod Moore at safety. Michigan added All-Big 12
defensive back Smith Snowden from Utah and Memphis safety Chris
Bracy, a disruptive playmaker. If the unit can stay healthy, Michigan’s
defense has the ability to become a top unit in college football.

SPECIALISTS Veteran assistant Kerry Coombs takes over a special teams
unit that struggled with fundamentals in 2025 and used the transfer
portal to restock. Cameron Brown from UNLLY is the favorite to win

the punting job, while Pitt’s Trey Butkowski was 20-of-23 on field-goal
attempts as a freshman. Marsh made some explosive plays in the return
game last year and is a front-runner to handle the job again this fall. Fel-
low wideouts Salesi Moa and Ffrench could be in the mix there as well.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The cupboard is far from bare for Whittingham, who
makes the jump to Michigan after more than 30 years at Utah, the final 21
as head coach. Whittingham and his staff used the portal to help fill some
holes, though concerns with depth and a difficult schedule could make for
some growing pains. A 9-3 season might be the ceiling for this team.

MICHIGAN STATE SPARTANS


===== Michigan State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 72 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 17 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 1-8 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Starting quarterback Alessio Milivojevic continues to run with
the opportunity after throwing for 986 yards across four starts in 2025.
Twenty-five sacks in those games show he’ll need a quicker trigger and
help from his offensive line, built from portal additions like tackle Ben
Murawski (UConn) and center Trent Fraley (North Dakota State). In the
backfield, UConn transfer Cam Edwards’ 1,240 yards from scrimmage
ranked 13th in the FBS last year, but that came against a weaker schedule.
Offensive coordinator Nick Sheridan's calling card is putting playmakers
in position to perform. Tight end-quarterback-receiver-magician Carson
Gulker, a transfer from Div. II national champion Ferris State, should give
him lots of options, as should receiver Chrishon McCray. This group will
float around average, but it’ll make you say “wow” a few times each game.

DEFENSE When coordinator Joe Rossi put together his staff, he focused on
energizers. [his defense will run into some personnel problems, but the
Spartans hope they can develop inferior talent beyond expectations.
Senior Jordan Hall is the Mike linebacker, and he gets some help with
productive transfers Caleb Wheatland (Auburn) and Dion Crawford
(Buffalo). Nikai Martinez returns at safety with old teammate Charles
Brantley, who spent last season at Miami (Fla.), coming back at corner.
Michigan State took the ball away from its opponents only nine times
last season, the sixth-lowest total in the FBS. That had more to do with
a defensive front that couldn't generate pressure than it did a secondary
lacking true playmakers. The defensive line room is thin again, which will
cause problems.

SPECIALISTS Coordinator LeVar Woods made Iowa special teams famous,
and now he leaves Kirk Ferentz’s staff to do the same at Michigan State.
The devil is in the details, he says, and his players learned well. It took
him four pages of notes to install plays for Aussie punter Rhys Dakin,

who follows his coach from Iowa and fills the shoes of NFL-bound Ryan
Eckley. Transfer placekicker Liam Boyd missed more short kicks (4-for-6)
than long (3-for-4) at Charlotte, but Michigan State’s offense may need
him kicking from long range anyway. In the return game, a lot of speedy
wideouts will compete for reps.

FINAL ANALYSIS A lot of players need to get a lot better, quickly, for Mich-

igan State to win more than four or five games this season. But that’s
expected for new head coach Pat Fitzgerald and Co. in what amounts to

a rebuilding year. Success will be measured by how this season prepares
the team for the next, which is probably the last thing the Spartan faithful
want to hear. That’s the price of changing coaches twice in three seasons.

===== PAGE 112 =====
BM MINNESOTA GOLDEN GOPHERS


===== Minnesota =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 42 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 11 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 5-4 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Head coach PJ. Fleck turned heads at Big Ten Media Days last
year when he shared his goal of a College Football Playoff bid in 2025

despite an inexperienced quarterback. That didn’t happen, but QB Drake
Lindsey built a foundation. His play — 63% completion rate, 2,382 pass-
ing yards, 18 touchdowns and only six interceptions — inspired hope for
a potential jump in his redshirt sophomore season.

Minnesota retained its Nos. 2-4 pass-catchers and added three wide-
outs via the portal, with Cincinnati's Noah Jennings topping the list.

To help Lindsey out, Minnesota must improve in the run game. The
Gophers ranked 116th in the nation at 3.57 yards per carry. Poor offen-
sive line play was a key factor, and Minnesota is banking on improve-
ments from returning starters and contributors.

Senior running back Darius ‘Taylor has worked with a chef and taken
Pilates classes to be at his best. He led Minnesota with 915 yards from
scrimmage (670 rushing, 245 receiving) last season. If he reaches 1,500

this fall, it will certainly enhance Minnesota’s postseason prospects.

DEFENSE ‘The Gophers have the seventh-highest percentage of returning
production for 2026 according to ESPN, with 71% of their offense and
65% of their defense coming back.

The key defection, however, was rising junior safety/returner/receiver
Koi Perich, who transferred to Oregon. While Perich had a sophomore
slump in 2025, his departure might lower Minnesota’s ceiling.

The Gophers retained redshirt junior safety Kerry Brown, who was
more reliable than Perich a year ago, and have other playmakers, nota-
bly linebacker Maverick Baranowski (tied for sixth in Big Ten with 103
tackles) and defensive end Anthony Smith (Big Ten-best 12.5 sacks). The
biggest addition via the portal is T'J Bush Jr. from Cal; the senior end has
amassed 91 pressures and 13 sacks across three years.

Minnesota lost its top four defensive tackles from last year, meaning

310-pound Marshall transfer Naquan Crowder needs to be an anchor.

SPECIALISTS Field-goal kicking was a struggle last season, leading to a
competition between Beckham Sunderland, a Michigan transfer, and
Daniel Jackson. But neither has attempted a collegiate field goal. Punter
Tom Weston had a quality first season, but with Perich’s exit, the Gophers
will need to find primary returners for both punts and kickoffs.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The Gophers are in the bottom half of the Big Ten in
paying players but have consistently finished in the top half of the league.

That should continue this fall, especially if Lindsey can make a leap.

===== PAGE 114 =====
NORTHWESTERN WILDCATS


===== Northwestern =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 57 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 14 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 4-5 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Northwestern has never made a hire quite like new offensive
coordinator Chip Kelly. The 62-year-old’s task is upgrading an offense
that has flatlined for some time — 129th nationally in scoring (21.3 ppg)
since 2014. “You don't feel like there are any wasted plays,” head coach
David Braun says of Kelly’s system. “The ability to change the tempo at
which youre playing, but still operate within the offense, is seamless.”

Kelly recruited Aidan Chiles while coaching UCLA and will work with
the Michigan State transfer quarterback, who showed promise early in his
career but was benched late last season.

Northwestern didn’t take any transfer wide receivers and will lean on
Griffin Wilde, a third-team All-Big Ten selection in 2025, and emerging
Haydon Eligon II. The team is bullish about tight end transfers Alex Ho-
nig (6-foot-6, 275) and Luke Dehnicke, but Braun notes, “The mid-tier
of that wide receiver room is going to have to elevate.”

Running back projects well with Caleb Komolafe, Joseph Himon II,
short-yardage ace Robby Preckel and several transfers. The offensive line
improved in 2025 but must replace multiple NFL departures. Oklahoma
State tackle Grant Seagren is the biggest addition, and he joins standout
interior lineman Ezomo Oratokhai.

DEFENSE Northwestern should have one of the Big Ten’s best secondaries,
headlined by all-league safety Robert Fitzgerald, cornerback Josh Fussell
and nickel Braden Turner. A healthy Damon Walters at field safety is key,
but Braun likes the depth and talent.

Linebacker and defensive end should be solid, especially if transfers

Kobie McKinzie (Oklahoma) and Jamaal Johnson (UCF) complement

holdovers Braydon Brus and Michael Kilbane, who had 7.5 tackles for a

loss and 3.5 sacks in 2025. Northwestern has questions at defensive tackle
around senior Brendan Flakes.

SPECIALISTS Northwestern added Bowling Green transfer placekicker
Jackson Kleather. Decorated recruit Nikola Dugandzic should take over

punting in his second season, while Himon and wide receivers Drew
Wagener and Chase Farrell should handle returns. Assistant coach Paul
Creighton will focus solely on special teams this season.

FINALANALYSIS Kelly’s arrival and the October opening of the $862 mil-

lion brand-new Ryan Field fuel excitement. Northwestern is investing in
football, and Braun wants to become a College Football Playoff con-
tender. His plan might take time, though, as Northwestern visits Indiana,
Oregon and Ohio State and hosts Penn State and lowa. A third bowl
appearance in four seasons is a reasonable goal.

OHIO STATE BUCKEYES


===== Ohio State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 1 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 9-0 BIG TEN

gan,

OFFENSE If Julian Sayin can remain efficient and further develop his game
— perhaps with more bursts from the pocket — there’s no reason why he
cant be a Heisman Trophy contender once again. He looks to build on
3,610-yard, 32-touchdown season in which he completed an eye-popping
77% of his pass attempts.

Sayin still has plenty of firepower at his disposal, headlined, of course,
by the beastly Jeremiah Smith and also featuring veteran Brandon Inniss
and newcomer Chris Henry Jr.

Sayin also will have the benefit of a power-running game, which is a
trademark of new offensive coordinator Arthur Smith. Running backs Bo
Jackson and Isaiah West were borderline sensational last year and figure
to help balance the attack while maneuvering behind an experienced
offensive line. Four starters return up front, a group led by centerpiece
Carson Hinzman and left tackle Austin Siereveld.

DEFENSE After guiding the nation’s top stop unit last year, coordinator
Matt Patricia has major holes to fill, including the loss of Arvell Reese,
Sonny Styles and Caleb Downs, all top-seven overall NFL Draft picks.
Still, there are athletes aplenty, and assistants Larry Johnson, James Lauri-
naitis and Tim Walton are all elite at developing their position groups.

The D-line will feature end Kenyatta Jackson Jr. and Alabama transfer
James Smith, one of several large interior defenders with surprising lateral
quickness and an ability to stuff the run. The linebacking corps will need
to learn on the fly but already is showing promise.

In the secondary, Jermaine Matthews Jr. is welcoming his new role as
the Buckeyes’ top corner, while Duke transfer Terry Moore should emerge
as a focal point at free safety.

SPECIALISTS Ill-timed missed kicks, lackluster punt returns and incon-
sistent kick coverage have haunted OSU over the years. Baylor transfer
Connor Hawkins will try to steady the ship at placekicker. Meanwhile,
Joe McGuire is back after averaging a middling 42.4 yards per punt last

season. Inniss should feature as a top return option.

FINAL ANALYSIS Head coach Ryan Day is a proven survivor who has

adapted well enough to scale the summit of modern college football. His
new CEO-type approach should be empowering to Arthur Smith and
Patricia, both former NFL head coaches. ‘This staff will have to hustle

to fine-tune a roster littered with a program-record 51 newcomers and

a loaded schedule, but if the defense is able to reload, all championship
possibilities are on the table.

===== PAGE 116 =====
OREGON DUCKS


===== Oregon =====
* NATIONAL FORECAST: 6 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 13-2, 8-1 BIG TEN

OFFENSE ‘The story of the offseason for Oregon was how many draftable
Ducks opted to return in 2026, headlined by quarterback Dante Moore.
The “thunder and lightning” duo of Jordon Davison and Dierre Hill Jr.
combined for 1,323 rushing yards as freshman backups, and now they're
the feature backs.

Receivers Dakorien Moore and Jeremiah McClellan are also stepping
into bigger roles as sophomores; the X-factors there will be incoming
UAB transfer Iverson Hooks and returner Evan Stewart, who has much
to prove after a season lost to a knee injury. Kenyon Sadiq is gone at tight
end, but there will be minimal drop-off to Jamari Johnson.

The Ducks hope the same can be said about the new-look line, led by
another returner who spurned the NFL Draft — center lapani Laloulu.

DEFENSE ‘The entire starting defensive front is back after also opting to
skip the 2026 draft and await 2027. With Bear Alexander and AMauri
Washington plugging up the middle, and Matayo Uiagalelei and Teitum
Tuioti manning the ends, this could be the best four-man front in the
country. [he Ducks added depth through the portal but also hope their
own development of Aydin Breland, Matthew Johnson and Nasir Wyatt
will pay dividends there.

Jerry Mixon leads the inside linebackers after a breakout 2025 season,
and Devon Jackson brings elite athleticism to the position. Brandon Fin-
ney Jr. is one of the best young corners in the country, if not one of the best
corners, period. The portal did bring help on the back end, in safety Koi
Perich (Minnesota) and nickel Carl Williams [IV (Baylor). Perich looks
like a more than capable replacement for the departed Dillon Thieneman.
Watch for a potential breakout season from corner Naeem Offord.

SPECIALISTS ‘The kicking game will be a question mark until proven
otherwise, after the Ducks graduated their kicker and punter. When the
placekicking unit takes the field, it’s possible all three guys who touch
the ball will be portal additions — long-snapper RJ Todd, punter/holder
Bailey Ettridge and placekicker Keaton Emmett, who will look to beat
out returners Gage Hurych and Rocco Graziano.

FINAL ANALYSIS Under head coach Dan Lanning, Oregon football is now

recruiting and competing like one of the top five or 10 programs in the
nation. [here’s one last hurdle to clear: bringing home the school’s first
national championship. With new coordinators on both sides of the ball,
it’s conceivable that there could be some early growing pains this fall. But
with all the talent returning, the goal is crystal clear.

PENN STATE NITTANY LIONS


===== Penn State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 18 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 3-6 BIG TEN

OFFENSE New head coach Matt Campbell speaks fondly of his decade

at lowa State, so it’s no surprise that six former Cyclones could start for
the Nittany Lions, led by quarterback Rocco Becht. A three-year lowa
State starter, Becht is the most experienced FBS quarterback in college
football, with 39 career starts, 26 wins and more than 2,500 snaps. He was
limited during spring drills while rehabbing from offseason surgery to his
non-throwing shoulder but threw in Beaver Stadium with a VR headset.

Becht brought his four favorite targets with him, notably 6-foot-7 tight
end Benjamin Brahmer, who will continue the program's recent string of
draft picks at the position. Carson Hansen, who rushed for 952 yards for
the Cyclones last season, will share running back duties with Ohio State
transfer James Peoples.

With four new starters, the offensive line needs a crash course in
preseason camp. Still, offensive coordinator Taylor Mouser is comfortable
throwing a lot of NFL concepts at the group.

DEFENSE Defensive coordinator D’Anton Lynn, an All-Big Ten corner-
back at Penn State, returned to his alma mater after three seasons in Los
Angeles (two at USC, one at UCLA). He signed four transfer tackles who
weigh at least 319 pounds. The headliner is Siale ‘Taupaki, who played for
Lynn at UCLA and is entering his eighth season of college football.

By adding interior size, Lynn seeks to funnel plays to the linebackers.
Campbell recruited three from lowa State, notably Caleb Bacon and
Kooper Ebel, who combined for 17.5 tackles for a loss last season. ‘They'll
work alongside Tony Rojas, Penn State’s top returning defensive player,
whose torn ACL before the UCLA game was a turning point.

In the secondary, Campbell has been particularly impressed with Zion
Tracy, a Swiss Army knife who can play multiple spots. Former Lowa

State safety Marcus Neal Jr. (11 TF Ls in 2025) will captain the secondary.

SPECIALISTS Campbell was able to retain kicker Ryan Barker, who led the
FBS in field-goal percentage (94.7) last season and didn't miss from inside

50 yards. Special teams coordinator Justin Lustig is back as well after a
successful first season with the program.

FINAL ANALYSIS Penn State was fortunate Campbell was available in De-

cember. His first season will lean heavily in an Iowa State direction, with
24 of the team’s 40 transfers coming from Ames. Becht is a gamer and
has the tools to score points. Lynn is reworking a defense that was a huge
disappointment last season but has cleaned up the majority of its issues.
Campbell is rebuilding Penn State for long-term viability. A College
Football Playoff bid, while possible, would be overachieving this year.

===== Purdue =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 85 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 18 | 2025 RECORD: 2-10, 0-9 BIG TEN

JFFENSE Ryan Browne is back for Year 2 as QB1. He has bulked up to

better withstand the rigors of the Big Ten. Browne can make all the
throws and can tuck and run — but he needs to be better.

Is there an alpha wideout in the house? Hopes are high for four
transfers and a juco. Drops were a big issue in 2025. Injuries have defined
George Burhenn’s career, but if he can stay healthy, the lithe tight end
could be special.

The offensive line received an infusion of size and depth with the addi-
tion of six portal linemen. But the best of the blockers is holdover tackle
Joey Tanona. It’s hoped that a beefier line and new OL coach (Zach
Crabtree) will pave the way for a promising collection of backs. Keep an
eye on Minnesota transfer Fame Ijeboi and Texas transfer Jerrick Gibson.

JEFENSE It was a bit of a surprise when Purdue brought back Kevin Kane
to reprise his role as defensive coordinator, a position he held for the Boil-
ermakers for two disastrous years under former head coach Ryan Walters.
Kane has a lot to prove for a defense that ranked 17th in the Big Ten in
total defense last year and has allowed at least 30 points per game each

of the past three seasons. [he unquestioned leader: linebacker Charles
Correa, the ultimate warrior.

Questions loom for a secondary that has six portal additions along with
two juco transfers. Hudauri Hines and Don Saunders, a Utah transfer,
should form a nice CB duo. Is there a pass-rusher in the house? All eyes
are on Georgia transfer Elo Modozie and juco transfer Jeremy Lewis. ‘The

tackle tandem of ‘TJ Lindsey and Ian Jeffries has potential.

SPECIALISTS Lots to sort out here. Purdue is auditioning kickers and punt-
ers, and fingers are crossed. Big things are expected from diminutive FCS
Jackson State transfer Travis Terrell Jr. in the return game. He is quick
and elusive. Purdue feels that special teams coach James Shibest gives
them a schematic edge.

“|NAL ANALYSIS Head coach Barry Odom couldn't stop the bleeding

during his maiden voyage last year, when he went 2-10 (0-9 Big Ten).
How bad have things been in West Lafayette? The program is riding an
18-game Big Ten losing streak and has gone 3-21 overall the past two
seasons with no victories over Power 4 opponents. The last Big Ten win?
It was in 2023. You get the picture. Odom has set his jaw and swears this
will be a bowl team. An easier schedule will help. The hope is that the ar-
rival of 29 portal players and four juco transfers brings instant results and
improvement for a beleaguered program in the midst of its worst stretch
in school history.

RUTGERS SCARLET KNIGHTS


===== Rutgers =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 69 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 16 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 2-7 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Rutgers must replace starting quarterback Athan Kaliakmanis,
who exhausted his eligibility after two of the best years at the position in
program history. [he Scarlet Knights will choose between rising redshirt
sophomore AJ Surace, who was Kaliakmanis’ backup for two years, and
Boston College transfer Dylan Lonergan, a former 4-star recruit who was
handpicked by Nick Saban at Alabama.

The Scarlet Knights retained their top two stars in running back
Antwan Raymond and wide receiver KJ Duff, who should be one of the
best players in the Big Ten this season, but they lost depth at wide receiver
with Ian Strong’s transfer to Cal and DT Sheffield’s graduation. Compe-
tition for the two spots behind Duff should be strong among a number of
returnees and talented incoming freshmen.

‘The offensive line is in flux, with starters Gus Zilinskas (center) and
Bryan Felter (left guard) having graduated. Kwabena Asamoah is a lock
to start at right guard, but the two tackle spots and left guard were wide
open in the spring. New offensive line coach Jim ‘Turner, who replaces
two-time Super Bowl champion Pat Flaherty, has a lot to sort out.

DEFENSE Looking to upgrade the worst Rutgers defense in the Big Ten
era, head coach Greg Schiano revamped his coaching staff, firing co-co-
ordinators Robb Smith and Zach Sparber and replacing them with FCS
South Dakota head coach Travis Johansen. Johansen then hired a number
of familiar faces from his lower-level stops to fill out the rest of his staff.

There will be multiple key position battles. Cornerback should be the
“most competitive” of the bunch, per Schiano, as all three starting spots
will feature new faces. Rutgers will look to strengthen its interior line
with the return of Doug Blue-Eli, who missed last season with injury, and
coaches hope that one of the defensive end transfers could be the first
Scarlet Knight to collect five sacks in a season in a decade.

SPECIALISTS Rutgers is returning its starting kicker (Jai Patel), punter (Ja-
kob Anderson) and long-snapper (Michael O’Connor) from a unit that
was solid last season. The Scarlet Knights fair-catch a majority of punts
and kick returns, but they remain very aggressive in trying to block kicks.

FINALANALYSIS Rutgers brought back two of the best offensive weapons

in the Big Ten, has depth at wide receiver and should be better defen-
sively than last year’s debacle. But between replacing one of their greatest
quarterbacks ever, the many unknowns on defense and a lot of movement
on the offensive line, there are enough question marks to make earning a
bowl bid — the minimum expectation for the Scarlet Knights in Year 7 of
Schiano’s second stint at the school — far from a guarantee.

Vel UCLA BRUINS


===== UCLA =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 43 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 3-6 BIG TEN

OFFENSE UCLA has a new head coach (Bob Chesney) and an influx of

transfers but a returning quarterback in Nico lamaleava, who has made
big portal news before but chose to stay in Westwood. lamaleava strug-
gled at times during his UCLA transition but also displayed immense
talent and completed 64.4% of passes while adding 505 rushing yards.

“He’s not timid, he’s not afraid, he’s not sitting back there wondering
what to do,” Chesney says. “He has a good grasp of the offense. He’s very
capable physically. He loves this game, and he’s not humble out there.”

lamaleava will throw to a transfer-heavy receiver group with speed and
a willingness to block. San José State transfer Leland Smith and Landon
Ellis, who joined Chesney from James Madison, should lead the group
alongside Power 4 transfers Brian Rowe (South Carolina) and Semaj
Morgan (Michigan).

Running back projects well with Wayne Knight, a star at JMU, joining
returnee Anthony Woods. UCLA will need a transfer tight end to
emerge. [he offensive line features portal pickups like tackle Jordan Davis
(South Alabama) and guard Riley Robell (James Madison) as well as a

key returnee in guard Eugene Brooks.

DEFENSE ‘The secondary is the unit’s strength, as safety Cole Martin, cor-
nerback Rodrick Pleasant and nickel Scooter Jackson return. They will be
joined by potential impact transfers like Utah safety Tao Johnson, whom

Chesney calls “a phenomenal football player,” and James Madison nickel
DJ Barksdale.

The Bruins have an intriguing linebacker tandem in returnee Jalen
Woods and Oklahoma transfer Samuel Omosigho, who had 50 tackles

and two sacks last season. “A really commanding presence,” Chesney says.

‘The defensive line is almost completely new. Sun Belt Freshman of the
Year Sahir West and Aiden Gobaira both made the move out West from
James Madison. Power 4 imports like Darold DeNgohe (Rutgers), Tyson
Ford (Cal) and Maxwell Roy (Ohio State) will be in the rotation.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Mateen Bhaghani has connected on 83% of his
career field-goal attempts, including 39-of-45 inside 50 yards. UCLA
added transfer punter Curtis Gerrand, who averaged 43 yards for Sam
Houston last season. Knight averaged 9.5 yards per punt return in 2025.

FINALANALYSIS Chesney enters the Big Ten with a largely overhauled
roster, and UCLA needs the new pieces to click — especially along both

lines and at receiver. The Bruins begin with challenging nonleague games
against Cal and San Diego State, but their conference slate isn't particu-

larly grueling until November. The JMU transfers and Iamaleava create a
chance for Year 1 success.

USC TROJANS


===== USC =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 14 | BIGTEN PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 7-2 BIG TEN

‘CG
OFFENSE USC returns one of the nation’s most efficient quarterbacks in
Jayden Maiava, an offensive line loaded with experience and a gifted run-
ning back tandem in Waymond Jordan and King Miller, who combined
to average 6.3 yards per rush in 2025. All five starters up front return,
anchored by tackles Elijah Paige and Justin Tauanuu, as USC seeks con-
tinued improvement in the run game.

“If we can keep these guys healthy, this group will have the opportunity
to be the most dominant that we've had,” head coach Lincoln Riley says
of his O-line. “Closing out those tough road games and winning these
championship games, that’s the next step. A lot of that will lean on our
line and on our backs.”

Maiava finished fifth nationally in passing yards (3,711) and second
in QBR (89.9) last season. He will throw to a mostly different group, as

USC loses Biletnikoff Award winner Makai Lemon, All-Big Ten tight
end Lake McRee and wideout J’Kobi Lane. USC brings back Tanook

Hines, adds NC State transfer Terrell Anderson and welcomes in decorat-
ed recruits like receiver Boobie Feaster and tight end Mark Bowman.

DEFENSE Hall of Famer Gary Patterson, the former head coach at TCU,

takes over a defense with elevated expectations, especially with increased
depth up front. USC’s defensive line was the group that Riley said had the
furthest to go when he arrived. ‘The front now features greater depth at
both spots with players like ends Braylan Shelby and Kameryn Crawford,
tackles Jide Abasiri and Jahkeem Stewart and transfers like end Zuriah
Fisher (Penn State). Riley also likes the depth at cornerback, where Mar-
celles Williams is back, Prophet Brown and Chasen Johnson return from
injury and transfer Jontez Williams (Iowa State) arrives.

The Trojans must replace All-America safety Bishop Fitzgerald but

return Christian Pierce, nickel Alex Graham and others.

SPECIALISTS New special teams coordinator Mike Ekeler arrives from
Nebraska to oversee a group that returns standout kicker Ryon Sayeri
while adding Memphis transfer Lachlan Carrigan at punter. Lemon also
handled most of the returns, so Hines, Trent Mosley and others figure to
be bigger factors in that department.

FINAL ANALYSIS ‘The Trojans’ roster is less plug-and-play and more home-
grown, bolstered by line depth, talented backs, a potentially elite quar-
terback in Maiava and an influx from one of the nation’s top recruiting
classes, which is comprised of players who will contribute without being
totally relied upon. “The roster has improved drastically in the last couple
of years,” Riley says. “It’s primed to take that next step.”

W WASHINGTON HUSKIES


===== Washington =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 17 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 5-4 BIG TEN

OFFENSE Washington took a massive step forward on offense in 2025,
rocketing up the ranks of the Big Ten to No. 4 in scoring offense (34.1
points per game) and No. 5 in total offense (409.9 yards per game). With
junior quarterback Demond Williams Jr. back in the fold after his brief
flirtation with the transfer portal, head coach Jedd Fisch is looking for his
unit to take another step forward.

However, Williams is going to have to do it without his best weapons.
Wide receiver Denzel Boston and running back Jonah Coleman departed
for the NFL Draft, as did left tackle Carver Willis, while running back
Adam Mohammed and wide receiver Raiden Vines-Bright both hit the
portal. But with four starters returning along the offensive line, Washing-
ton is very confident after reeling in the highest-rated recruiting class in
school history.

The Huskies are projecting big roles for 4-star recruits Jordan Clay,
Mason James and Trez Davis at wide receiver, while running back Brian
Bonner Jr. is expected to be part of the rotation at an inexperienced po-
sition. Left tackle Kodi Greene, a 5-star recruit, is expected to start right
away and give the offense a boost.

DEFENSE Defensive coordinator Ryan Walters has similar expectations for
the youth on his defense, which includes an overhaul of the defensive line.

Led by junior Elinneus Davis, 4-star freshman Derek Colman-Brusa and
a trio of transfers in DeSean Watts, Darin Conley and Kai McClendon,
the line should be stout against the run and in rushing the passer.

The Huskies can also boast one of the nation’s best linebacking corps,
including seniors Jacob Manu and Xe’ree Alexander and sophomore
Zaydrius Rainey-Sale. Ihe secondary features All-Big Ten honorable
mention selection Alex McLaughlin and Freshman All-American Rylon
Dillard-Allen at safety, and cornerbacks Emmanuel Karnley, a Virginia
transfer, and sophomore Dylan Robinson, who has “first-round traits”
according to the staff.

SPECIALISTS Coordinator Chris Petrilli added second-team All-Sun Belt
kicker ‘Tyler Robles, who converted on 91.3% of his field goals (21-for-
23) at Texas State. Washington needed to upgrade at punter and landed
one of the nation’s best in Hunter Green from San Diego State, who
ranked No. 6 in the nation in average yards per punt last season at 47.0.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The schedule includes a few tough road trips — USC, Ne-
braska, Oregon — but if Williams can take a step forward after struggles

in the big moments last year, Washington should be able to get to 10 wins
and potentially knock on the door of the College Football Playoff.

WISCONSIN BADGERS


===== Wisconsin =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 55 | BIG TEN PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-7 BIG TEN

OFFENSE ‘The Badgers are pinning their hopes on a transfer quarterback for

the fourth consecutive season under head coach Luke Fickell. Former Old
Dominion QB and Sun Belt Offensive Player of the Year Colton Joseph
will take over with the hope that his rushing ability and downfield passing

will inject Wisconsin’s offense with much-needed explosiveness. Wis-
consin finished last season with the worst passing offense and the worst
scoring offense in the Power 4 and failed to generate shots down the field.
Joseph excelled at such plays at ODU and is expected to take advantage of
play-action snaps that create one-on-one situations for receivers.

Every position on the offense got a facelift through the transfer portal,

most notably the offensive line, a group that has failed to be the program's

Abu Sama III and USC back Bryan Jackson II arrived to bolster a back-
field that fell apart down the stretch of 2025. Joseph had more runs of 10

or more yards (37) than Wisconsin did as a team (33) last season.

DEFENSE Linebackers Cooper Catalano and Mason Posa were major reten-
tion wins for the Badgers after the pair emerged as freshmen to lead the
defense. Wisconsin recruited multiple inside linebackers to ensure it could
deploy Posa as a pass-rusher and in other situational roles to get him near
the ball as often as possible.

Transfer cornerbacks Cai Bates, Eric Fletcher Jr., Javan Robinson and
Bryce West all figure to be major pieces, and Missouri transfer Marvin
Burks Jr. gives the safety group the experience it lacked. There’s more
depth in this unit than there has been throughout Fickell’s tenure.

This unit’s success may come down to the progression of homegrown
edge defenders such as Nicolas Clayton and Jaylen Williams. If they ele-
vate the rush behind senior Sebastian Cheeks, the Badgers figure to build
upon a surprisingly good front last season.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Gavin Lahm redshirted last season in order
to be eligible this season. He’s shown a big leg as a kickoff specialist.

===== PAGE 126 =====
DELAWARE BLUE HENS


===== Delaware =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 107 | CUSA PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 4-4 CUSA

OFFENSE In their first year at the FBS level, the Blue Hens led Conference

USA in total offense and passing offense and were second in points per
game in 2025. Quarterback Nick Minicucci successfully orchestrated
head coach Ryan Carty’s spread offense, and he returns after earning
second-team All-CUSA status and leading the league in passing yards
(3,683) and total offense (3,918). He also rushed for 10 TDs.

The Blue Hens did lose the two best players on their offensive front
with Fintan Brose’s graduation and Anwar O’neal’s transfer to Louisville.
But all-league center Steven Demboski is a solid anchor for what should
still be a strong offensive line.

The Blue Hens return their top four rushers, led by Jo Silver, while
adding a versatile transfer in Kaderris Roberts from Bowling Green.
Leading receiver Sean Wilson (68 catches for 877 yards and three TDs) is
also back, along with the reliable Donovan Lewis, speedy Nick ‘Tyree and

several promising transfer additions.

DEFENSE Delaware ranked among CUSA’s worst in points, total yards
and passing yards allowed last year. The Hens were way too susceptible to
allowing explosive third- and fourth-down plays and aim to improve in
that area this year, as well as in forcing turnovers.

In safety KT Seay, a first-team AlI-CUSA choice, they return their best
defensive player, one who can cover ground and deliver a hit or snare a
takeaway. Inside linebacker Gavin Moul, third in CUSA in tackles last
year, is also a proven stopper.

Up front, Delaware will be looking for Noah Matthews, in his second
UD season, to exhibit the playmaking ability that landed him at Kentucky
to start his college career. Cornerback Jamarion Kolagbodi was the sec-
ondary’s most accomplished freshman last year, and the Hens will look for
contributions from newcomers such as George Mullins from Michigan
State and Jaquise Alexander from North Dakota State.

SPECIALISTS Second-team All-CUSA kicker Nate Reed will be tough

to replace, with Div. III Salisbury transfer Tucker Smack and freshman
Kason Mullis competing for that key role. Punter Josh Cupitt returns
after missing 2025 with a knee injury and has proven reliable in the past.
Delaware has a slew of potentially explosive return candidates.

FINALANALYSIS Delaware was better than most expected while going 4-4
in Conference USA and 7-6 overall in its debut FBS season, capped by
its 20-13 68 Ventures Bowl win over Louisiana. Now eligible for the first
time to play for the Conference USA title, Delaware — and its ardent
followers — will expect to be in championship contention this year.

FIU PANTHERS


===== FIU =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 112 | CUSA PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-3 CUSA

Keyone Jenkins left for UCF via the transfer portal, and head coach
Willie Simmons replaced Jenkins with transfer JJ Kohl. At 6-foot-7 and
250 pounds, Kohl is larger than most quarterbacks, and he is coming off
a solid 2025 performance at Appalachian State, completing 61.6% of his
passes for 1,465 yards, 12 touchdowns and only two interceptions.

Kejon Owens departed for the pro ranks following a 1,300-yard season,
but the Panthers return their No. 2 rusher, Anthony Carrie (469 yards,
five touchdowns last year). FIU also returns wide receivers Kyle McNeal,
Maguire Anderson and Jojo Stone, and the Panthers added Greg Gaines
III, a 1,000-yard receiver at FCS Rhode Island.

FIU lost all five offensive line starters and will rely on returnees Zaire
Flournoy and Antonio Tripp Jr. to anchor the unit.

DEFENSE Defensive end Kenton Simmons returns after a strong 2025
campaign in which he posted eight tackles for a loss and six sacks, earning
second-team All-CUSA honors. Dante Anderson, who had three sacks
last season, also returns to the edge. Germaine Carter, who had 19 tackles
and 1.5 sacks, will play a key role at defensive tackle, and West Virginia
transfer Elijah Simmons will factor in as well.

Sophomore middle linebacker Josiah ‘Taylor is the leading returning
tackler, having racked up 76 stops with 6.5 for a loss and two sacks in
2025. Percy Courtney Jr. returns as the weakside linebacker after finishing
third on the team with seven tackles for a loss.

Defensive back Shamir Sterlin, a Freshman All-CUSA performer last
year, returns after making 65 tackles. The secondary also boasts safety
Demetrius Hill, nickel DeAndre Boykins and corners Da’Vontae Floyd,

Jai-Ayviauynn Celestine and Lawrence Johnson.

SPECIALISTS Robert Czeremcha is poised to take over placekicking duties
after making 2-of-3 attempts last year. Former Miami (Fla.) transfer
Stone Springman is slated to be the punter, and Devonte Lyons and
Maguire Anderson will be the main return men. Anderson had a 97-yard
punt-return touchdown against UT'SA in the First Responder Bowl.

FINAL ANALYSIS FIU took a big step forward in its first year with Simmons

at the helm, notching the program's first winning season since 2018 and
first bowl appearance since 2019, boosting the team’s confidence and
proving to the players that the Panthers can taste success. But the depar-
ture of several key contributors due to graduation or transfer means that
the Panthers will have to rely on newcomers to keep the program moving

in the right direction.

JACKSONVILLE STATE GAMECOCKS


===== Jacksonville State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 106 | CUSA PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 9-5, 7-1 CUSA

OFFENSE Year 2 under head coach Charles Kelly will feature many familiar

faces, although the offense transitions to first-year coordinator Taylor
Housewright. Dual-threat quarterback Caden Creel returns after throw-
ing for 1,514 yards and running for 1,075 last season, with 16 total touch-
downs. Redshirt freshman Jack Moran is likely to serve as the backup.
The running back room will lose Conference USA MVP Cam Cook,
but it still has depth in Justus Savage, Andrew Paul, Khristian Lando,
Jalen Likely and Lucas Farrington. The offensive line features experienced
returnees Jeremiah Orr, Lance Johnson and Lucas Mrachkovskiy.
Leading receiver Deondre Johnson, who had 30 catches for 724 yards
and six touchdowns and stands 6-foot-8, returns alongside Dylan Gentry,
Franklin Baret Jr. and Gabe Glover. Missouri State transfer Ronnel John-
son (463 yards, two touchdowns) and FCS Murray State transfer Darius
Cannon (737 yards, three touchdowns) are also expected to be involved.

DEFENSE Despite some departures, the defense has a solid core returning.
Cornerbacks Tre’Quon Fegans and Jaheim Jenkins had strong seasons,
with first-team All-CUSA safety Caleb Nix also back after recording four
interceptions and three fumble recoveries in 2025.

Leading tackler Mac Sanders returns after recording 85 total stops
and a team-high 11.5 tackles for a loss as a freshman. Fellow linebacker
Walker O’Steen, who ranked second on the team with 84 tackles, will line
up alongside lan Mitchell and Myles Butler.

The Gamecocks took a hit on the defensive line, losing their two sack
leaders, and will turn to returnees Khurtiss Perry (21 tackles, 4.5 tackles
for a loss), Gharin Stansbury (six quarterback hurries, five tackles for
a loss) and Jawaun Campbell (22 tackles). Wisconsin transfer Angel
Toombs is also likely to find a spot in the rotation up front.

SPECIALISTS Jax State’s special teams unit returns first-team All-CUSA

kicker Garrison Rippa, who went 40-for-40 on extra points and had a
season-long field goal of 52 yards last season, and starting punter Conor
Donaghy. A native of Ireland, Donaghy averaged 39.6 yards per punt last

season. Cannon and Gentry are among Jax State’s options on returns.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The roster returns a strong core group and has made some
solid additions via the transfer portal. Establishing a run game alongside
Creel at quarterback will be the biggest key entering 2026 under a new
offensive coordinator. Ihe Gamecocks have played for a CUSA champi-
onship in each of the past two seasons — winning one — and made three
straight bowl appearances, proving they belong in the CUSA conversation

on a consistent basis. [his season looks to be no different.

KENNESAW STATE OWLS


===== Kennesaw State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 105 | CUSA PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 10-4, 7-1 CUSA

OFFENSE KSU’s high-octane attack tied for fourth in Conference USA

in total offense (402.4 yards) last season. Ihe Owls lost many of the key
components of their attack, notably quarterback Amari Odom, who trans-
ferred to Syracuse, and wide receiver Gabe Benyard, who graduated. The
quarterback job will fall to either Rickie Collins, who transferred from
Syracuse, or Landon Varnes, who came in from Mississippi Gulf Coast
CC. The pair emerged from spring practice essentially even.

The all-new crew of running backs is led by transfers Triston Morgan,
who ran for 809 yards for Div. II playoff team Benedict, and Latrelle
Murrell, who ran for 893 yards at FCS West Georgia. The similarly
retooled receiver group features holdover Javon Rogers, who began to
emerge in spring camp.

The offensive line will be bigger this year and features two holdovers in
tackles JaDarious Lee and Nikola Milovac, with Middle Tennessee trans-
fer Mateo Guevara coming off a dominant spring locked in at center.

DEFENSE ‘Ihe Owls allowed the fourth-fewest points (25.5) per game in
CUSA and tied for first with 31 sacks, and they were able to hold onto

important pieces of their defense.

Key returners include defensive end Marcus Patterson, rush end Dono-
van Westmoreland and middle linebacker Baron Hopson. Westmoreland,
formerly at South Carolina, was finally healthy and recorded 37 tackles
and four sacks.’Team leader Hopson decided to return as a grad student
after making 72 tackles (nine for a loss) and one interception.

The secondary returns nickel Kody Jones and athletic safety Isaac Paul,
who came back after dipping a toe in the transfer portal.

SPECIALISTS Daniel Kinney is finally healthy after battling an undis-

closed injury and will have the placekicking job to himself this year. He
played four games and was 6-for-7 on field goals with a 50-yarder. Matt
McKenzie is an Australian punter who appeared in six games last year for

Arizona State, averaging 34.6 yards on 12 kicks.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The Owls were one of the nation’s biggest success stories
last year, as new head coach Jerry Mack led the team to a 10-4 record, the
Conference USA championship and the school’s first bowl appearance.
Mack was named CUSA Coach of the Year and the Bear Bryant National
Newcomer Coach of the Year. It was only the fourth time a team has won
10 or more games in only its second season of FBS reclassification. ‘The
Owls won't be sneaking up on anyone this time, but they do host regu-
lar-season champion Jacksonville State in early October.
