===== PAGE 130 =====
Ee LIBERTY FLAMES


===== Liberty =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 91 | CUSA PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 CUSA

OFFENSE ‘There’s another quarterback battle on the Mountain as Liberty
hopes to get back to the offensive explosiveness that it displayed in 2023.
After a disappointing 2025 season, Ethan Vasko (151-of-266 passing,
1,961 yards, 10 TDs, 12 INTs) was sidelined throughout the spring
following offseason shoulder surgery to repair an injury that plagued him
throughout much of his first season. Liberty has brought in a pair of talent-
ed quarterbacks via the transfer portal in Deshawn Purdie (Wake Forest)
and Jaylen Henderson (West Virginia), both of whom are battling for the
job. The QB’s top target figures to be Jamari Person, the Flames’ leading
returning receiver (28 catches, 315 yards in 2025).

Liberty's rushing attack led the nation in 2023 and ranked fourth
nationally in 2024. Last season, the team’s production dipped below 250
rushing yards per game (194.0) for the first time in head coach Jamey
Chadwell’s tenure at Liberty. Now, Chadwell and the Flames will have to
sort through five incoming transfer running backs who will be running
behind an offensive line that should be the early strength of the offense,
returning four starters.

DEFENSE Shawn Quinn has arrived in Lynchburg as the team’s new de-
fensive coordinator, coming to Liberty from Virginia Tech, where he was
Brent Pry’s outside linebackers coach. Quinn will be looking to bring a
toughness and physical mindset to a Flames’ defense that ranked 10th out
of 12 CUSA teams in rushing defense last season.

He gets to work with some key returning players in the front seven, led
by linebacker Derrell Farrar, who paced the team with 71 tackles a season
ago. Will this group be able to pressure the opposing quarterback? ‘That’s

something that has plagued the Liberty defense the prior three seasons
under Chadwell.

SPECIALISTS Liberty finds itself looking for new specialists, as placekicker
Jay Billingsley and punter Max Morgan have exhausted their eligibility.
Tyler Black was on the team last year after transferring from Wake Forest
but lost the job in training camp. This season, he will have to compete
with incoming Div. I] McKendree transfer Chase Reeves. Reeves will also
be competing at punter with Sam Crossan.

FINALANALYSIS Coming off a 4-8 season, the program’s first losing
campaign in 20 years, Chadwell made several changes to his coaching
staff for the first time during his Liberty tenure. In addition to Quinn,
the Flames have also hired Cory Peoples from Georgia Tech to help lead
the secondary. Those changes, coupled with some enthusiasm behind the
incoming transfer quarterbacks, give Liberty some optimism heading into
this season.

Sy~| MIDDLE TENNESSEE BLUE RAIDERS


===== Middle Tennessee =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 133 | CUSA PREDICTION: 9 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 2-6 CUSA

OFFENSE Middle Tennessee lost quarterback Nicholas Vattiato (2,150

passing yards, 16 IDs), who ranked third in Conference USA in passing
efficiency at 122.9, to graduation. It’s sophomore Roman Gagliano’s job
now after he passed for 1,005 yards and seven touchdowns in five games
last season. Having some stability at quarterback is good news for the
Blue Raiders, who are coming off consecutive three-win seasons.

First-year offensive coordinator Anthony Scelfo has very few returning
skill players to work with. AJ Jones is the lone returning starter on offense.
Running back Antonio Martin Jr. (Kansas State) is an experienced trans-
fer portal addition to the backfield alongside DJ ‘Taylor, who averaged 7.7
yards per carry last season (albeit on only 26 carries).

The offensive line must improve after the unit surrendered 30 sacks for
224 lost yards in 2025. Otto Janse Van Rensburg returns from injury up

front.

DEFENSE ‘There was some slight improvement for the MTSU defense last
season, although the Blue Raiders still gave up 30.9 points and 399.3
yards per game. [he run defense was a bright spot, finishing second in
CUSA in allowing 143.1 rushing yards per game, compared to 197 yards
the year prior.

‘Three returning starters on the defensive line helped bring those num-
bers down, but all three are now gone. Defensive tackle Zeion Simp-
son-Smith had three sacks and a fumble recovery as a freshman and is
due for a bigger role.

Reggie Johnson leads all returning linebackers after tallying 34 tackles
and 3.5 sacks. Jackson Lowe (four pass breakups last season) returns to a
secondary that ranked 11th in the league in pass defense.

SPECIALISTS Dominic Bourgeois, a transfer from Div. II] Susquehanna,

is in line for placekicking duties. Ashton Logan returns at punter. He
averaged 44.7 yards on 66 punts and downed 29 of them inside the oppo-
nent’s 20-yard line in 2025. Taylor shared kickoff return duties last year,
averaging 17.4 yards per return in that role.

FINAL ANALYSIS Head coach Derek Mason finds himself in a potential
make-or-break season in his third year at the helm of the Blue Raiders.

MTSU ended its 2025 campaign on a positive note, winning its last two
games with its two highest-scoring outputs of the season (31 points in
both games), but the offense has to replace plenty of lost production while
adjusting to five new offensive coaches. Ihe defense was more physical last
season, but it will have to replace its four leading tacklers and returns only

7.5 of its 23 sacks.

(2) MISSOURI STATE BEARS

===== Missouri State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 128 | CUSA PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-3 CUSA

OFFENSE New Bears head coach Casey Woods will try to blend several
key returners with transfers at quarterback and other key offensive spots.
Quarterback transfers Henry Belin IV (Duke) and Skyler Locklear
(UTEP) have experience and different skill sets, but Woods thinks either

can effectively run the offense. “Both have got a chance,” Woods says.
“We've got versatility in our offense to fit their strengths.”

The quarterbacks have options in the passing game as Jmariyae Rob-
inson, who led Missouri State in receptions (44) and touchdown catches
(seven) last fall, is back alongside key returnees James BlackStrain and
Makai Cope and Tulsa transfer Mekhi Miller.

Missouri State also retained running back Ramone Green Jr., who will
pair with Boise State transfer Jambres Dubar. Woods likes the tight end
depth with Jeron Askren, Dillon Hipp and James Rexroat.

Offensive line depth is a concern, as the staff will need transfers such
as Isaiah Villanueva and Sebastian Espinoza to contribute, along with
returnees including Makhete Gueye and Logan Scrivner.

DEFENSE ‘The unit has a centerpiece in linebacker Jared Lloyd, who tied
for the team lead in tackles in 2025, and Woods likes the depth at edge.
Transfers Kellen Lindstrom, Jalile Elliott and Josh Lukusa will com-
plement Caden Wiest, who had two sacks last fall. Tim Brantley Jr.
headlines the tackle group, which includes lowa State transfer Markell
Chapman. “If that depth develops like I think it can, we've got a chance
to be pretty good on the defensive line,” Woods says.

Lloyd leads a linebacker group also returning Cooper Roy, Austin
Yelder, Jashawn Cooper and Martavis Blackmon. “He’s been a great off-
season leader for us,” Woods says of Lloyd.

Safety projects well with Dylan Dixson and Christian kord, who com-
bined for 102 tackles last season, both returning. Missouri State went to
the portal for two Joels — Kpassou and Boamah — at cornerback.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Yousef Obeid, who earned honorable mention
All-Conference USA honors last season, and primary punter Stewart

McDonald are both back. Robinson shared kickoff return duties in 2025
and averaged 19.4 yards per return.

FINALANALYSIS ‘There’s significant turnover on both lines, but if the

offensive front can protect Belin or Locklear, there should be playmaking
opportunities with the wideout and tight end depth. Missouri State has
trips to Texas A&M and SMU before beginning Conference USA play.
“I’m really excited for them to get in front of that,” Woods says. “It will be
a great measuring stick.”

gled to produce consistent offense, finishing 113th nationally in yards
per game. Io address that deficiency, they’re turning to junior transfer
quarterback Trey Hedden from FCS Furman. Hedden inherits a receiving
group with vertical threats, including TK King, who totaled 588 receiving
yards and three touchdowns last season after missing time early with an
injury. [he return of AJ Williams III adds intrigue after he missed 2025
due to injury following his transfer from Div. II] Mary Hardin-Baylor.
Protection and line play were also concerns. New Mexico State allowed
nearly three sacks per game in 2025, prompting the staff to bring in
multiple experienced offensive linemen through the transfer portal to
reinforce the unit and improve both pass protection and physicality.
The biggest issue last season came on the ground. The Aggies tied
for last in the FBS at just 2.6 yards per carry, often leaving the offense
one-dimensional. FCS Delaware State transfer James Jones and lowa
transfer I'J Washington Jr. join returning starter Dijon Stanley, and even
modest improvement in the run game should open up the passing attack

a SAM HOUSTON BEARKATS


===== Sam Houston =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 137 | CUSA PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 2-10, 1-7 CUSA

OFFENSE ‘There are reasons to be optimistic about this offense in Year 2
of the Phil Longo era. Ihe Bearkats will enter the 2026 campaign with
a QB1 in place, as Landyn Locke has solidified his hold on the job after
playing in six games for the Kats last year, completing 71 of his 141
attempts for 765 yards and four touchdowns.

Luckily, the Kats will have a lot of weapons returning who will have
spent an entire spring, summer and fall camp working with a single
starting quarterback and not a rotation. Senior wideout Chris Reed gives
Locke a dependable target. Reed played in all 12 games and led the Bear-
kats with 460 receiving yards and five touchdowns.

In the backfield, Landan “Coco” Brown and Alton McCaskill return
after combining for 858 rushing yards.

‘The biggest concern for this unit: rebuilding an offensive line that lost
every starter either to graduation or to the transfer portal.

JEFENSE ‘This marks the second season under defensive coordinator
Freddie Aughtry-Lindsay, and the Kats are hoping that they can mus-
ter some improvement after ranking last in CUSA in total defense last
season, allowing 483.5 yards per game. This will be the first year that
Aughtry-Lindsay will have had a full offseason to recruit his own players
and hire his own staff, but for a team that surrendered 37.8 points per
game, every move will need to yield positive results.

Defensive tackle Dylan Frazier provides a nice building block after
posting 41 tackles, four tackles for a loss and two sacks. Some Power 4
transfers — Josh Alexander-Felton (NC State), Stacy Bey (Arizona) and
Jace Arnold (Wisconsin) — should slide into the starting lineup after
seeing limited time at their former schools.

SPECIALISTS It seems that the placekicking job will belong to either trans-
fer Sam Carpenter or Tate Ryan. Carpenter went 1-of-3 on field goals

at Pitt last season, while Ryan took a redshirt at Sam Houston. Punting
duties will fall to transfer Cal Joseph, who averaged 38.1 yards per punt at
Wake Forest last year.

FINALANALYSIS It’s only his second season in charge, but this is looking

like a make-or-break year for Longo. A year after getting 10 wins and a
New Orleans Bowl victory in 2024, the Bearkats struggled to only two
wins in Longo’s debut. Things could shift in the staff’s favor, though, as
the team returns to play in refurbished Elliott I’ Bowers Stadium after
playing every game away from campus. Of course, that also removes any
excuses for another disastrous campaign.

WKU HILLTOPPERS


===== WKU =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 89 | CUSA PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 CUSA

OFFENSE Under head coach Tyson Helton, entering his eighth season, the
Hilltoppers have become accustomed to learning a new playbook on a
near yearly basis, with no offensive coordinator staying for more than two
years. Now it’s Bodie Reeder’s turn to work with the offensive-minded
Helton to keep the Tops among CUSA'’s best passing offenses. Reeder
spent the past two seasons at Middle Tennessee, and last season, the Blue
Raiders averaged 266.1 passing yards per game, fourth in CUSA.

The Tops likely have a quarterback battle brewing between sophomore
Rodney Tisdale Jr. and Florida State transfer Brock Glenn. Tisdale took
over starting duties at midseason and performed well, passing for 1,432
yards and nine TDs in nine games, but he lacks Glenn’s game experience.

Graduation and portal losses hit hard, but versatile senior K.D.
Hutchinson (team-high 66 catches for 618 yards, three TDs) is a proven
returning talent, and the Tops had no trouble attracting promising trans-
fers at the skill positions, including running back Ajay Allen (Tulsa and
Nebraska), wide receiver Nigel Johnson (FCS South Carolina State) and
tight end CC Ezirim (Toledo). ‘The offensive line is almost a complete
rebuild, with right tackle Karsten Upchurch the only returning starter.

DEFENSE Last year, the Tops allowed 22.8 points per game (second in
CUSA) despite giving up 400.8 yards per game (seventh in CUSA).
WKU was particularly strong in the red zone, surrendering touchdowns
only 34.7% of the time, the nation’s best mark.

Redshirt senior Jaylen Lewis emerged as an impact player at safety (74
tackles, team-high three interceptions) and joins fellow returning starter
Dave Herard to form a tough combo. Defensive end Venson Sneed and
defensive tackle Jakeem Fletcher return to provide some stability on the
defensive front, which lost standout Harper Holloman in the portal to
West Virginia. [he Tops are thin on experience at linebacker — leading
tacklers Jaylen Wester and Anthony Brackenridge both exhausted their
eligibility, while Koron Hayward transferred to 'CU and Miller Malone

went to Houston. Fall camp will feature plenty of competition for jobs.

SPECIALISTS Australian punter Daniel Wall, a freshman, looked strong in
the spring, while sophomore Michael Buck likely gets first shot at place-

kicking. Hutchinson is a dangerous option to return punts, with redshirt
sophomore Cameron Flowers back to handle kickoff returns.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The early schedule is brutal, and the Tops will have to

navigate that stretch and maintain cohesion to contend for a CUSA title
again. At the least, expect WKU to extend its bowl streak to eight straight
under CUSA’s longest-tenured current head coach.

===== PAGE 136 =====
NOTRE DAME FIGHTING IRISH


===== Notre Dame =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 10-2

OFFENSE With the departure of running backs Jeremiyah Love and
Jadarian Price, quarterback CJ Carr will be the focus following his 2,741
passing yards and 24-to-6 touchdown-to-interception ratio in his debut
season. Carr has the accuracy and moxie to mount a Heisman Trophy

candidacy.

The Irish have an unusually high number of receivers from which to
choose, led by 2025 leading receiver Jordan Faison, 2024 national champi-
onship game standout Jaden Greathouse and Micah Gilbert. Ohio State
transfers Mylan Graham and Quincy Porter will also be significant factors.

The offensive line features two young rising star tackles in Guerby
Lambert and Will Black. Third-year starter Anthonie Knapp shifts to left
guard from left tackle. The return to health of center Ashton Craig and
guard Charles Jagusah would make this a dynamic front wall.

Running backs Aneyas Williams and Nolan James Jr. won't match the
Love/Price explosiveness, but both have burst and tackle-breaking ability.

DEFENSE Second-year defensive coordinator Chris Ash has a bevy of

experience returning on the back seven, where linebackers Drayk Bowen
and Kyngstonn Viliamu-Asa, cornerback Leonard Moore and safeties
Adon Shuler and Brauntae Johnson helped lead the Irish to the nation’s
No. 10 scoring defense (17.6 points per game). Notre Dame’s 21 intercep-
tions in 12 games were the fourth-most nationally.

A pair of defensive tackle transfers — powerful Francis Brewu (Pitt)
and 6-foot-5, 330-pound Tionne Gray (Oregon) — join forces with
returnees Jason Onye and Elijah Hughes. Defensive end Boubacar Traore
is set for national acclaim, while Bryce Young should be ready to put it
all together. Alabama transfer Keon Keeley, who was committed to Notre
Dame before signing with the Crimson Tide, has returned to the Irish fold.

SPECIALISTS After two seasons of inconsistency, the Irish hope to stabilize
their placekicking with former Purdue standout Spencer Porath, who
converted 15-of-17 field goals in 2025. For the second time in three years,
the Irish have turned to an Australian punter — this time, Jasper Scaife.

FINALANALYSIS Since their College Football Playoff snub, Notre Dame and

head coach Marcus Freeman have been on the “Leave No Doubt” tour.
Freeman has been relentless in his approach to get his players dialed in to
right the wrong of last season. “I need to say, ‘keep the pain’ to get them to
remember why we have to struggle and why we have to sacrifice,” Freeman
says. “Nothing great is achieved if you don't have that edge, that motiva-
tion to struggle and sacrifice. We have that edge.” The Irish also have a
favorable schedule and the firepower to run the table in the regular season.

UCONN HUSKIES


===== UConn =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 103 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4

OFFENSE After a historic year for UConn offensively — its first ever with
a 3,000-yard passer (Joe Fagnano), a 1,000-yard receiver (Skyler Bell) and
a 1,000-yard rusher (Cam Edwards) — new head coach Jason Candle had
to start from scratch. He’s done so with a base of players who followed him
from Toledo, including quarterback Kalieb Osborne, who is likely to com-
pete with Tennessee transfer Jake Merklinger for the starting job, and run-
ning back Kenji Christian, who will be part of a room that includes Trey
Cornist from Central Michigan and Cyncir Bowers from West Virginia.
Candle has said that he hopes to have an offense that will take shots
down the field, and that should be a possibility with receivers like Ky
“Flash” Wilson from FCS Youngstown State and Toledo transfers Jediyah
Willoughby and Ryder ‘Treadway. Shamar Porter is one of the few return-
ers and provides a big target down the field.
The offensive line, with guard Ty Chan returning, is largely made up
of players who helped Toledo tie for 17th nationally in sacks allowed last

s€asonl.

DEFENSE ‘The Huskies’ strength under new defensive coordinator Ryan
Manalac figures to be at the linebacker position, which he coached for the
past five seasons at Pitt. K’Von Sherman, who had 94 tackles and 4.5 sacks
last year at [oledo, figures to lead an experienced group that includes FCS
Penn transfer John Lista and Luke Murphy from Coastal Carolina.

Esean Carter, another former Rocket, headlines the defensive line in
his senior season and is joined by a handful of players who have waited for
their opportunity, such as Joshua Mickens from Ohio State and Tahjae
Mullix from Oregon State. The defensive backfield is less experienced,
with a number of players, like junior safety Braedyn Moore, competing for
their first full-time role in college.

SPECIALISTS ‘The Huskies have brought in Mike Baker to kick after he

spent his first two seasons as a backup at Ole Miss, only attempting

and making four extra points. Spencer Sullins, who started his career at
Memphis, and Australian Tommy Warner, who started his career at FCS
Northwest Missouri State, are competing for the punter spot. Julian Al-
len, a former Toledo receiver, took most of the return reps in the Huskies’
spring game, but that role has yet to be determined.

FINAL ANALYSIS UConn brought in more than 70 new players and almost

an entirely new staff, but there is optimism around the program that Can-
dle, who never had a losing season in 10 years at Toledo, will be successful
in Year 1. That might not mean a third-straight nine-win season, but the
foundation is there for the Huskies to remain competitive.

ZZ, WKRON ZIPS


===== Akron =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 125 | MAC PREDICTION: 9 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 4-4 MAC

OFFENSE Akron is handing the offense over to transfer quarterback Reese
Poffenbarger, who has served as a backup to Cam Ward at Miami (F'la.)
and Drew Mestemaker at North Texas the past two seasons. He was very
productive at FCS UAlbany, throwing for 60 touchdowns and running
for another eight when he started in 2022 and ’23, averaging 3,306 yards
passing and completing 60% of his passes.

Poftenbarger will have experience at the skill positions around him.
Fifth-year senior Jordan Gant returns after rushing for 1,032 yards in his
first season following his transfer from FCS Tennessee State. He earned
second-team All-MAC honors. Backup Sean Patrick is also back after
averaging 6.9 carries per game as a freshman.

Two of Akron’s top three receivers are also back. Marcel Williams (641
yards) and Kyan Mason (481) combined for more than 1,000 receiving
yards a year ago. Williams is an AII-MAC candidate on the outside, and
Mason does his damage from the slot with his shifty athleticism.

The offensive line has seven players returning who were at a minimum
rotational players, and the unit has also added some transfers.

DEFENSE ‘The secondary is the strength of a unit that was ninth in the
MAC in scoring defense last season. Rodrick Hunter and ‘Terence Thom-
as are names to watch, and the coaches are high on Aamii Branch at nick-
el. ‘The hope is that former Oregon transfer Daymon David will return
from injury to man a safety spot.

Jason Hocker takes over at middle linebacker after seeing action in five
games last year. Ihe Frazier twins, Dahvon and Jahvon, return along the
defensive line and will provide support for Cyrus Durham, who will start
again after a productive sophomore season. Jon’[rell Mixon and Ron-
ald Hull were rotational players last season and give the defense needed
experience.

SPECIALISTS Both placekicker Matthew Schramm and punter Joseph
Castle return. Schramm made 12-of-13 field goals, including 4-of-5 from
beyond 40, after taking over the job early in the season. Castle averaged
42.9 yards per punt and had a long of 68 yards. Patrick will again be the

primary punt returner.

FINAL ANALYSIS Akron’s bowl ban for poor APR scores has been lifted,

and making the postseason is a reachable goal for the Zips. Poffenbarger
impressed in the spring and will be productive if the offensive line can
come together. [he schedule has three tough non-conference games in
UNLY, Wake Forest and Minnesota, and no Sacramento State in league
play, which probably makes six wins the ceiling for the Zips.

===== Bowling Green =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 122 | MAC PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-6 MAC

© Oregon transfer Austin Novosad finally gets the chance to start
after being QB2 behind Bo Nix, Dillon Gabriel and Dante Moore for
the Ducks. The former highly regarded 4-star recruit is mobile and has a
strong arm. He was in competition with Div. I] Assumption transfer Jay
Kastantin in the spring, but expect Novosad to be the guy in the fall.

The Falcons ran the ball 62% of the time, and if that ratio holds, expect
Austyn Dendy to get a lot of work. The former Missouri running back
came on strong at the tail end of 2025, rushing for 100-plus yards in three
straight games. He is BG’s leading returning rusher and has a shot at
1,000 yards if he stays healthy.

The offensive line gets a reboot with all five starters gone. James
Thomas Jr. had two starts and played 405 snaps, so his experience will pay
dividends at right tackle. Alexis Sanchez was a rotational player at guard.

The top pass-catcher at BGSU the past three years has been a tight
end, and sophomore Jeremiah Scoby will have a chance to be that fea-
tured player. Wideouts Brennan Ridley and Winn Sharp will move up

from reserve roles a year ago.

- ‘The front six has the potential to be strong for the Falcons. De-
fensive end Myles Bradley and defensive tackle Eriq George (son of head
coach Eddie George) led the team in sacks with five and four, respectively,
and will look to increase those numbers this year.

Middle linebacker Dorian Pringle was off to a fast start in 2025 with
27 tackles, two forced fumbles, an interception and a fumble recovery be-
fore being lost for the season in Week 3 against Liberty. He has a chance
to be an All-MAC player if he can stay healthy. Coaches also have high
hopes for Purdue transfer Sanders Ellis.

Safety Kal-El Pascal is the only returning starter in the secondary. He
was second on the team with 80 tackles and also recorded an interception
and a fumble recovery. Cornerback JoJo Johnson is projected to start after

eading the MAC with 13 pass breakups in 2025.

) ‘The Falcons will depend on walk-on placekicker Travis
Kenner, which isn't always ideal. Ryan Kingston averaged 41 yards per
punt last season at FCS Southeast Missouri State and was second-team

All-OVC.

IN. » Eddie George enters Year 2 with several unknowns but a

notentially el lite quarterback in Novosad. The running game has a depend-
able back, but there are questions along the offensive line. Defensively, the
secondary isn't deep, but there are difference-makers in the first two levels.

A bowl berth is the ceiling for this squad.

BUFFALO BULLS


===== Buffalo =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 118 | MAC PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 4-4 MAC

OFFENSE ‘The Bulls have only two returning starters on an offense that
ranked No. 93 nationally in points per game (24.0) last season. At quar-
terback, Jason Wright is a promising redshirt freshman with good size
and athletic ability. He has a high ceiling as a two-way threat, and it will
be worth watching how quickly he can pick up new offensive coordinator
Tony Tokarz’s system.

‘There is some experience in the backfield with Terrance Shelton Jr., the
team’s second-leading rusher a year ago (244 yards, one I'D) and Messiah
Burch, a gadget back who is capable of explosive plays. The wide receiver
room lost two All-MAC players to the transfer portal and must be rebuilt.
Jasaiah Gathings is the top returner with 410 yards and will be Wright’s
top target. Chance Morrow is a 6-foot-4 red-zone threat, and Dwayne
Early Jr. and ‘Tyrell Simmons Jr. turned heads during the spring.

On the offensive line, only one starter returns in guard Alex Heininger.
This is the area with the most questions on offense.

DEFENSE Buffalo’s secondary has two experienced corners returning in
Kobi Blackwell (seven starts) and Keontez Bradley (six starts), and nickel
SaVeon Brown (eight starts) is expected to be the biggest playmaker on
the backline. Miles Greer was in the safety rotation a year ago and was
third on the team in tackles. The Bulls ranked No. 23 in the nation in pass
defense (186.4 ypg) last season.

The defensive line will have to be rebuilt, with defensive tackle Cornell
Evans the lone returning starter. Will Clark had a nice spring at defensive
end after contributing on special teams last season, and transfer edges Lo-
gan Goodwin and Sedrick Vessah were all-league at their previous stops.

The linebacking corps lost All-American Red Murdock and will try to
piece it together with transfers and backups from last season. FCS Cor-
nell transfer Joey Cheshire impressed coaches early, and Virginia transfer
Stevie Bracey looks like he will play a big role. Redshirt freshman George

Hill also will challenge for a rotational spot.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Oliver Hautanen did not see any action as a true
freshman last season, and Ethan Stumpf has yet to punt. The Bulls also
brought in Andrew VanSlyke from James Madison to compete for the
punter spot.

FINAL ANALYSIS Head coach Pete Lembo is starting over in a lot of ways,
and a lot of the rebuild will depend on the quarterback. Wright will likely
have some growing pains, but he has potential. Ihe lines need to be better
than a year ago, and it is not clear that the Bulls upgraded there. Reaching
a second bowl in Lembo’s first three years will be a challenge.

===== PAGE 144 =====
WZ CENTRAL MICHIGAN CHIPPEWAS


===== Central Michigan =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 98 | MAC PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-3 MAC

OFFENSE Second-year head coach Matt Drinkall plans to use multiple
quarterbacks again this season. Angel Flores will primarily be used as a
running threat, and Jadyn Glasser is a traditional pocket passer. Flores
missed four games due to injury last season, but he had a team-high 107
rushing attempts for 527 yards and eight IDs. He threw just 28 passes,
completing 19. Glasser has played in six games over two years but will be
featured plenty after a strong spring.

Vaughn Blue arrives from Liberty after three up-and-down years and
will take over RB1 duties. Brock Townsend averaged just over six carries
per game last season and will be a factor.

Senior Langston Lewis, a third-team All-MAC honoree, gives Glasser
and Flores a reliable target as the only returning starting wideout. Sopho-
more Nasir Williams also had a strong spring.

The offensive line is deeper and more experienced. Left guard Jacob
Russell started every game last year and is an all-league candidate.

DEFENSE ‘Three starters return on a unit that ranked seventh in the MAC
in total defense. Quavion Bird is back at defensive tackle after taking over
the position halfway through the year. Korver Demma is another edge
with starting experience, and the coaching staff is excited about sopho-
more JJ Douglas, who moved early in his true freshman season from tight
end to defensive end and looks like he will break out in Year 2.

The linebacker room has to be completely rebuilt. Xavier White,
whose father played in the NFL and great uncle Charles White won the
Heisman Trophy in 1979, was in the rotation last season. Victor Earl,
another projected starter, saw limited action off the bench last season.

In the secondary, corner Jaion Jackson will be a third-year starter and
is the unquestioned leader of the back line. JaJuan Jones and Ty Williams
will battle for the spot opposite Jackson. Safeties will be inexperienced,
with Justin Taylor transferring in from Wyoming.

SPECIALISTS Declan Duley will be back at punter. He averaged 41.1 yards

per punt in his first year after transferring from Illinois. Caden VanHuis,
who played in the Under Armour All-America game as a senior in high

school, will take over the placekicking duties.

FINALANALYSIS Can CMU make it to back-to-back bowls? The Chippe-

was miss two of the four other MAC teams that reached the postseason
last year, but they also play national champion runner-up Miami (Fla.)
and New Mexico (9-4 last season) on the road in non-league play. The de-
fense has a lot of questions at all three levels, but the offense has potential
if Glasser can translate his spring performance at QB into fall production.

BROCK TOWNSEND

“(Head coach] Matt Drinkall is doing a great job
at that place. He's built a culture coming from
Army. He's got a good group of young coaches,
and guys who are getting their first shot at this
level. He's doing good for what he has. ... Yes,
that's a very hard school to recruit to. You're

in the middle of the state of Michigan. There's
literally nothing around that place. It's a place
that if you can win, when you've got the iron

EASTERN MICHIGAN EAGLES


===== Eastern Michigan =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 124 | MAC PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 MAC

OFFENSE Quarterback Noah Kim is back for his fifth year and second as
the starter for Eastern Michigan. He connected on 61% of his passes and

threw for 2,817 yards a year ago with 18 touchdowns and 11 intercep-
tions. He will enter 2026 as one of the MAC’s more experienced passers;
this season marks the first time since 2017 that EMU is returning its
starter at quarterback.

Three of Kim’s top four pass-catchers return as well, with second-team
All-MAC tight end Joshua Long (37 catches, 357 yards, three TDs) likely
to be a featured target in his junior season. Nick Devereaux was third-
team All-MAC after his 510-yard, seven-touchdown season, and Harold
Mack had a 179-yard game against Miami (Ohio) last year.

The primary running back will be graduate transfer Braydon Bennett
from Virginia Tech, who saw little action with the Hokies but rushed for
almost 2,000 yards in four years at Coastal Carolina.

Center Nicholas Gallegos leads an offensive line that has two starters
back.

DEFENSE ‘The backline of the Eagles’ defense will be deep and experienced.
Bryce Llewellyn was second-team All-MAC at safety and the team’s
defensive MVP after recording 102 tackles. Barry Manning is capable
of playing safety after being forced to start at linebacker for much of last
season due to injuries. Manning tied for the team lead in tackles for a loss
and was second in total tackles. Former Michigan State transfer Caleb
Coley will be a full-time starter at corner.

Senior Zach Mowchan, who has battled injuries the last two seasons,
will try to fortify the linebacking corps.

Veteran end Carter Evans leads the defensive line, and Jefferson Adam
projects to start after missing all of 2025 with a preseason injury. [he Ea-
gles will need to improve upon a rushing defense that allowed a league-

high 232.1 yards per game.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Rudy Kessinger returns after a season that saw
him connect on 17-of-23 field goals. He was 8-of-14 from beyond 40
yards. Daniel Hull, who signed with Missouri out of high school, may
take over the punting duties in his third year with the Eagles.

FINAL ANALYSIS Having Kim and his top receivers back is a big plus for

EMU. Head coach Chris Creighton likes to maintain offensive balance,
but the Eagles’ passing game should be a step ahead early in the season.
Defensively, EMU needs more from its front seven if it wants to contend
for a bowl. EMU must find three wins beyond MAC newcomer Sacra-
mento State, UMass and FCS Lindenwood to make the postseason.

===== PAGE 146 =====
KENT STATE GOLDEN FLASHES


===== Kent State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 126 | MAC PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 4-4 MAC

OFFENSE ‘The Golden Flashes were fortunate to hold on to quarterback Dru
DeShields after he turned in a strong 2025 season. DeShields threw 18

touchdowns with only three interceptions and completed 57% of his passes
in his first season. He is the top player on a unit that must improve on last
seasons 21.3 points per game, and DeShields gives it a chance to do that.

Tight end ‘Terik Mulder will figure heavily in the passing attack and is
an All-MAC candidate after catching 19 passes for 198 yards and four
touchdowns last season. Kick return specialist Da’Realyst Clark is expect-
ed to carry more of a load at wide receiver, and slot man Wayne Harris
took a big step forward in spring practice.

Spring ball did not produce a No. 1 tailback, as Cincinnati transfer
Jakorion Caftey, FCS Illinois State transfer Mason King, Brandon White
and Donovan Delaney Jr. will all compete in the fall. This position has the
most uncertainty heading into the season.

‘The offensive line has holes to fill, but some incoming transfers make
this unit deeper than last season.

DEFENSE Defensive tackle Thomas Aden is back after starting for the first
time since transferring from Pitt. Jaihien Roy and Antonio Bottiggi, two

players who are both in their third year in the program and have worked
their way to the top of the depth chart, will man the edges.

Rocco Nicholl returns to Kent State at linebacker, where he started in
2024 before spending a year at USF. He will be one of the leaders on a
unit that finished 125th in FBS in total defense last season.

Both starting safeties — Derrick Jackson HI and Joel Cordoba — are
back, and nickel DJ Barnes played 70% of the team’s snaps. [he corners

will be new, and there could be a learning curve there.

SPECIALISTS Clark took two kickoffs back for touchdowns last season and
was first-team All-MAC as a return specialist. Harris also had a touchdown
on a punt return and averaged a MAC-best 13.2 yards per punt return.
Australian Jake Stoeckel lost his punting job last season but has regained
the top spot heading into camp. There is a three-way battle for kicker.

FINALANALYSIS Kent State’s five-win season in 2025 might not seem like

much, but consider that the Flashes went 1-23 the previous two seasons
and saw their head coach placed on leave last March and fired on April
11. Mark Carney sorted through the chaos, found a quarterback and went
4-4 in MAC play after starting from scratch in his first season as head
coach. He needs to replace some starters lost to the portal, but because he
was able to hang on to his QB, expectations have risen, although a bowl
might be just out of reach this year.

MIAMI (OHIO) REDHAWKS


===== Miami (Ohio) =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 86 | MAC PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 7-7, 6-2 MAC

OFFENSE Sophomore Thomas Gotkowski is expected to be QB1 in the
fall after taking the reins late last season. He sparked a stagnant offense
in his first two games with four T’D passes, but both he and the offense
regressed significantly in losses to Western Michigan in the MAC Cham-
pionship Game and to Fresno State in the Arizona Bowl. Former Kansas
backup David McComb is the secondary option.

Rodney Nelson is expected to get the bulk of the carries at running

back for the RedHawks. He is transferring in from FCS Monmouth,
where he was the CAA Player of the Year after rushing for 1,802 yards

and 18 touchdowns. Wisconsin transfer Cade Yacamelli will be there in
a supporting role, and it will be interesting to see if there is a timeshare,
which the RedHawks typically employ.

Miami mostly uses four wideouts, and almost all the receivers will be
newcomers. [ransfers Quintin Simmons (Kentucky), Devin Hyatt (Ari-
zona) and Damarion Witten (Ohio State) bring Power 4 credibility.

Left tackle Eric Smith was third-team AIl-MAC a year ago, and fellow

third-team honoree Evan Malcore, a Northern Illinois transfer, joins him.

DEFENSE ‘The strongest unit on a defense that has been top five in the
MAC four years running will be the linebackers. CJ Young, a sec-
ond-team All-MAC performer at Kent State, is transferring in, and
fellow newcomer Blayne Myrick was a first-team All-Sun Belt honoree at
South Alabama. Both should contend for all-conference honors. Malcolm
McCain is the team’s leading returning tackler, and Brock Uihlein is also
a steady performer.

On the defensive line, rotational players will step in alongside returning
starter Roosevelt Andrews III, and Bai Jobe, Devin Bell and Grant Lyons
are among the edge rushers.

Miami lost its starting safeties, both of whom were among the best in

the conference, although Mychal Yharbrough impressed in the spring.

SPECIALISTS Junior Kellan McLaughlin has been the kickoff specialist
for three years, but with the departure of all-league placekicker Dom
Dzioban, McLaughlin will add field goals and extra points to his duties.
Punter Pierse Stainton averaged 42.3 yards per kick last season.

FINAL ANALYSIS Miami has been to three straight MAC title games, and

there is a good chance for a fourth if the higher-end transfers turn out to
be as good as advertised. Ihe offense should be improved as Gotkowski
gains more experience and Nelson emerges at running back, and head
coach Chuck Martin consistently fields a strong defense.

===== PAGE 148 =====
bax! OHIO BOBCATS


===== Ohio =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 111 | MAC PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 MAC

OFFENSE Offensive coordinator Scott Isphording is back, and that’s about
it. Ihe Bobcats lost nearly all of their offensive production to graduation
or the transfer portal. The biggest storyline is under center, where a mix
of inexperienced returners and highly productive FCS New Hampshire
transfer Matt Vezza will compete for the starting job.

The ground game says goodbye to 1,300-yard rusher Sieh Bangura, but
returnee Duncan Brune (585 yards, eight TDs) and UConn transfer Vic-
tor Rosa (302 total yards, three IDs) provide adequate reinforcements. At
receiver, Ohio benefits from experienced targets like Dom Dorwart, Max
Rodarte and Eian Pugh, who combined for 24 receptions and 280 yards.
Whoever wins the QB battle will have reliable options on key downs.

The offensive line is almost entirely rebuilt through the transfer portal.
It’s one of the most uncertain position groups entering 2026, with all five
starters having departed. Ohio's staff targeted size and experience through
the portal. The goal is to maintain the physical identity that has defined
the program's rushing attack in recent years.

DEFENSE Ohio’s defense will dictate its win-loss record. Linebackers Jack
Fries and Michael Molnar return to anchor the unit, along with defensive
linemen Austin Mitchell, Nehemiah Dukes and Derek Reagans. The
secondary has a blend of returnees and transfers that hopes to lock down
opposing quarterbacks and wide receivers.

Defensive coordinator Kurt Mattix was on the 2024 Ohio staft. Now,
he’s back to implement an aggressive, attacking style that prioritizes dis-
ruption at the line of scrimmage and increased pressure. Ihe philosophical
shift could pay dividends if the front seven adapts quickly.

While the talent level appears solid, execution in a new system often
takes time. If the defense can quickly adjust and create negative plays, it
has the potential to become one of the more impactful units in the MAC.

SPECIALISTS Magnus Haines was one of the top punters in the MAC last
season, averaging 42.3 yards per punt. Placekicker Will Hryszko trans-

ferred from Kent State, where he made all 31 extra points and went 8-of-
11 on field goals. Brune and Rodarte give the Bobcats big-play ability in

the return game.

FINALANALYSIS For the third consecutive season, Ohio is dealing with a

coaching change. That’s difficult enough, but in the transfer portal era, it’s
only exacerbated. The Bobcats are facing this season with a mostly brand-
new roster. Head coach John Hauser is at least a familiar face, as he spent
the previous three seasons on the staff. But can he sustain the momentum
that Ohio has built with 40 wins over the past four years?

(S$ y SACRAMENTO STATE HORNETS


===== Sacramento State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 136 | MAC PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 7-5, 5-3 FCS BIG SKY

OFFENSE The Hornets welcome back quarterback Carson Conklin after

one year at Fresno State. Conklin spent the first two seasons of his college
career with Sacramento State, earning Freshman All-America honors

at the FCS level and finishing third in voting for the Jerry Rice Award,
given to the FCS’ top freshman. He was a full-time starter in 2024 but led
the Hornets to only three wins. He did complete 62% of his passes with
28 touchdowns and 12 interceptions before leaving for Fresno State. With
the Bulldogs, Conklin moved into a starting role late in the year.

Conklin has some weapons in running backs Jamar Curtis and Curron
Borders. Curtis was an FCS All-American at Lafayette who transferred to
Sac State for 2025. He was injured most of last season, but he had more
than 3,500 total yards and 35 touchdowns over three years at Lafayette.

New head coach Alonzo Carter will be starting over at wide receiver
as the position hit the transfer portal almost as a unit. Matt Coleman has
significant experience coming over from San José State, and Onterrio
Smith Jr. has seen action in 20 games with the Hornets over two years.

‘The offensive line will also have to be rebuilt around Bryson Summers,
who saw extensive action last year, and Jeremiah Stallworth.

DEFENSE ‘The best unit for the Hornets clearly will be their linebackers.
Derek Houston and Alex Rocha are both back after a frustrating 2025

that saw only one quarter where both were healthy at the same time.
Houston and Rocha awere 100% in spring ball and will be critical in
the middle of a unit that will be adapting to a new 3-3-5 scheme under
defensive coordinator Adam Clark.

The defensive line was shifting players during the spring to see who
would be in the rotation. Wisconsin transfer defensive tackle Jamel How-
ard won a starting job in the spring.

The secondary will also be a complete overhaul, with Boogsie Silvera

arriving at safety after stops at FCS schools Charleston Southern and
McNeese and another at USF.

SPECIALISTS Grant Meadors returns as the Hornets’ kicker. The former
Oregon Duck hit 12 of his 15 field goals in his first year in Sacramento
with a long of 42. Ihe punter situation won't be finalized until the fall.

FINALANALYSIS Sacramento State will likely struggle in its first year in

the Mid-American Conference with a first-time head coach and a roster
that was built on the idea it would be competing as an FCS Independent.
Only after the transfer portal closed did Sacramento State receive an in-
vitation to the MAC. ‘There are questions along both lines, which is never
good when a team is stepping up a level.

9ffop TOLEDO ROCKETS


===== Toledo =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 99 | MAC PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 6-2 MAC

OFFENSE Amid all the turnover that followed head coach Jason Candle’s

departure to UConn, the quarterback position is something of a known
commodity. Junior John Alan Richter returns after serving as a backup the
past two seasons and has shown flashes in limited opportunities. He had
three starts as a redshirt freshman in 2024 and some backup duty in 2025,
and he has completed 66% of his passes with seven TDs and one INT.

Running back CJ Miller is one of 14 FCS Mercer transfers who
followed new head coach Mike Jacobs to ‘Toledo, and he should be the
top running back. Miller had 1,267 yards from scrimmage and scored 17
touchdowns for the Bears. Connor Walendzak led the Rockets in rushing
two years ago and should also be a factor.

‘The offensive line welcomes back four players with starting experience,
led by All-MAC candidate Stephen Gales at left tackle.

The wide receiver room is full of transfers, with Kalvin Gilbert Jr. (FCS
Southeast Missouri State), Anthony Simpson (Michigan) and Adjatay
Dabbs (Mercer) making early impressions. Bryson Hammer should also
be a factor after being primarily a punt returner thus far in his career.

DEFENSE ‘The most important player arriving from Mercer with Jacobs
is defensive end Andrew Zock, the FCS Defensive Player of the Year in

2025. Zock, who had 20 tackles for a loss and 11.5 sacks, decided to stay

with Jacobs despite Power 4 offers and will be the leader on the defense.
How the transfers adjust will be the story. Linebacker Kari Jackson
spent two seasons at Penn State and was making an early impression in
the spring. Linebackers Julian Fox and Isaac Prince earned All-Southern
Conference honors at Mercer. Safety Armorion Smith is spending his
final season at [Toledo after seeing seven starts at Michigan State. Corner
Cam Jones was an All-Patriot League player at FCS Holy Cross, and
former Div. III nickel Mo Conteh was turning heads in the spring as well.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Reice Griffith was 14-for-17 on field goals last
season at Mercer, with half his makes coming from beyond 40 yards. ‘The

punter position is up for grabs between returner Emilio Duran and two
newcomers. Hammer will be the primary punt returner after leading the
MAC in punt return yardage the past two seasons.

FINALANALYSIS Jacobs has a track record of success, most recently going
15-1 in Southern Conference play at Mercer the past two seasons. Toledo
has not had a losing record in 16 years, and the new staff is confident that
this won't be a slow build despite essentially starting with a brand-new
roster. How well the Mercer transfer contingent adjusts will determine
whether the Rockets can continue to be an upper-level MAC program.

UMASS MINUTEMEN


===== UMass =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 138 | MAC PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 0-12, 0-8 MAC

OFFENSE ‘There is excitement surrounding the arrival of quarterback Wil-
liams “Pop” Watson III, who was a local star in Springfield before playing
for three years at Virginia Tech. Watson could never win the permanent
QB1 job in Blacksburg, but he showed flashes and has taken control of a
dormant UMass offense that finished last in the nation in scoring average
(11.1 ppg) and yards per game (247.5) last season.

Wide receiver T-Y. Harding is back after missing the final nine games
last season and should be Watson's top target. Another receiver to watch
will be Watson’s high school teammate, Joseph Griffin Jr., who comes
back to UMass after two years at Boston College and one at Wisconsin.
New offensive coordinator Max Warner, who coached tight end Harold
Fannin Jr. to All-America status at Bowling Green, will feature tight ends
Max Dowling and Reece Adkins.

FCS Murray State transfer Jordan Washington will be the top back
behind a revamped offensive line that needed that revamping. Left guard
Brock Taylor returns after starting four games last season, with left tackle
Greg Knox (FCS McNeese) joining as a transfer and right tackle Zachary

Franks coming back from an injury that cost him the season.

DEFENSE ‘The Minutemen welcome two critical players back from injury.
Defensive end Joshua Nobles, who might have been the team’s top player,
missed all of last season. Cornerback TJ Magee will bolster the secondary
and also return punts after he missed the final nine games last season.

‘The linebackers are productive and experienced. Tyler Martin is an
All-MAC candidate after recording 104 tackles with an interception and
fumble recovery last season. Timmy Hinspeter averaged 9.9 tackles per
game in his seven games before an injury ended his campaign.

Both starting safeties — Zeraun Daniel and Kendall Bournes — are
back. Transfer Isaiah Reed turned heads in the spring and will pair with
Magee at cornerback.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Derek Morris was 10-of-16 on field goals, including
3-of-5 from 50-plus. Australian Edward Phillipson takes over at punter

with Magee and Harding giving UMass a threat in the return game.

FINAL ANALYSIS Several key injuries decimated a thin roster last season and
kept UMass from being competitive. If the Minutemen can stay healthier
and Watson lives up to expectations at quarterback, UMass will be capa-
ble of competing most weeks in the MAC. Its 16-game losing streak en-
tering the season is the nation’s longest, and the average margin of defeat
during the streak is 26.8 points. The streak should end in September with
FCS Sacred Heart and Stonehill as well as FBS newcomer Sacramento

State on the schedule.

===== PAGE 156 =====
AIR FORCE FALCONS


===== Air Force =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 80 | MWC PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 MWC

TM

OFFENSE Air Force found its quarterback of the future last season, and
the Falcons should look forward to two more years of Liam Szarka. ‘The
junior saw action in 10 games last season before breaking his wrist. He
led the Mountain West Conference with 13 rushing touchdowns, was
second in the league with 92.2 rushing yards per game and threw nine
touchdowns to five interceptions. In each of his first three career starts,
he threw for 200-plus yards and rushed 110-plus yards in each, the best
three-game start for an Air Force QB in program history.

AFA’s second-leading rusher also returns in Owen Allen. ‘The fullback
produced five 100-yard games last year.

The offensive line lost two AllI-MWC players, but two full-time starters
are back, along with senior Luke Vonderhaar, who has extensive experi-
ence. Expect the Falcons to reload along the line.

The biggest question heading into the fall is the receiver unit, with

Jonah Dawson the leading returner with 12 receptions, but AFA runs the
ball 80% of the time.

DEFENSE Air Force fell to 10th in the league in total defense last season
and allowed 30.3 points per game, as young players took their lumps.

The unit should be markedly improved this season with almost the
entire back eight intact after a season of growing pains. Inside linebacker
Blake Fletcher was second-team All-MWC last season and should com-
pete for first-team honors after leading the team with 108 tackles. Dallas
Daley and Isaac Hubert will be alongside Fletcher in what is expected to
be a very solid linebacking corps.

Safety Roger Jones Jr. was an honorable mention All-MWC selection
last season and headlines a backline with a ton of returning experience.
Corners Mikhail Seiken and Korey Johnson are tested, and safety Max
Mustell picked off three passes as a true freshman.

Graduation wiped out the defensive line, and Air Force does not take

transfers, so the Falcons will be inexperienced up front.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Jacob Medina was first-string last season coming
out of camp, but he lost his job after missing half of his six field-goal
attempts. He had a solid spring and is once again the No. 1 kicker. Will

Duncan will be the new punter.

FINALANALYSIS After two straight losing seasons, the arrow is pointing up
for head coach Troy Calhoun in his 20th season. A veteran quarterback, an

experienced defense and a conference that has bid farewell to Boise State
and San Diego State, among others, give Air Force the chance to get back
over .500 and compete for a top-three spot in the Mountain West.

SCOUTING THE FALCONS

“Honestly, it’s really cool that they don't do

the transfer portal. They're not gonna make
mistakes, and when you make a mistake, they're
gonna make you pay for it. If you turn the ball
over against them, it'll kill ya because they're a
possession-based team. ... Alot of kids these
days who are signing out of high school to go

to a Mountain West school, they're happy to go
there, but in two or three years, give them truth
serum and they d like to be at a Power 4 school.
A lot of [Air Force recruits] are thinking, ‘| want
to serve. They recruit a specific type of kid.
Now, there's enough evidence that you can still,
if you end up being a heck of a ballplayer, reach
your NFL dreams and stay there.”

— An opposing Mountain West assistant coach

HAWAI'! RAINBOW WARRIORS


===== Hawai'i =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 77 | MWC PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 5-3 MWC

OFFENSE It all starts with quarterback Micah Alejado, who is coming off a
season in which he earned Mountain West Freshman of the Year honors.
He averaged 282.4 passing yards per game and completed 66% of his
passes in head coach Timmy Chang’s four-wide, spread attack. Alejado
had 24 touchdown passes and nine interceptions and also rushed for a
score. His quick decision-making and understanding of where to go with
the ball were critical for an offense that was eighth nationally in passing.
‘The two slot receivers look strong once again. Pofele Ashlock is a
fourth-year starter who has averaged 73 catches and 763 yards in his first
three seasons. Joining him is Washington transfer Audric Harris, who had
limited production with the Huskies but looks ready to break out in this

offense. ‘The outside receivers are lesser-known quantities.

===== PAGE 158 =====
=. NEVADA WOLF PACK


===== Nevada =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 113 | MWC PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 2-6 MWC

OFFENSE ‘There is plenty of room for improvement for the Nevada offense,
and that starts at quarterback. Wolf Pack QBs, including this year’s pro-
jected starter, Carter Jones, threw 10 TDs and 17 INTs last season, which
was the worst ratio in the country. Jones was a true freshman forced into
action earlier than the staff would have liked, but he is making strides as
he tries to hold off UCLA transfer Luke Duncan for the No. 1 job in new
offensive coordinator Brett Bartolone’s system.

Herschel Turner figures to earn the No. 1 running back job after
averaging 5.1 yards per carry last season. Dominic Kelley flashed as a
freshman power back.

Nevada lost its top five receivers from last season, so transfers will play
key roles. The Wolf Pack will lean heavily on senior Damien Morgan,
who was productive at FCS Idaho State. Gary Givens [I] comes in from
Northern Illinois after limited production. Marshaun Brown is the lead-
ing returner with 16 catches last season.

The offensive line returns two starters and brought in some impact
transfers, so this group could set a foundation for the offense.

DEFENSE Nevada has a chance to be solid on defense thanks to the return
of a potential All-Mountain West player at each level. Defensive end
Dylan LaBarbera will be a candidate for MWC Defensive Player of the
Year and looks to have an NFL future after recording a league-best 17
tackles for a loss. Defensive tackle Logologo Va’a also returns.

At linebacker, EJ Smith missed the second half of last season due to in-
jury but had become one of the team’s leaders and should have a big year.
Colorado State transfer Jeremy Naborne-Owens saw significant action
with the Rams last season.

In the secondary, cornerback Aiden Walker emerged during his true
freshman season and projects as the team’s top player along the back line.
At the other corner, Bryson Snelling has starting experience, and starting
safety Bishop Turner also returns.

SPECIALISTS Senior placekicker Joe McFadden was 19-for-25 on field

goals last season, with a long of 50 yards. ‘There will be a new Australian
punter for the Wolf Pack as Ewan Baulch and Jacob Fernandez are com-
ing to the U.S. to vie for the job.

FINALANALYSIS Nevada's four-year bowl drought is the program’s longest
since 1997-2004, and it may be an uphill climb to end it this season.
‘There are wins to be found on the schedule, but to get to six, a jump
forward will be needed in the passing game with either Jones or Duncan
at the controls.

NEW MEXICO LOBOS


===== New Mexico =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 61 | MWC PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 MWC

OFFENSE Starting quarterback Jack Layne is back after completing 65% of
his passes and averaging 191 yards per game with 13 TDs and 10 inter-
ceptions. Layne was a limited participant in spring drills, and he may have
competition in the fall from Oregon transfer Luke Moga. The former
4-star prospect left the Ducks when Dante Moore returned to school.

A pair of transfers could be the team’s top two pass-catchers. Troy
Omeire, who has played at Texas, Arizona State and UNLV, had 31

catches for 514 yards and five TDs for the Rebels last year. Miles Wil-

===== PAGE 160 =====
NOUR, NORTH DAKOTA STATE BISON


===== North Dakota State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 78 | MWC PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 12-1, 8-0 MVFC

OFFENSE Senior quarterback Nathan Hayes will take the reins after being
QB2 on last season's 12-1 team that lost a heartbreaker in the second
round of the FCS playofts. Hayes saw limited action in 10 games last sea-
son, but he will now be responsible for directing the offense as it moves
up a level to the FBS Mountain West Conference. Hayes is a running
threat and has good speed and a strong arm at 6-foot-3, 218 pounds.

‘Three starters are back along the offensive line, and Kansas transfer Kene
Anene looks like an immediate fit at right tackle. Head coach Tim Polasek,
who is entering his third season, should be able to rely on this group.

The skill positions have some unknowns. Running back DJ Scott aver-
aged 7.3 carries per game last year and finished the year with 502 yards,
and he will likely try to double those numbers as the lead back for 2026.
Redshirt freshman Myles Mitchell will have a larger role as well, and
incoming freshman Braylon Toliver could earn serious playing time.

Tight end Reis Kessel returns after averaging an impressive 22.3 yards
per catch last season. The leading wideout is Jackson Williams, who start-
ed two games and had 293 receiving yards for the season. Young players
will need to step up.

DEFENSE ‘The Bison lost defensive captain and linebacker Nathaniel Stae-
hling on the final day of the portal period to Michigan, weakening what
looked like a stellar linebacking corps. All-conference junior Donovan
Woolen is back, but there will be inexperience around him. Freshman
Gavin Sell will get a long look.

Starting safeties Darius Givance and Taylen Eady have solid depth
behind them. At cornerback, there are questions. [wo Division II transfers
arrive to help replace both starters who transferred, and there are four play-
ers at the position who were depth pieces a year ago.

On the line, all-conference performer Keenan Wilson leads the group
from his defensive tackle spot.

SPECIALISTS Drew Klein takes over the placekicking duties after making

his only attempt from 46 yards a year ago. Punter Aaron Bickerton is back
after averaging 45.4 yards per boot in 2025.

FINALANALYSIS It will not be a surprise to see North Dakota State elevate

and make the adjustment to the Mountain West given its long history
of success. Ihe respective lines are usually a concern when you step up
a level, but NDSU should be fine there. If some wide receivers develop

for Hayes, and some defensive backs emerge, the Bison could be a fringe

contender in the MWC in Year 1.

NORTHERN ILLINOIS HUSKIES


===== Northern Illinois =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 134 | MWC PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 2-6 MAC

OFFENSE ‘The Huskies have a choice to make at quarterback that will
influence their offensive philosophy, which needs an overhaul after NIU
finished 132nd nationally in total offense in 2025. Sophomore Brady Da-
vidson had two starts; senior Jalen Macon closed last season as the starter;
and transfer Kan Hamric arrives from Div. II University of Charleston.

Davidson is a traditional pocket passer who averaged 27 passes in his
two starts as a true freshman. Macon is a run-first athlete who is explosive
with the ball in his hands but did not get the opportunity to throw many
passes. And Hamric is a dual-threat who averaged 155 passing yards and
61 rushing yards per game while leading his team to a league title.

The top running back will be Telly Johnson Jr. He averaged 5.7 yards
per carry last year, ranking second in the MAC. Included in that was an
80-yard run against Eastern Michigan.

DeAree Rogers (46 receptions, 506 yards) is the No. 1 target in the
passing game. Rickey Taylor Jr. is another weapon who missed half of last
season due to injury, but he averaged a team-best 14.9 yards per reception.

The offensive line will be young, with center Landon Hron and left
tackle Ross Liegel the only experienced starters.

DEFENSE ‘The secondary enters the season as the best position group on
the team. Justin Harris and Tyrese Stanley are a couple of junior-college
transfers who should comprise a solid safety tandem. Dev’ion Reynolds
will man one corner, and transfer Yassine Falke from NAIA Marian was
all-conference and is expected to make an impact.

In NIU’s 4-2-5 scheme, Broderick Windham is moving from safety to
linebacker, and Cooper Mau will try to hold off Kimar Nelson, who came
on strong toward the end of his true freshman season and should be a key
factor in a defense that finished eighth in the MAC last season.

‘The defensive line will be counting on Amari Washington and Jacob
McGhee, a transfer from Div. II Lincoln (Mo.) University, to step up.
Washington, a defensive tackle, made significant progress last year and has
coaches excited.

SPECIALISTS Jake Ference is back after sharing punting duties last year and
averaging 43.7 yards per kick. Rogers and Reynolds should again handle

punt return and kick return duties, respectively.

FINAL ANALYSIS Northern Illinois, which limped through a 3-9 season last

year, is moving to the Mountain West Conference and will go through
the year with interim head coach Rob Harley after Thomas Hammock
left in FKebruary to join the Seattle Seahawks staff. With questions at
quarterback and youth along both lines, it projects to be a difficult season
for the Huskies in their new conference.

===== PAGE 162 =====
Ry VP»

| SAN JOSE STATE SPARTANS


===== San Jose State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 116 | MWC PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 2-6 MWC

OFFENSE The Spartans were second in the Mountain West in passing
offense a year ago but were undone by 17 interceptions. Whoever wins the
QB battle between last year’s third-stringer Robert McDaniel, Hawai'i
transfer Luke Weaver and freshman Daniel Rolovich will need to take
better care of the football. McDaniel was the leader during spring practice.

The wide receiver room, which has produced the nation’s leader in re-
ceiving yardage (Danny Scudero in 2025 and Nick Nash in 2024) in both
of head coach Ken Niumatalolo’s first two seasons, will be starting from
scratch. Malachi Riley, who was slated to start last season before being
lost for the season in Week 1 to a lower-body injury, should be the No. 1
pass-catcher. Penn State transfer Anthony Ivey will also be one to watch.

At running back, Jabari Bates returns and will get the bulk of the car-
ries after averaging a team-best 7.1 yards per rush and just under 50 yards
per game as a reserve.

The offensive line allowed a league-low 13 sacks a year ago, but only
two starters return. Senior center Joseph Harbert could be in line for all-
league honors if he continues to develop.

DEFENSE ‘The Spartans are rebuilding on defense. Ethan Powell is back at
outside linebacker, and Zane Carter, who was slated to start but missed
2025 due to injury, should be the starter at the other outside backer. On
the inside, FCS Weber State transfer Aizik Mahuka made a big impres-
sion on the coaching staff early in spring.

The defensive line will look to Justin Stearns to lead the way at end. He
has six career starts. Nate Lewis is an athletic end who showed signs of
development in the spring.

SJSU’s allowed the MWC’s second-highest passer rating and tied for a
league-low seven interceptions. Corner Greco Carrillo would have started
but missed 2025 due to injury. Kejuan Bullard Jr. has also worked his way
to the top of the depth chart, and FCS Cal Poly transfer safety Brian
Dukes Jr. and converted wideout Tuli Tagovailoa-Amosa will be working
with new pass game coordinator/cornerbacks coach Brian Norwood.

SPECIALISTS Punter Trent Carrizosa is an all-league candidate after aver-
aging 45.6 yards per kick last season. The placekicking duties will come
down to Mathias Brown, who was 4-for-7 on field goals last year, and
FCS Idaho State transfer Trajan Sinatra.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The Spartans are starting over after a surprising 3-9 season

snapped a three-year run of bowl games. There is a wide variance between

SJSU’s floor and ceiling. There is a recent track record of an elite pass
offense, but the defense is full of questions.

UNLV REBELS


===== UNLV =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 59 | MWC PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 10-4, 6-2 MWC

®

OFFENSE Auburn transfer quarterback Jackson Arnold is hoping that he
experiences what Anthony Colandrea did in his one year at UNLV. Col-
andrea was the Mountain West Offensive Player of the Year in head coach
Dan Mullen’s first season, and Arnold will try to pick up where Colandrea
left off. Arnold has starting experience at Oklahoma and Auburn, but he
ultimately lost his job at both schools. If he loses his job at UNLV, former
Michigan transfer Alex Orji is the backup as a run-first option.

Jai’ Den “Jet” Thomas will be a fourth-year starter after rushing for
1,036 yards and 12 TDs last season. He is the face of the program and
will be a candidate for MWC Offensive Player of the Year.

‘The wide receiver room has no starters returning, but the coaches are
excited about sophomore Kayden McGee. He made big plays on special
teams last season, scoring the winning I'D against Utah State, and he is
the team’s fastest player. Taeshaun Lyons was productive last season in a
reserve role.

All-conference lineman Austin Boyd is moving from tackle to center,
and starting left tackle Will Thomas also returns.

DEFENSE One starter returns at each level for a defense that ranked 89th
in the nation in scoring. Blesyng Alualu-Iuiolemotu emerged late last
season at linebacker and will be a cornerstone. Linebacker transfer Cam

Santee was the CAA Defensive Player of the Year at FCS Holy Cross last

year and will be in the rotation with starter Kamuela Ka aihue.

Up front, junior Lucas Conti returns for his fourth season and will
anchor the line. Andre Porter and Landen Thomas will move into more
prominent roles on the ends.

In the secondary, Jake Pope spent two years at Alabama and one at
Georgia before starting all 14 games last year for UNLV. He will be the
leader of a much-improved backline. Kyron Chambers arrives from SMU
and ‘Tony Louis-Nkuba from Arizona State to bolster the unit.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Ramon Villela hit 15-of-20 field-goal attempts and
was 4-for-7 beyond 40 yards. He had a game-winner against Miami
(Ohio) and forced OT vs. Utah State with another made field goal late.
Australian Matt Choules will handle the punting chores.

FINAL ANALYSIS Five of UNLV’s 11 losses since 2023 have come to Boise
State, including three in the Mountain West Championship Game. That
obstacle is gone with the Broncos’ departure to the Pac-12. Can the Reb-
els win the conference? [hat will be on the shoulders of Arnold as well as
the defense, which surrendered at least 38 points five times last year.

JrrENSE UTEP is bringing in EJ Colson, the latest touted FCS Incarnate

threw for 2,142 yards and 16 touchdowns in 10 games last season. UW
went 3-6 in Colson’s nine starts. He averaged almost 12 carries per game
as a starter and ran for three touchdowns, so the Miners will look to tap
into his dual-threat ability.

Missouri transfer running back ‘Tavorus Jones saw limited action with
the Tigers for three years, but he showed the ability not only to run be-
tween the tackles but also to catch passes out of the backfield. He should
be RB1 with help behind him from Buffalo transfer Lamar Sperling and
sophomore Elijah McCoy.

Wide receiver Jaylan Brown redshirted last season at Michigan State
and will be looked to as a primary downfield target. FCS Northern Colo-
rado transfer Carver Cheeks turned some heads in the spring as well.

‘The offensive line will start four transfers along with right tackle Juan
Camacho Jr., a native of El Paso.

WYOMING COWBOYS


===== Wyoming =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 110 | MWC PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-6 MWC

OFFENSE If there is reason for optimism for a Cowboys offense that aver-
aged only 16 points per game last year, it starts at quarterback. Wyoming
welcomes l'yler Hughes from FCS William & Mary, where he threw
for 2,330 yards and 20 touchdowns and ran for 670 yards and 11 scores.
Hughes worked with new offensive coordinator Christian Taylor at
W&M before ‘Taylor left for the NFL. The two are reunited in Laramie.

Running back Samuel “Tote” Harris is back after leading the team in
rushing as a true freshman with 558 yards and 5.6 yards per carry. FCS
Western Illinois transfer Markell Holman, who led the Ohio Valley Con-
ference in rushing last season with 1,063 yards, will spell him.

Deion DeBlanc is a slot receiver who is also a dynamic return specialist
and will be the leader of a young group. Eric Richardson saw some spot
duty, while Jackson Holman battled injuries.

Wyoming traditionally has featured the tight end, and there are two
good ones on hand in Jake Wilson and Kyle Frendt.

The offensive line returns both starting tackles, and the coaches are
excited about Nebraska transfer center Jason Maciejezak.

DEFENSE Ethan Stuhlsatz takes over at middle linebacker and had a very
strong spring both physically and in his leadership role of lining up the

defense. Wyoming will count on him to lead a unit that finished fifth in
the Mountain West Conference in total defense. Gary Rutherford will
join him as the linebacker group turns over. USC transfer Anthony Bea-
vers is also one to watch.

In the secondary, safety Jones Thomas is the leading returning tackler
and one of two returning starters on the backline. He had two intercep-
tions and four pass breakups a year ago and will be a candidate for all-
league honors. Nickel Desman Hearns is the other returning starter.

Up front, defensive tackle Dante Drake returns after missing all of
2025 with an Achilles injury. He should be the top player on the line,

along with veteran Jayden Williams.

SPECIALISTS Both punter Bart Edmiston and placekicker Erik Sandvik

return, with Edmiston a candidate for all-league honors after he averaged
46.1 yards per kick a year ago. DeBlanc averaged 16.7 yards per punt

return and took one back for a touchdown.

FINAL ANALYSIS ‘The necessary offensive turnaround will depend on
Hughes at quarterback, and the arrow was pointing up in spring ball. The
defense will have a lot of new faces, but it looks strong up the middle at
all three levels and will have a chance to keep Wyoming in games. Head
coach Jay Sawvel’s first bowl appearance in his third season is a possibility

in a weakened MWC.

===== PAGE 168 =====
BOISE STATE BRONCOS

L


===== Boise State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 24 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 9-5, 6-2 MWC

OFFENSE Redshirt senior quarterback Maddux Madsen is heading into his

third season as Boise State’s starter, and he’ll be working with a relatively
fresh-faced receiving corps and offensive line.

The Broncos lost their top four pass-catchers from 2025. However,
junior wide receiver Cameron Bates offers both a vertical threat and speed
on end-around plays and is expected to step up in 2026. He’ll be assisted
by 6-foot-5 tight end Matt Wagner, who has proven to have safe hands
over the past two years, recording 34 catches for 359 yards in 27 games.

Boise State returns its two leading running backs in junior Dylan Riley

and redshirt sophomore Sire Gaines. The pair combined for 1,977 rushing
yards in 2025 and should split the carries in 2026.

DEFENSE ‘The Broncos lost nearly their entire secondary from last season,
but former Notre Dame transfer Jaden Mickey is expected to lead the
defense at the cornerback and nickel spots in his second year with the
program.

The Broncos are also in search of a new linebacker group. Redshirt
junior Boen Phelps offers great speed and coverage at the position, but
tackling could remain an issue — missed tackles at both the first and
second levels proved costly in 2025.

Sack production dropped from 55 in 2024 to 27 in 2025, but that stat
will likely jump back up in 2026. The Broncos return upperclassman edge
rushers Max Stege and Jayden Virgin-Morgan, the latter of whom led

Boise State last season in tackles for a loss (13) and sacks (six).

SPECIALISTS Special teams were a sore point for Boise State last year;
however, the Broncos will have consistency with the return of place-
kicker Colton Boomer and punter Oscar Doyle. Boomer hit all three of
his attempts from 50-plus yards and has been bullish about his accuracy
improvements. Ihe main issue in 2025 was a lack of explosiveness in the
return game. Speedy receivers Bates, Quinton Brown and Terrious Favors
are among the favorites to handle punt and kickoff returns.

FINALANALYSIS Boise State enters the reborn Pac-12 as the rightful favor-

ite, but the question is, how far can the Broncos go? Madsen has proven
his capabilities against Group of 6 opponents, but he has gone 0-4 against
Power 4 opponents. Breaking that streak against either Oregon in Week 1
or in the postseason would go a long way for the Broncos.

If Boise State can consistently get its run game going to help open the
pass game for an experienced QB, an inaugural Pac-12 title and a shot at

the College Football Playoff are certainly on the table in 2026.

FRESNO STATE BULLDOGS


===== Fresno State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 68 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 5-3 MWC

xe
—

fe
7 ct

OFFENSE ‘The Bulldogs lack experience at the quarterback position — the
five players who took snaps in spring ball have played a total of 82 snaps
at the FBS level. Khristian Martin and Jayden Mandal were the top two
contenders, and both add a run-game element that head coach Matt Entz
says the Bulldogs will utilize this season. Both also have the arm strength
to push the football down the field, something the Bulldogs did very
rarely last season. Martin, a transfer from Maryland, appears to be the
front-runner to win the job.

The Bulldogs return their top three running backs in Rayshon Luke,

Bryson Donelson and Brandon Ramirez as well as their top three wide-

outs in Josiah Freeman, Ezekiel Avit and Jahlil McClain.

DEFENSE Fresno State was able to retain defensive coordinator Nick Bene-
detto and his staff after a stellar first season in the San Joaquin Valley.
That’s a big plus for the Bulldogs, who allowed only 285.7 yards per game,
ranking first in the Mountain West Conference and 10th in the nation.
They also were second in the nation in passing defense (153.8 ypg),

tied for fourth in fewest plays of 20 or more yards allowed (36), fifth in
interceptions (20) and 16th in scoring defense (18.8 ppg). They did that
without a single player on the all-conference first team and only two on
the second team.

The Bulldogs have potential standouts returning, including nickel
Simeon Harris and linebackers K’vion Thunderbird and Tytus Khajavi,
and this unit should continue to play at a high level. Similar numbers this
season should result in more all-conference recognition.

SPECIALISTS Fresno State has one of the best special teams coaches in the
country in John Baxter. Last season, the Bulldogs led the Mountain West
in opponent kickoff and punt returns, allowing just 16.1 and 3.0 yards per
return, respectively. Punter Nick Verdugo returns, but the Bulldogs have
to replace kicker Dylan Lynch.

FINALANALYSIS Fresno State will be an improved team in Entz’s second
season, but will those gains outpace a schedule that will be more difficult
than a year ago? The Bulldogs went 9-4 in 2025 and beat Miami (Ohio)
in the Snoop Dogg Arizona Bowl, but their win total included victories
over FCS Southern, Oregon State, Nevada, Wyoming and San José State,
teams that went a combined 14-46.

To meet the challenge of a tougher schedule, expect a more diverse and
dynamic offense that should have the Bulldogs in conference title conten-
tion and in a bowl for a sixth year in a row.
