OREGON STATE BEAVERS


===== Oregon State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 101 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 2-10

UFFENSE For the first time since 2019, Oregon State has the chance to
start the same Week 1 quarterback in back-to-back years. Former 4-star
recruit Maalik Murphy has to win the job first, though. First-year head
coach JaMarcus Shephard says that Murphy, along with transfers Braden
Atkinson (FCS Mercer) and Brady Jones (Western Michigan), are in

an open competition for QB1. Shephard said this spring that he didn't
intend to name a starter until mid-August, at the earliest.

Whichever of the three wins the job has a bevy of young, unproven
weapons around him. Redshirt sophomore running back Cornell Hatcher
Jr. looked impressive in limited reps last fall and is the Beavers’ leading
returner in the backfield. Wide receiver Xayvion Noland had a breakout
year at FCS upstart UT Rio Grande Valley last season and looks ready to
be a factor in the passing game.

Tackle transfers Teko Shoats and Kwan Johnson played lots of football
in 2025 at the FCS level and look to anchor the Beavers’ offensive line
this fall. Tyler Voltin missed the spring but is a key piece at guard.

DEFENSE Aiden Sullivan, the Beavers’ No. 2 tackler last season, and

transfer Dylan Layne from FCS Idaho make for an intriguing tandem at
linebacker. Cornerback TJ Crandall made noise in the spring after miss-

ing 2025 with an injury and looks like a cornerstone of defensive coordi-
nator Mike McIntyre’s unit. Cornerback ‘Trey Glasper played as much as

anyone during an up-and-down freshman season and, like Sullivan, was a
key piece to retain for 2026.

Takari Hickle battled injuries throughout his career until last season
and, according to Shephard, was “unblockable” in the spring. Only time
will tell whether it’s a testament to Hickle’s growth or a condemnation of
Oregon State’s offensive line.

SPECIALISTS Oregon State endured one of the worst (if not the worst)
special teams seasons of all time in 2025 after numerous long-snapper in-

juries. Placekicker Caleb Ojeda and punter AJ Winsor are back for 2026
with a pair of new snappers, Jonathan Zarut and Tug Sanford, brought in.

FINALANALYSIS It’s hard to perform much worse than Oregon State’s’
2-10 season a year ago, but Year 1 in the new-look Pac-12 will challenge

Shephard’s first Beavers team. Physically, the team looked small in the
spring, on the defensive line in particular, and had numerous pieces absent
with lingering injury issues. With non-conference opponents Houston and
Texas Tech leading off the schedule, September may be unkind to Oregon
State. Ihe fan base is hungry for the team to get back to where it was in
2023 when it won eight games, but bowl eligibility would be a win in itself.

===== PAGE 172 =====
SAN DIEGO STATE AZTECS


===== San Diego State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 67 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 9-4, 6-2 MWC

D-

OFFENSE Senior Jayden Denegal (143-for-243, 1,807 yards, nine TDs,
eight INIs) gives the Aztecs something they haven't often had of late
— a returning starting QB. Even better, Denegal is healthy after being
bothered by shoulder issues that limited his practice time and required
postseason surgery on his left (non-throwing) shoulder.

Senior Lucky Sutton (1,297 yards, 10 TDs) is among the nation’s top
returning running backs. Colorado State transfer RB Javion Kinnard pro-
vides a boost as a receiving threat out of the backfield. Senior backup QB
Bert Emanuel Jr. moves to wide receiver to take advantage of his unique
skill set. It was evident when he started in the New Mexico Bowl, rushing
for 170 yards and two TDs in a quarter and a half. Starting wideouts
Jordan Napier and Jacob Bostick both are coming off reconstructive knee
surgery, but there’s more depth at the position than SDSU has had in
more than a decade.

DEFENSE ‘The Aztecs will be hard-pressed to match last year’s unit, which
ranked sixth nationally in scoring defense (15.4 ppg) and 11th in total
defense (293.7 ypg), after losing their top three defenders — edge Trey

White to Texas Tech, linebacker Owen Chambliss to Nebraska and
cornerback Chris Johnson to the NFL. ‘Transfer Nate Henrich (Div. II

Gannon University) is among the candidates they're counting on at edge.

Senior [ano Letuli is a standout linebacker, but he’s coming off recon-
structive knee surgery. Senior transfer Sione Hala (FCS Weber State)
could be another productive ’backer. Senior safety Dalesean Staley is the
only returning starter in the secondary.

SPECIALISTS The Aztecs’ kicking and punting has been a strong suit for
the better part of a decade. But there’s uncertainty following the gradua-
tion of Gabriel Plascencia, the most accurate placekicker in school history,
and the transfer of punter Hunter Green, who left for Washington. Soph-
omore Nick Clegg assumes the kicking duties, and senior Tashi Dorje,
who averaged 50 yards on three punts, will handle the punting chores.
‘The return game should be solid, especially with Napier and Kinnard,
who both were All-Mountain West punt returners last season.

FINALANALYSIS SDSU moves into the reconstituted Pac-12 on the heels

of a massive turnaround in 2025, improving from 3-9 to 9-4. The Aztecs
gained more than they lost in the transfer portal, so it’s reasonable to
believe they could approach nine wins once again — assuming that the

offense, which ranked 78th nationally in scoring (26.4 ppg), is more pro-

ductive with an experienced, healthy QB and more weapons around him.

TEXAS STATE BOBCATS


===== Texas State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 93 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 3-5 SUN BELT

OFFENSE Head coach GJ Kinne has lived up to his promise to “light up
the scoreboard” since he arrived at Texas State in 2022. Ihe Bobcats have
led the Sun Belt Conference in total yards and finished at least second in
scoring in each of the past three seasons.

As Texas State joins the revived Pac-12 Conference, Kinne will finally
have the same starter behind center for consecutive seasons. Sophomore
Brad Jackson returns after throwing for 3,224 yards and 21 touchdowns
and rushing for 744 yards and 17 scores.

The Bobcats were the only FBS program with two 1,000-yard receivers
in 2025 — Beau Sparks (1,200) and Chris Dawn Jr. (1,007) — and both
are returning as seniors. With their most impactful contributors returning,
the Bobcats will be in the end zone often.

DEFENSE As exciting as Texas State’s offense has been under Kinne, the
defense has been a point of aggravation. Late-game mistakes and an
inability to hold on to leads led to offseason changes.

South Alabama defensive coordinator Will Windham, who promises
to bring multiple looks and a more disciplined attitude, replaces two-year
defensive coordinator Dexter McCoil.

‘The retention rate isn’t as high on defense as it is on offense, but
Windham will have the services of three of the Bobcats’ top six tacklers
from 2025 — senior safety Ryan Nolan (79) and senior linebackers Ayden
Jones (56) and ‘Terrence Cooks II (36).

SPECIALISTS ‘Texas State’s past two kickers transferred to power programs
following strong seasons — Mason Shipley to Texas after 2024 and Tyler
Robles to Washington last season. Toledo transfer Dylan Cunanan hopes
to replicate their success after making 23-of-32 (72%) field-goal attempts
the past two seasons. Australian freshman Thomas Flintoff takes over the
punting duties. Jaylen Jenkins returns for his senior season after leading

the Sun Belt in yards per kickoff return (27.0) in 2025, including a 100-
yard touchdown against South Alabama.

FINALANALYSIS ‘Texas State is averaging 36.5 points per game the past
three seasons under Kinne and is returning nearly all of its skill-position
players on offense. But Texas State can’t truly contend on offense alone, or
it would have done so already. Final records of 8-5 in 2023, 8-5 in 2024
and 7-6 in 2025 prove that a potent offense can net a winning record,

but an undisciplined defense will prevent any contention for a conference
championship. If the changes made on defense work, the Bobcats will
compete for a Pac-12 championship in 2026.

UTAH STATE AGGIES


===== Utah State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 92 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 6-7, 4-4 MWC

OFFENSE ‘The Aggies are likely turning to a familiar face to orchestrate the
offense. Quarterback McCae Hillstead has transferred back to Utah State
after two seasons at BYU. The junior played a lot as a true freshman for
the Aggies and threw for 399 yards at home against James Madison.

USU lost two solid performers at wide receiver but does return Swiss
Army knife Anthony Garcia, who produced seven touchdowns in 2025.
The Aggies have brought in Eli Wood, who finished strong last season at
Virginia, and slot target Javon Robinson (Georgia State) from the transfer
portal to shore up that position. Robinson caught 58 passes a year ago.

The Aggies, who averaged 30.9 points and 409.5 total yards last season,
return a proven commodity in running back Javen Jacobs, who accounted
for 1,058 all-purpose yards and eight TDs as a junior.

USU head coach Bronco Mendenhall has brought in a familiar face at
offensive coordinator in Robert Anae, who has worked with Mendenhall
for 15 seasons, to replace Kevin McGiven, who departed to Utah.

DEFENSE ‘There is some continuity on defense for the Aggies, who will
need to improve on that side of the ball after allowing 440.1 yards — last
in the Mountain West — and 28.7 points per outing last season.

Defensive coordinator Nick Howell is back, and the Aggies also
returned a pair of honorable mention All-Mountain West honorees in
safety Brevin Hamblin and linebacker Bronson Olevao Jr. [he other three
returning starters are defensive linemen Tyree Morris and Carson Tujague
and linebacker Chris Joe.

The Aggies have brought in some notable athletes from the portal,
highlighted by safety Chapman Lewis and cornerback Antonio Bluiett,
who was an honorable mention FCS All-American at North Dakota last
fall. Lewis started at Texas Tech in 2024.

SPECIALISTS USU has a new kicker in Kian Afrookhteh, who was an

honorable mention All-Sun Belt performer last year at Coastal Carolina.
USU returns punter Landon Rehkow, who averaged 42.9 yards per punt.
The Aggies have a proven commodity at long-snapper in 2024 freshman

All-American Hyrum Hatch. Jacobs is a weapon on returns.

FINALANALYSIS The Aggies, who went 6-7 during Mendenhall’s first sea-
son at the helm, will play two power-conference opponents on the road
— just like they did last season — in Washington and Utah. USU will
likely have to face Boise State twice in’26. Making a return trip to a bowl
game will be a challenge. USU beat only one opponent that went bowling
a season ago and will likely need to defeat three 2025 bowl teams.

WASHINGTON STATE COUGARS


===== Washington State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 88 | PAC-12 PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6

ed

A

OFFENSE In 2026, Washington State’s offense could shift back to the
high-flying approach that fans across the country have come to recognize.

But many of the faces behind the operation will be new. WSU’s quarter-
back could be rising redshirt sophomore Caden Pinnick, a transfer from
FCS UC Davis, where he put up gaudy numbers and won the Big Sky
Freshman of the Year award. Or it could be returner Owen Eshelman, a
redshirt freshman, who has also impressed during spring practices.

The good news for the Cougars is that no matter who starts, he’ll have
some experienced teammates around him. WSU returns a number of ex-
perienced offensive linemen — left tackle Ashton Tripp, left guard Jonny
Lester, center Kyle Martin, right guard Noah Dunham, right tackle Jaylin
Caldwell — plus a transfer in veteran Maximus McCree. That group will
help carve out lanes for the running back trio of Kirby Vorhees, Maxwell

Woods and Leo Pulalasi, who combined for 212 carries for 1,006 yards

and five touchdowns last season, the majority coming from Vorhees.

DEFENSE ‘The Cougars’ defense will likely sink or swim based on the

performance of the line. They lost a lot at that position over the offseason,
including six key players who followed former head coach Jimmy Rogers
to Lowa State, but they may have replaced much of that production via the
transfer portal.

WSU can likely count on a consistent pass rush from ends Matyus
McLain (42 tackles, 13.5 for a loss, 4.5 sacks, one forced fumble at FCS
Idaho last year) and Linus Zunk (15 tackles, 3.5 for a loss, three sacks at
Vanderbilt last year), but the Cougars have less experience on the interior.
They will roll out a combination of Kaden Beatty, Purdue transfer Damar-

jhe Lewis, Marshall transfer Paul Hutson III and FCS Eastern Washing-

ton transfer Jirah Leaupepetele.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Jack Stevens is back after his WSU debut season last
fall, when he connected on 16-of-19 field goals and made all 33 of his ex-

tra points. Punter Max Dean, a transfer from New Mexico State, totaled
55 punts for an average of 42.3 yards for the Aggies last season. Look for
Florida transfer wideout Tank Hawkins to return kickoffs and/or punts.

FINALANALYSIS After another offseason of big roster turnover at WSU,

the Cougars’ offense will look more familiar. In the first year of the rebuilt
Pac-12, new head coach Kirby Moore will guide an offense replete with
talent, even if there are some unanswered questions at the QB spot. De-
pending on how the Cougs’ defense fares, this team could compete for a
high finish in the new conference.

===== PAGE 178 =====
ALABAMA CRIMSON TIDE


===== Alabama =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 15 | SEC PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 11-4, 7-1 SEC

OFFENSE Remember when Ryan Williams was a 17-year-old phenom ear-
ly in the 2024 season? Now he’s a junior and Alabama's most experienced
returning offensive player — and he’s looking for a fresh start. He’s now
Ryan Coleman-Williams and wears a new jersey number (1) after a rocky
sophomore season marred by one of the nation’s highest drop rates.

He'll have a different quarterback throwing him passes this season,
with fourth-year Austin Mack and second-year Keelon Russell in a
competition to replace Ty Simpson. But it’s Alabama’s offensive line and
running game where the focus lies after the Crimson Tide averaged 3.4
yards per carry in 2025, their third-worst mark since 1970. The offensive
line, which lost Kadyn Proctor and Parker Brailsford to the NFL draft,
is leaning on 2025 5-star recruit Michael Carroll to anchor a group that
dipped heavily into the portal this offseason for help.

Alabama is also sorting through several options in its backfield, which

is led by bruising Daniel Hill and added 5-star freshman EJ Crowell.

DEFENSE If you're doing some early scouting for the 2027 NFL Draft,
Alabama's defense features at least two players who could be first-round

picks next year — outside linebacker Yhonzae Pierre and cornerback Za-
bien Brown. The decision by Pierre to return for his fourth season bolsters
a front that suffered significant losses to the draft or transfer portal.

The Tide’s defense was overpowered in a Rose Bowl loss to Indiana,
prompting a search for bigger bodies. Oregon's Terrance Green (6-foot-5,
319) and USC’s Devan Thompkins (6-foot-5, 298) are the headliners of
the portal class, and Alabama needs an impact from Virginia Tech trans-
fer Caleb Woodson at an otherwise inexperienced inside linebacker spot.

In contrast, the secondary remains almost intact, with plenty of experi-

ence at safety in Bray Hubbard (fourth year) and Keon Sabb (fifth year).

SPECIALISTS Special teams struggles proved costly last season, including
some key missed kicks by Conor Talty. Talty decided to remain at Ala-
bama for a fourth season but is facing competition from Marshall transfer
Lorcan Quinn. The Tide will have a new punter — either second-year

Alex Asparuhov or FCS North Alabama transfer Adam Watford.

FINALANALYSIS Head coach Kalen DeBoer believes that his team took a

step forward in his second season, appearing in the SEC Championship
Game and winning a College Football Playoff game for the first time since

the 2021 season. But a season bookended by embarrassing losses to Flori-
da State in the opener and then to Georgia and Indiana left a sour taste in

the mouths of Tide fans, who are expecting better results in Year 3.

SCOUTING THE CRIMSON TIDE

“With whoever was going to follow Nick Saban,
there was going to be a measuring stick on the
toughness or the softness of the program.

The run-game statistics do not vouch for
toughness. They have to fix that. Losing [QB Ty
Simpson] doesn't help that. The top two run-
ning backs are gone, three of the top offensive
linemen are gone and [WR] Germie Bernard was
a special player. Losing all of them doesn't allow
people to play you honest so that you can run
the ball. People are going to be able to play two-
high safeties, create multiple looks and create
issues for them. ... Double-digit [point] losses
were never a thing [under Saban]. Now, they're a
thing. That creates concern, talking points and
narratives, which is tough to deal with because
the players see it.”
— An opposing SEC assistant coach

ARKANSAS RAZORBACKS


===== Arkansas =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 51 | SEC PREDICTION: 16 | 2025 RECORD: 2-10, 0-8 SEC

OFFENSE Can either of the two quarterbacks — returnee KJ Jackson

or Memphis transfer AJ Hill — help return the Arkansas Razorbacks
respectability in the rugged SEC? First-year head coach Ryan Silverfield
is banking on the pair, who have scant major-college experience. Jack-
son looked more effective in decision-making, mechanics and with the
schemes during spring drills, although Hill has more background in the
system imported by Silverfield and offensive coordinator Tim Cramsey.

Returning thumper Braylen Russell will compete with Memphis
transfer Sutton Smith for reps at tailback. CJ Brown, Ismael Cisse, Jamari
Hawkins and the talented but well-traveled Chris Marshall will lead the
receiving corps, and Jaden Platt returns at tight end.

Center Caden Kitler and guard Kobe Branham are back on the interior
of the offensive front, and the Hogs added large transfers in tackles Kavi-
on Broussard (Ohio) and Bryant Williams (Louisiana) and guard Malachi
Breland (Memphis) to form a potential starting five.

DEFENSE End Quincy Rhodes Jr. had a breakout year in 2025, while line-
backer Bradley Shaw and safety Miguel Mitchell have logged significant
time. Otherwise, there are plenty of newcomers for defensive coordinator
Ron Roberts in his base three-down scheme.

The Razorbacks expect big things from Virginia transfer tackle Hunter
Osborne and nose David Oke, while transfers Xadavien Sims (Oregon),
Carlon Jones (USC) and Trajen Odom (Ohio State) and elite freshmen
Danny Beale III and Anthony Kennedy Jr. will help populate the front.
The hybrid “Jack” role should be shared among Charlie Collins and others.

Shaw, a solid contributor last season, and Buffalo transfer Ja’ Quavion
Smith have taken the most linebacker reps with the first unit.

Khmori House has flashed the potential for a major impact at “Star.”
Finding depth at cornerback and safety was a priority for the new staff.
Transfers Jahiem Johnson, La’Khi Roland, Shelton Lewis, DJ Hairston Jr.
and Braydon Lee are in position for the most time at cornerback. At safe-
ty, Mitchell has received frequent reps with Carter Stoutmire (Colorado
transfer), Christian Harrison (Cincinnati transfer) and others.

SPECIALISTS The Razorbacks have no returning players in the kicking
game, but placekicker Max Gilbert, punter Gavin Rush and return spe-

cialist Sutton Smith give the coaching staff a potentially solid core.
FINALANALYSIS ‘The Razorbacks have a ton of ground to make up, and

Silverfield thinks a drastic improvement in turnover margin and a stron-
ger measure of team cohesion will help. A six-win season in Silverfield’s
debut, given the schedule, would display significant improvement.

2025 RESULTS (2-10, 0-8 SEC)

===== PAGE 180 =====

===== Auburn =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 34 | SEC PREDICTION: 12 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 1-7 SEC

OFFENSE Auburn hired head coach Alex Golesh from South Florida to

spark an offense that never got on track under former head coach Hugh
Freeze and hasn’t finished in the top 50 in scoring nationally since 2019.
Golesh brought in key staff members and offensive performers from USF,
including quarterback Byrum Brown, center Cole Best and wide receivers
Chas Nimrod, Keshaun Singleton and Jeremiah Koger.

Brown became an elite dual threat under Golesh, eclipsing 3,000 pass-
ing yards and 800 rushing yards in 2023 and’25 (he missed half the 2024
season with a lower-leg injury). Now he must deliver in the SEC.

Wide receiver projects well with the USF imports, as Nimrod and
others stood out in the spring. Auburn also has ball-carrying options with
leading rusher Jeremiah Cobb returning and talented Baylor transfer
Bryson Washington arriving. Transfers Jake Johnson (North Carolina)
and Jonathan Echols (South Florida) will compete at tight end.

Best, an all-league performer last year, anchors a transfer-heavy often-
sive line that also includes James Madison tackle Jo Simmons.

DEFENSE Golesh retained defensive coordinator DJ Durkin, a candidate

for the head-coaching role after serving as interim, to stabilize a defense
that showed promise but remains young in spots. Durkin has a center-
piece in linebacker Xavier Atkins, a first-team All-SEC selection with
nine sacks, 17 tackles for a loss and two forced fumbles in 2025.

Elijah Melendez will complement Atkins, and Auburn has depth at
safety with returners Eric Winters, Sylvester Smith and AnQuon Fegans.
Cornerback is more of an unknown, although UCLA transfer Andre Jor-
dan Jr. should help. “He’s been really impressive,” Golesh says of Jordan.

The defensive front is transfer-heavy with Cody Sigler (Arkansas
State), Da’Shawn Womack (Ole Miss), Nate Johnson (Missouri), Saint
Farrior (Appalachian State) and Walter Mathis Jr. (LSU) joining sopho-
mores Jourdin Crawford, Jared Smith and Darrion Smith.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Alex McPherson enters his senior season as a
Lou Groza Award candidate; he holds Auburn’s career record for field-
goal percentage (88.9). Punter Hudson Kaak enters his second year as the
starter, and primary kickoff returner Rayshawn Pleasant is also back.

FINALANALYSIS Golesh is relying on imports to get Auburn on track.
Brown is certainly capable, and if a remade offensive line holds up, the
Tigers should re-enter the top 50 nationally for scoring. If the defensive
line transfers hit and returning players keep progressing, that unit could
flourish, too. “You're meshing almost two teams into one, and you're look-
ing for leadership in every imaginable way,” Golesh says.

FLORIDA GATORS

i


===== Florida =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 26 | SEC PREDICTION: 9 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 2-6 SEC

OFFENSE Florida returns one of the SEC’s top tailbacks in Jadan Baugh,

along with potential rising stars in receivers Vernell Brown III and Dallas
Wilson. New head coach Jon Sumrall also inherited an offense that man-
aged only one touchdown in five different losses. Sumrall hired innovative
offensive coordinator Buster Faulkner from Georgia Tech to restore pro-
duction and excitement at a program accustomed to both during its hey-
day. Faulkner brought quarterback Aaron Philo, the Yellow Jackets’ former
backup, to compete with returning redshirt freshman Tramell Jones Jr. for
the starting job that opened up when DJ Lagway transferred to Baylor.

Receiver Eric Singleton Jr., who played for Faulkner in 2023-24 at Tech,
arrives from Auburn to headline a wave of portal additions. Ihe group
includes spring game standout Micah Mays Jr. (Wake Forest) and Cincin-
nati tailback Evan Pryor, a home-run threat as a runner and receiver.

Up front, transfers TJ Shanahan Jr. (Penn State/Texas A&M), Harrison
Moore (Georgia Tech) and Emeka Ugorji (Stanford), along with three-
year starting guard Knijeah Harris, are key to building a reliable line.

DEFENSE Florida could field its best front seven since 2019, when the Ga-
tors allowed just 15.5 points per game. Linebackers Myles Graham, Jaden
Robinson and Aaron Chiles, edge rusher Jayden Woods, tackle Brendan
Bett and end Kamran James anchor a talented, experienced group capable
of setting the tone defensively in the 3-4 scheme that defensive coordina-

tor Brad White brought from Kentucky.
End LJ McCray has star potential but must stay healthy after logging

only four snaps in 2025. Tackles Joseph Mbatchou and Jeramiah McCloud
look poised for Year 2 jumps after flashing as freshmen. Jacksonville State
transfer Emmanuel Oyebadejo adds size (6-foot-6, 303) and athleticism.
The secondary remains less certain. Redshirt freshman Ben Hanks II]
impressed in the spring, while veterans Dijon Johnson and Cormani Mc-
Clain must find consistency. Safety Bryce Thornton is a proven playmaker,

while Baylor transfer DJ Coleman adds needed physicality.

SPECIALISTS Patrick Durkin, who made 25-of-28 field goals in 2025, and
Alec Clark (46.5 yards per punt) followed Sumrall from Tulane. Brown

adds a dangerous return element.

FINALANALYSIS Sumrall’s teams reached the conference title game two
times apiece at [roy and Tulane and won at least 11 games three times.
He brought strength coach Rusty Whitt, a former Green Beret, to instill
toughness and culture. Florida's fourth head coach since 2017, Sum-

rall faces roster holes and NIL challenges, but if he’s the right hire, the

43-year-old could awaken a program he called “a sleeping giant.”

GEORGIA BULLDOGS


===== Georgia =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 2 | SEC PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 7-1 SEC

OFFENSE It’s Year 2 for quarterback Gunner Stockton. Last season saw
him serve as a difference-maker in a few big Georgia wins, but it also saw
him hamstring the offense at different points when he wasn’t at his best.
Stockton has to shrink the gap between his ceiling and his floor.

‘The backfield is loaded. Nate Frazier returns as the team’s lead back.
Chauncey Bowens, Dante Dowdell and Dwight Phillips Jr. have each
shown the ability to generate big plays.

At receiver, the Bulldogs grabbed Isiah Canion (Georgia Tech) out of
the transfer portal for experience, but they'll lean on a talented, explosive
group of youngsters, including Talyn Taylor and Landon Roldan. London
Humphreys brings big-game experience. A deep, extremely talented
group of tight ends will also carry some of the load.

The offensive line brings back four players with significant experience.
True freshman Zykie Helton and 6-foot-10 junior Jahzare Jackson also
stepped up this spring. [hose two are vying for the No. 5 and No. 6 spots
in the trenches, with Juan Gaston ready to play right guard or right tackle.

DEFENSE The Bulldogs bring back 21 defenders who played meaningful

action either at UGA or another program last season. Fourth-year junior
Xzavier McLeod and ultra-talented sophomore Elijah Griffin anchor the
defensive front.

The pass rush is a priority, and that’s where edge Quintavius Johnson is
looking to take a big step forward. Georgia brought in pass-rush tech-
nician Larry Knight as outside linebackers coach to get the best out of
Johnson and redshirt freshman Chase Linton, who had a breakout spring.

Inside linebacker features the three-headed monster of Raylen Wilson,
Chris Cole and Justin Williams. UGA is set at cornerback, led by Ellis
Robinson IV, but the safety spot alongside KJ Bolden is up for grabs.
Clemson transfer Khalil Barnes figures to start either there or at the Star/
nickel spot.

SPECIALISTS Drew Miller, a redshirt sophomore, is a big, strong-legged
punter who is praised for his hang time and work ethic. Peyton Woodring
is going into his fourth season as placekicker. He is as reliable as they
come, including in big moments. Wide receiver Sacovie White-Helton is
aiming to replace Zachariah Branch as the punt returner.

FINALANALYSIS ‘The Bulldogs were young a season ago, relying on a lot
of second- and third-year players. That group is now a year older, and as
we've seen since 2019, experienced teams thrive in this era of college foot-

ball. But the Bulldogs will go only as far as Stockton will take them.

with Oregon offensive coordinator Will Stein to bring a more exciting
attack to Lexington.

Former Notre Dame backup Kenny Minchey will start at quarterback.
The staff added three offensive line starters with multiple years of pow-
er-conference starting experience. Former starters at Oklahoma and Texas
will be among the featured options at running back and wide receiver.

If the staff’s gamble on Minchey, who narrowly lost the Notre Dame
starting job a year ago but has played few meaningful snaps in three col-
lege seasons, pays off, the offense could be Kentucky’s best in years. That
quest would be helped with breakout seasons from returning pass-catch-

ers DJ Miller and Willie Rodriguez.

fetaTey MISSISSIPPI STATE BULLDOGS


===== Mississippi State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 44 | SEC PREDICTION: 13 | 2025 RECORD: 5-8, 1-7 SEC

OFFENSE ‘There is optimism at Mississippi State, and much of it comes
courtesy of the Bulldogs’ ultra-talented sophomore quarterback Kamario
Taylor. The highest-rated quarterback recruit in program history flashed
last season, winning a pair of SEC Freshman of the Week awards on his
way to becoming the team’s starter by the regular-season finale and then
passing for 241 yards and a touchdown in the Duke’s Mayo Bowl loss to
Wake Forest.

The dual-threat signal-caller is surrounded by talent at the skill posi-
tions. Running back Fluff Bothwell looks for a fully healthy year after
injury derailed a great start to 2025. The receiver room has depth and
always seems to find playmakers under head coach Jeff Lebby. However,
a big question mark surrounds MSU's offensive line play after that unit’s
inconsistencies hampered State far too often last season.

DEFENSE MSU’s defense has been its biggest stumbling block the past

two seasons, finishing last or next to last in the SEC in total defense both
years. Lebby has brought back former State head coach and defensive
coordinator Zach Arnett to try to restore order on that side of the ball.
Arnett shined in his previous stint as defensive coordinator at State from
2020 through 2022. MSU finished fifth in the SEC in total defense in
each of those seasons.

To get back to that level of success, the Bulldogs will need an improved
pass rush — something that seems likely given Arnett’s aggressive ten-
dencies. They'll also look for stronger secondary play and have the pieces
to get it, including senior safety Isaac Smith and corner Kelley Jones, who

could very well play his way into the first round of the 2027 NFL Draft.
SPECIALISTS The Bulldogs bring back one of their most consistent place-

kickers of all time in senior Kyle Ferrie. Ferrie set State’s all-time career
scoring record for a kicker last season, and he now stands at fourth in pro-
gram history for career points. Junior Ethan Pulliam, a former two-sport
star at MSU, is back to punt for the Bulldogs. State has multiple speedy
kick return options, from the returning Anthony Evans III, to transfer
Marquis Johnson, to redshirt freshman Kolin Wilson.

FINAL ANALYSIS On paper, this is the most talented roster of the Lebby
era at Mississippi State, and the group certainly has the potential to take

another step forward after jumping from two wins in 2024 to five victo-
ries last season. If the offensive line is simply adequate (or better) and the
Bulldogs can improve to somewhere around the middle of the pack in the
SEC defensively, the cowbells could clang louder than they have in recent
years in Starkville.

Ce)

In eight wins last season, Missouri averaged 41.1 points per
game; in five losses, the Tigers averaged 12.8. Mizzou roared out of the
gate with an offense driven by Ahmad Hardy’s physical running and the
controlled passing of Beau Pribula, but the Tigers couldn't control the line
of scrimmage as effectively in SEC play, and scoring had already begun to
dissipate when Pribula dislocated his ankle against Vanderbilt.

Hardy and Jamal Roberts return after combining for 2,402 rushing
yards and 22 touchdowns, and they'll run behind three seniors in all-SEC
tackle Cayden Green and guards Dominick Giudice (who could move to
center) and Curtis Peagler. But new offensive coordinator Chip Lindsey,
most recently of Michigan, must build a passing game from scratch with a
new starting quarterback (Ole Miss transfer Austin Simmons) and three
new starting receivers. Simmons has a huge arm and beat out Trinidad
Chambliss in Ole Miss’ fall camp last season, but he threw five intercep-
tions in five games and then lost his job after getting hurt. Mizzou’s fate
could rest on Simmons cutting down on mistakes.


===== Missouri =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 29 | SEC PREDICTION: 11 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 4-4 SEC

Missouri allowed just 18.9 points per game last season, its best
defensive performance since 2015. ‘Third-year coordinator Corey Batoon
will have his work cut out for him in 2026, however: All 11 defenders
who started at least seven games are gone. Plenty of experienced players
return — end Darris Smith, tackle Marquis Gracial, linebacker Nicholas
Rodriguez and safety Santana Banner have all seen starter-level snaps.
But head coach Eliah Drinkwitz needed to strike portal gold.

‘The secondary appears well-stocked with cornerback transfers Chris
Graves Jr. (Ole Miss) and Jahlil Florence (Oregon), and linebacker
Robert Woodyard Jr. (Auburn) could be a like-for-like replacement for
2025 star Josiah ‘Trotter. Seven defensive linemen arrive via the portal, but
none are as proven as three high-profile departures (ends Zion Young and

Damon Wilson II and tackle Chris McClellan). If the pass rush regresses
too much, that could derail everything.

Placekicker Blake Craig suffered a torn ACL in last season's
opener, and his backups didn't have his range or reliability. Craig returns,
as does return man DaMarion Fowlkes, so improvement is likely over last
year’s poor special teams showing.

Mizzou has won 29 games over the past three seasons,
fewer than only Georgia, Texas, Alabama and Ole Miss in the SEC.
Drinkwitz’s program should be treated as a proven entity of sorts, but
between massive defensive turnover and Simmons all-or-nothing tenden-
cies, Mizzou faces quite a bit of uncertainty in 2026.

OKLAHOMA SOONERS


===== Oklahoma =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 8 | SEC PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 10-3, 6-2 SEC

OFFENSE Quarterback John Mateer’s 2025 season was clearly split into

pre-thumb-injury and post-thumb-injury segments, with the Sooners
failing to reach 400 total yards in a single game after Mateer underwent
surgery in late September. However, if an offseason of recovery helps Ma-
teer get back to pre-injury levels, the OU offense could be in for a bigger
improvement in coordinator Ben Arbuckle’s second season.

OU pushed some young linemen into action, and further progress is
expected from them, in particular left tackle Michael Fasusi. Even when
Mateer played well last year, the run game had lackluster results, end-
ing the year averaging 3.5 yards per carry. Still, the running back duo of
Xavier Robinson and Tory Blaylock possesses the talent to be productive
behind more experienced blockers.

The receiver group should see the ball spread to several players once
again, but slot Isaiah Sategna III is back as a key target, and transfers
Parker Livingstone from Texas and Trell Harris from Virginia Tech look
ready to make an immediate impact.

DEFENSE ‘The Sooners had one of the best defenses in the country, allow-
ing 15.5 points and 272.5 yards per game, which is what carried them to
the College Football Playoff. ‘The unit lost a couple of pivotal players but
returns leading tackler Kip Lewis at linebacker and sacks leader ‘Taylor
Wein at defensive end, with other experienced pieces up front.

The Bowen brothers, Peyton and Eli, are back in the secondary after
picking off two passes apiece last season. Across the board, depth is more
of a concern this year, but there’s room for young players such as defensive
end Danny Okoye or cornerback Jacobe Johnson to step forward.

Under head coach Brent Venables, defense has been the hallmark of the
program, and while replicating last year’s success won't be easy, the unit
looks capable of continuing that trend.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Tate Sandell is back after an All-America season

in which he was 16-of-18 on field goals from 40 yards and beyond and
8-of-9 from 50 and beyond. Punter Grayson Miller also returns after
averaging 45.8 yards per attempt. Sategna is a threat in the punt return
game because of his elusiveness in space.

FINAL ANALYSIS Venables is in a safer situation than a year ago thanks to
the playoff appearance. Building off that, the Sooners need the offense to
take a step forward under Mateer’s guidance and the defense to remain
close to last year’s levels. If those things happen, the Sooners should be
positioned for another playoff run.

Ole —‘OLEMISS REBELS


===== Ole Miss =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 11 | SEC PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 13-2, 7-1 SEC

Mess

OFFENSE After fighting the NCAA for eligibility this offseason, quarter-
back Trinidad Chambliss returns to play quarterback for Ole Miss in
2026. Chambliss went from backup to superstar last season, finishing
eighth in Heisman Trophy voting and leading the Rebels to their first
College Football Playoff berth. He was 10th nationally with 297.6 yards
of total offense per game. He will be flanked by one of the nation’s best
running backs, Doak Walker Award finalist Kewan Lacy, who ran for
1,567 yards and 24 touchdowns.

The Rebels must replace more than 3,000 yards in receiving production
and both starting tackles.

DEFENSE While losing leading edge rusher Princewill Umanmielen to
LSU was a tough pill to swallow, Ole Miss returns a formidable defensive
front headlined by a pair of homegrown talents entering their junior sea-

sons. Lackle Will Echoles — the Sugar Bowl defensive MVP — finished
with 11.5 tackles for a loss and five sacks, while end Kam Franklin had

nine tackles for loss and five sacks.

Versatile linebacker Suntarine Perkins returns as well; he has 179
tackles, 32 tackles for a loss and 18.5 sacks in his career. Defensive backs
Antonio Kite and Jaylon Braxton figure to headline a revamped secondary
that includes highly touted transfers Edwin Joseph (Florida State) and
Jalyn Crawford (Auburn).

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Lucas Carneiro is one of the top kickers in the
nation; he made two 50-plus-yard kicks and the game-winning 47-yarder
in the Sugar Bowl alone, and he hit 31-of-35 field goals overall. Punter
Oscar Bird averaged 46 yards per punt and pinned 14 punts inside the

20-yard line as a freshman.

FINAL ANALYSIS Ole Miss got a taste of life after Lane Kiffin in the CFP,

and the Rebel faithful have to like what they've seen so far in head coach

Pete Golding, the team’s former defensive coordinator. Ole Miss blew out
Tulane in its CFP opener before winning an epic comeback over Georgia
in the Sugar Bowl. The Rebels came up just short of an appearance in the
national title game, falling to Miami (Fla.) 31-27 in the Fiesta Bowl.

A No. 3 finish in the AP Poll and the return of Chambliss and Lacy,
among others, have set sky-high expectations in Oxford. The schedule

is loaded, even by SEC standards. Ole Miss’ SEC opener on Sept. 19
against Kiffin and LSU at Vaught-Hemingway Stadium will be appoint-
ment television and could set the tone for what might be another special
season for the Rebels.

SOUTH CAROLINA GAMECOCKS


===== South Carolina =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 28 | SEC PREDICTION: 10 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 1-7 SEC

OFFENSE LaNorris Sellers is back for another go-round, hoping to make
up for what was a disappointing second season under center. He'll have
new coordinator Kendal Briles and a rebuilt running back room to help,
but the offensive line is a major concern, especially with tackle Josiah
Thompson out for the year and transfer tackle Jacarrius Peak questionable
at best to play any games this year due to a lingering knee injury.

As he proved last year, Sellers can’t operate without line support (43
sacks allowed, 15th in the SEC) and with a playbook that forced him to
limit his running and hold onto the ball until his receivers could get loose.
The Gamecocks offense ranked 15th in the SEC in scoring and 14th in
rushing, and former coordinator Mike Shula was fired at midseason.

‘The assumption is that Briles will try to overcome the line’s weaknesses
with Sellers’ mobility and that he will maximize what the Gamecocks do
have — depth and talent at receiver and tight end. Nyck Harbor, Nitro
Tuggle, Donovan Murph, Mazeo Bennett Jr., Brady Hunt, Mike Tyler
and Sellers’ brother Jayden should all have a turn catching the ball.

DEFENSE In 2024, the South Carolina defense was one of the best in the
SEC. A year later, deprived of playmaking draft picks and having to be on
the field too much due to a misfiring offense, it ranked 10th. Worse, it lost
two draft picks and a four-year starter in DQ Smith. With Dylan Stewart
sitting out of spring ball recovering from a back injury, the early part of
the season for coordinator Clayton White's unit will be crucial.

USC has to be stronger up front and brought in several transfers to aid
that effort. Stewart’s back issues, and the fact that he was double-teamed
on almost every snap, limited him to 12 tackles for a loss and 4.5 sacks
last year. He can team with heralded prospect Julian Walker at the other
edge spot to create a fearsome pass rush.

Judge Collier and Peyton Williams (two interceptions in 2025) lead the

secondary, with true freshman J’Zavien Currence coming on.

SPECIALISTS Mason Love returns after finishing fourth in the SEC in

punting last year (45.1-yard average), but South Carolina needs a new
placekicker. Vicari Swain returned three punts for touchdowns in 2025.

FINAL ANALYSIS USC cratered under head coach Shane Beamer last year,

going 4-8 a year removed from a 9-4 season. [he Gamecocks return
Sellers but have a new offensive coordinator and a beat-up O-line. Their
defense was good but not great and lost some talent.

Expectations were high last year and went unmet. USC is a program
that has struggled to sustain success when reality says a winning season
should be the norm. A winning season should be the ceiling this year.

===== Tennessee =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 21 | SEC PREDICTION: 8 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 4-4 SEC

The 2026 season is a critical one for head coach Josh Heupel,
and adding to the stress, he’s breaking in a new quarterback. Joey Aguilar
keyed Tennessee’s offensive resurgence in 2025, but he is out of eligibility.
George MacIntyre might have the edge because he’s been in Heupel’s
system for a year. Colorado transfer Ryan Staub (427 passing yards, three
TDs, four INTs last season) has the most experience, and 5-star freshman
Faizon Brandon, a top-10 recruit nationally, has the highest ceiling.

Leading wide receiver Chris Brazzell II is off to the NFL after an
impressive campaign, but Braylon Staley’s return bolsters an otherwise
young receiving corps.

Running back DeSean Bishop returns after rushing for 1,076 yards
during an All-SEC campaign, and he'll help ease the new quarterback’s
transition. Tulane transfer Javin Gordon and Daune Morris comprise the
rest of the backfield. They’ll play behind an offensive line that returns five

starters and a number of contributors.

The defense was the Vols’ biggest liability last season. Heupel
fired coordinator Tim Banks after five seasons and brought in ex-Penn
State DC Jim Knowles as his replacement.

Chaz Coleman followed Knowles from State College and could be the
next standout edge on the defensive front. Tackle Daevin Hobbs brings
experience, but he missed much of the spring with a foot injury.

Amare Campbell is another addition from Penn State, and persuading
Arion Carter to return was huge for the linebacking corps. Edwin Spill-
man and Jadon Perlotte are back after solid freshman seasons.

Injuries plagued the cornerbacks last season.’ Ty Redmond took ad-
vantage of his opportunities and was third-team All-SEC as a freshman.
Transfers TJ Metcalf (Michigan) and DJ Burks (Appalachian State) bring

experience to the safety spots alongside returning starter Edrees Farooq.

Cooper Ranvier, an All-ACC kicker at Louisville, replaces
Max Gilbert, who transferred after starting the past two seasons. Punter
Jackson Ross returns for his fourth season after averaging 44.0 yards per
punt as a junior. Joakim Dodson, who returned a kickoff for a touchdown
vs. Illinois in the Music City Bowl, could provide a threat on returns.

The offense carried the Vols last season, but defensive
struggles led to a disappointing 8-5 finish. Heupel took a big step toward
fixing those issues by hiring Knowles and adding impact transfers familiar
with his system. [oo many questions remain at quarterback to know if the
offense can approach what it did last season. Couple that with a challeng-
ing schedule, and there will be very little room for error.

===== PAGE 192 =====
A M TEXAS A&M AGGIES


===== Texas A&M =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 9 | SEC PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 11-2, 7-1 SEC

OFFENSE The Aggies have no shortage of big-play ability. They need
consistency, though, and that starts with junior quarterback Marcel Reed.
Last season, Reed passed for 3,169 yards and 25 touchdowns. He rushed
for another 493 yards and six TDs. But he threw a dozen interceptions
and struggled in season-closing losses to Texas and Miami (Fla.). New of-
fensive coordinator Holmon Wiggins and quarterbacks coach Joey Lynch
are tasked with helping Reed boost production and stability.

The return of leading rusher Rueben Owens II will help. So will All-
SEC receiver Mario Craver, who had 917 receiving yards in 2025. Craver
is joined by Ashton Bethel- Roman, who also emerged in the second half
of last season, and Alabama transfer Isaiah Horton. Ihe 6-foot-4 Horton
fills a need for a big receiver who can win on 50-50 balls.

Center Mark Nabou Jr. is the only returning starter on the offensive
line. However, A&M restocked the line with four transfers with SEC
starting experience. [he most notable additions are tackles Wilkin Form-

by from Alabama and Tyree Adams from LSU.

DEFENSE ‘The secondary projects as the strength of the defense. Safeties
Marcus Ratcliffe and Dalton Brooks and cornerback Dezz Ricks are
proven. Ihe Aggies were third in the SEC in passing defense last season.
‘That’s the good news. the bad news is that they managed only three
interceptions, a total that must rise.

A&M was fourth in the nation in sacks last season, but SEC Defen-
sive Player of the Year Cashius Howell is gone. Fingers are crossed that
sophomore Marco Jones or transfer Anto Saka can step into Howell’s role
and lead another fierce pass rush.

Linebacker Ray Coney, a transfer from Tulsa, was 10th in the nation in
tackles. Perhaps he can help end the Aggies’ frustrating tendency to give
up long runs.

SPECIALISTS Illinois transfer David Olano should remedy an erratic kicking

game. His career field goal success rate is 86%, which would be a massive
upgrade. A&M has a history of explosive return specialists. Craver (punts)
and fellow wideout Terry Bussey (kickoffs) can continue that trend.

FINALANALYSIS More than 30 years have passed since A&M had consecu-

tive 10-win seasons. Ending the drought likely requires an improved Reed
and an effective offensive line. Head coach Mike Elko appears confident.
“I think we have a unique opportunity to double down on success,” he says.
“We have found ways, at times over the years, to hit one-year peaks. ‘The
next step for us is consistently being a top-10 team, consistently being a
playoff team, consistently being a team that finishes and gets to Atlanta.”

===== Vanderbilt =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 45 | SEC PREDICTION: 14 | 2025 RECORD: 10-3, 6-2 SEC

What does life without Diego Pavia look like for Vanderbilt?

That’s the big question with true freshman Jared Curtis expected to win
the Commodores’ starting job. Vandy pulled off an all-time stunner by
flipping the 5-star recruit from his commitment to Georgia, and early
buzz suggests Curtis is at least as good as advertised. But the fact remains
that Curtis has zero game experience at the college level.

Only six returning SEC players racked up more receiving yards than
wideout Junior Sherrill (784 yards, seven IDs) last year, and the Commo-
dores added a couple of impact transfers: Ja’;Cory Thomas from Old Do-
minion and Cole Adams from Alabama, the latter of whom turned heads
during spring practice. Tight end should be a strength with Cole Spence
returning, while East Carolina transfer Jayvontay Conner takes over for
Mackey Award winner Eli Stowers in the Commodores’ flex role.

An improved offensive line boosted the run game last season, but Van-
dy has to replace four starters. Pairing an unproven line with a freshman

quarterback feels risky.

Vandy’s defense features myriad familiar faces, with seven players
who have served as full-time starters at the Power 4 level.

Pass defense has never been a strength under head coach Clark Lea,
although Vandy took a step forward there last year. The rush remains a
concern with projected starting edges Miles Capers and Brian Allen Jr., a
transfer from Iowa, having combined for just five sacks in 2025.

A deep group of tackles and an experienced linebacker corps led by
Bryan Longwell should make for a competitive run defense. The second-
ary, however, is where Vandy could take a big step forward with Jordan
Matthews and Cayden Daniels emerging at cornerback, while Martel
Hight rejoins the defense full-time after playing some wideout in 2025.
Clemson transfer Ricardo Jones should improve the Commodores’ back
end after he tied for the ACC lead with six interceptions last fall.

Vandy’s special teams have ranked among the nation’s best
according to metrics like SP+ over the past two years, and that should
continue thanks to continuity and a solid pickup via the transfer portal

in punter Tyler Ebel (FCS South Dakota). Brock Taylor is one of college
football's top placekickers and has range to 60-plus yards if necessary.

Despite efforts to assemble an experienced supporting cast,
Vandy has questions to answer. A potentially improved defense could help
ensure that Curtis isn't regularly facing huge deficits, but there are likely to
be growing pains. If Curtis has a true freshman campaign similar to Trevor
Lawrence's at Clemson in 2018, that would change the outlook.

A\ APPALACHIAN STATE MOUNTAINEERS

Mowntoineers

===== Appalachian State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 117 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 5-8, 2-6 SBC

OFFENSE The Mountaineers finished in the bottom third of the Sun Belt

offensively last season, averaging only 24.2 points per game while turning
the ball over 22 times, including 15 interceptions. Io improve production,
head coach Dowell Loggains will turn to a new starting quarterback via
the transfer portal. App State added Malachi Singleton, Henry Hassel-
beck and Tommy Ulatowski to the roster, with Singleton and Hasselbeck
battling for the starting job. Singleton previously played at Arkansas and
Purdue, making 11 appearances for the Boilermakers last season. ‘The
redshirt junior’s dual-threat ability should boost App State’s offense.

The running game looks good with Jaquari Lewis back for his sopho-
more season after rushing for 618 yards and six touchdowns in 2025.

Wide receivers Sam Mbake and Alex Sanchez need to increase their
production after combining for 33 catches a year ago. Portal additions
Chris Lofton and Dazmin James will help in major ways. Lofton led the
FCS Big South with 934 receiving yards at Gardner-Webb last season.

The offensive line needs Tyriq Poindexter and Trent Ramsey to return
to starting form after injuries cut their seasons short a year ago.

DEFENSE After surrendering 416.6 yards per game, App State seeks more
consistency. Productive years from edge rushers Aiden Benton and Caleb
Sandstrom are essential. Ihe Mountaineers are also counting on lineback-
er Colton Phares, the team leader with 101 tackles and three intercep-
tions last season. He'll need help in the middle from portal additions Alex
Mitchell (Middle Tennessee) and Carson Sloan (FCS Samford).

The secondary needs a big season from cornerback Juan Berchal, who
gained valuable experience as a freshman. Junior Ja Den McBurrows can
be a playmaker if he is fully healed from the injury that cost him most of
last season. A host of transfers, including former ACC players Joe Red-

dish (Virginia Tech) and Khalil Conley (North Carolina), will be pivotal
to App State’s fortunes here.

SPECIALISTS In 2024, James London kicked six field goals of 50 yards or
more for FCS Murray State and was a third-team FCS All-American.
He was at Pitt prior to last season but left the team in August after losing
a kicking competition. Punting is questionable as sophomore Jaxson
Dunn, an Arkansas State transfer, has never played at the college level.

FINALANALYSIS A renowned giant-killer (hello, Michigan), App State

started its FBS existence in 2014 and reeled off eight consecutive winning
seasons. But the Mountaineers have had only one winning season in the
past four. To reverse the slide, App State needs instant impact from the
transfer additions and a strong return from injuries for several players.

COASTAL CAROLINA CHANTICLEERS


===== Coastal Carolina =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 119 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 6-7, 5-3 SBC

OFFENSE New head coach Ryan Beard has brought offensive coordinator
Nick Petrino with him from Missouri State. Petrino runs a pro-style
offense with the quarterback often under center.

No Chanticleers QB is returning from 2025. The contestants for the
job include redshirt freshman Missouri State transfer Deuce Bailey, Ar-
kansas redshirt sophomore transfer Irever Jackson, junior-college transfer
Tre Guerra and freshman Osiris Lopez, the high school touchdown pass
record-holder in Massachusetts. All four have run-pass versatility.

Running back Dominic Knicely, wide receiver Robby Washington and
tight end Zach Courtney are the most impactful returning skill-position
players. Kennesaw State transfer Clayton Coppock Jr. and tight end Cyrus
Ellison from FCS South Carolina State figure to be featured targets.

‘The offensive line features a number of offseason additions and must
replace both starting tackles, who left for Power 4 programs.

DEFENSE New defensive coordinator L.D. Scott, who also arrived from
Missouri State, believes that his defensive line will be the strength of his
base 4-3 unit. Oklahoma State transfer Chandavian Bradley, Samford
transfer Donovan Grayson and senior Ibrahim Diawara are expected to
pressure the quarterback at defensive end, while largely experienced trans-
fers will man the interior of the line.

Linebackers ‘Tray Brown, Wyatt Gedeon and Se’Von McDowell return,
and junior Marcellius Pulliam transferred in from Michigan State.

Cornerbacks Ja Marion Wayne and Myles Woods return after being
among the 2025 stats leaders in the secondary. FCS South Dakota State

transfer safety Jalen Lee is a welcome addition.

SPECIALISTS A pair of FCS transfers, Nico Cavanillas Alti (South Caroli-
na State) and Daniel Porto (Florida A&M), are vying for the placekicking
job. Both were 2025 all-conference selections. Punter Emile Sebafundi
returns after averaging 42.7 yards per punt with 15 inside the 20 and only
four touchbacks. Washington and receivers Colton Hinton and Karmello
English are punt return candidates. Arkansas transfer Krosse Johnson,
Breyahn Townsend and Knicely have elite speed for kickoff returns.

FINALANALYSIS Beard led Missouri State to a 7-5 record in its first season
at the FBS level. It’s a young staff, as Beard, Petrino and Scott are all

in their 30s. Beard has welcomed at least 56 incoming players, includ-
ing at least 38 from the transfer portal, 13 high school signees and five
juco transfers. He also has an entirely new coaching staff, many of them
following him from Missouri State. The incoming players and new staff
members will largely determine CCU’s fate this season.

===== PAGE 198 =====
GEORGIA SOUTHERN EAGLES


===== Georgia Southern =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 102 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 4-4 SBC

/ GEORGIA

OFFENSE Whatever other concerns the Eagles have about their new
quarterback, resilience isn’t one of them: After six turbulent years at LSU,
Texas A&M and North Carolina, 24-year-old journeyman Max Johnson
has endured just about every trial big-time college football has to offer.
Johnson still has another year to burn, which he opted to spend in his
home state. Even if he goes the distance as QB1, there should be plenty
of reps reserved for heir apparent Weston Bryan, who accounted for six
touchdowns on just 49 snaps as a freshman.

The skill positions suffered significant graduation losses. Outgoing
running back OJ Arnold left a 1,100-yard hole in the backfield to be
filled by a homegrown committee of backs in at least their third year in
the program. GSU invested its transfer portal capital on a fleet of Power
4 wideouts who never quite lived up to their recruiting rank at their
previous stops. One name locals will recognize is Josh Dallas, a breakout
candidate who missed nearly all of 2025 to a hip injury after hauling in a

team-high six TD catches in 2024.

DEFENSE First-year coordinator Mike Mutz is charged with reviving a
unit that ranked last in the Sun Belt in both total defense and yards per
play allowed. ‘The headliner is All-Sun Belt edge rusher MJ Stroud, and
Mutz also inherited established players at defensive tackle (Caleb Moore
and Latrell Bullard), linebacker (Brandon Tyson) and cornerback (‘Tracy
Hill Jr. and Chance Gamble).

Like Mutz himself, most of the new arrivals come from the FCS ranks,
including a handful of transfers he either coached or coached against
during a three-year stint at Stephen F. Austin. Safety Aaron Sears was an

all-conference pick at SKA, as was linebacker Rashon Myles Jr. at rival
Abilene Christian.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Tripp Bryant was reliable as a freshman, up to a
point, connecting on 16-of-16 field-goal attempts from inside 40 yards.
Beyond that point, he was just 2-of-5. Senior Alex Smith, a former Aus-
tralian tennis pro, is back for his fourth year as the primary punter. here
is some experience in the return game but not much proven juice.

FINALANALYSIS Clay Helton’s tenure has been nondescript, yielding a 6-6

regular-season in three of his first four seasons and a 27-25 record overall.
‘The good news in Year 5 is that the defense will almost certainly be im-
proved, if only because there’s not much room for it to get much worse. If
the Eagles settle on a quarterback and a playmaker or two from the portal
haul at wide receiver, a dark-horse run in the Sun Belt East is not out of
the question. Otherwise, the outlook is more of the same.

===== PAGE 200 =====
JAMES MADISON DUKES


===== James Madison =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 82 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 12-2, 8-0 SBC

OFFENSE After producing a string of Sun Belt Players of the Year at
quarterback, James Madison enters its first season under new head coach
Billy Napier with a four-way competition for the starting job. Transfer
quarterbacks Arrington Maiden (Memphis) and Davi Belfort (UCF)
both played sparingly as freshmen but came to JMU for an opportunity to
start. Camden Coleman redshirted last season but has the most starting
experience dating back to his time at FCS Richmond, while JC Evans
enters his third year in the program. Evans has thrown only 21 passes in
his career but was the Boca Raton Bowl MVP in 2024.

Wideout Braeden Wisloski should be a popular target for whoever
starts. He’s coming off a strong finish to last season, when he scored four
touchdowns over the final six games.

DEFENSE Linebacker Gannon Weathersby is back in a bigger role after

recording 57 tackles and a sack last season. Safety KJ Flowe is in a similar
spot heading into his junior year after coming on strong late last season
with a pick-6 against Coastal Carolina.

Amar Thomas is back after a great season at defensive end, and WKU
transfer Chukwunedu Okeke, should start, but JMU looked largely to the
lower levels to find defensive linemen early in the portal window. Division
II transfers such as Kevin Roberts, Jakolbi Wilson and Mason Purham
have an opportunity to play a lot up front, while Toledo transfer DJ Kelly

might step right into the starting role at corner.

SPECIALISTS Placekicker Morgan Suarez and punter Patrick Rea are back

after transferring to JMU last season and immediately earning the start-
ing jobs. Rea punted 48 times for an average of 41 yards with a long of
61 and placed 16 inside the 20-yard line. Suarez didn't have a great start
to the season but finished 14-of-19 on field goals and didn't have a miss
inside 40 yards.

FINALANALYSIS Coming off a College Football Playoff appearance after
only four seasons as an FBS program, the Dukes predictably had to
reload. Napier replaced new UCLA head coach Bob Chesney, who took
several JMU players with him hoping to replicate the national champion-
ship formula of fellow former JMU head coach Curt Cignetti at Indiana.

Napier returns to the Sun Belt after a failed stint at Florida, but he
previously dominated the conference in four seasons at Louisiana. Much
like his successful JMU predecessors, Napier’s first recruiting class focused
more on standouts from lower levels than Power 4 transfers. [here are
sure to be some growing pains, but Napier takes over a program where
winning the Sun Belt is now the expectation.

MARSHALL THUNDERING HERD


===== Marshall =====
~ NATIONAL FORECAST: 95 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 5-7, 3-5 SBC

OFFENSE Last season, first-year head coach Tony Gibson assembled an
offense that produced on par with the one that won the conference the
previous season. [he bones of that group are back, including quarterback
Carlos Del Rio-Wilson, who led the Herd in rushing and was an efficient
passer in a breakout campaign. Marshall’s run-first offense revolves
around its quarterback, but it utilizes multiple running backs. Look for
JoShon Barbie to assume a larger share of the carries, supplemented by
transfer TJ Lester (FCS West Georgia).

Receivers De’Andre Tamarez and Adrian Norton are back, and tight
end Toby Payne will start for the third consecutive season. Wideout Owen
Sweeney (FCS VMI) also joined the fold after a breakout season. Wide
receiver Christian Hamilton and guard Robby Martin are among the con-
tingent that made the move from West Virginia to Marshall.

DEFENSE The Herd had one of the worst defenses in the Sun Belt last sea-

son, and P4 teams still picked off several of their top players in the portal.
The front was decimated, as three of the top four sack leaders transferred
out, and the team’s top three tacklers graduated.

Javae Gilmore is the lone starting linebacker returning. Perhaps new-
comers Kelvon McBride (NC State) and Mickel Williams (UTSA) can
replace the lost pass-rushing production.

But the more pressing concern for new defensive coordinator Brad
Lambert, who's back for his third stint at Marshall, is in the secondary.
Only a handful of teams allowed more passing yards last season. Safety
Daytione Smith and cornerback Josh Pierre-Louis are the main holdovers
from a group that routinely allowed explosive plays. Ihe Herd took swings
on a slew of transfers, including Charles McCartherens and Eddie Pleas-
ant III from Buffalo. With Boogie Trotter (five interceptions) gone, more
playmakers will have to step up on that side of the ball.

SPECIALISTS Transfer Jorge Diaz Nicolas (Virginia) has big shoes to fill af-
ter All-MAC placekicker Lorcan Quinn transferred to Alabama. Nicholas

has yet to attempt a kick in college, and punter Cooper Heimbach is a true
freshman. Cornerback Brayden Hall, a transfer from FCS Central Con-
necticut State, could handle punt return duties. Kickoff returner Xayvion
Turner-Bradshaw is the only established starter of the group.

FINALANALYSIS Marshall was in the mix for a bowl game until the season

finale despite incredibly difficult circumstances. Now, Gibson has a stur-
dier foundation to build from and a talented quarterback in Del Rio-Wil-
son. [aking the next step as a team starts on defense, and that hinges on
how well the transfers perform.

.: J) OLD DOMINION MONARCHS


===== Old Dominion =====
—, NATIONAL FORECAST: 94 | SUN BELT EAST PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 10-3, 6-2 SBC

OFFENSE The Monarchs matched their best season in program history in
2025. But the core of that roster — on offense, at least — scattered when
the portal opened. Quarterback Colton Joseph, the Sun Belt Offensive
Player of the Year, was the most high-profile departure.

The Cure Bowl against South Florida provided a glimpse of what the
2026 team might look like. Head coach Ricky Rahne’s teams run the ball
at an above-average rate, and that held true in quarterback Quinn Heni-
cle’s second career start. He and running back Devin Roche each finished
with 100-plus rushing yards in the win over the Bulls. Though ODU
added a signal-caller with starting experience in Jaxon Potter (Washing-
ton State transfer), it looks like Henicle’s job to lose.

Henicle takes the reins of an offense that’s breaking in five new starters
along the line and trotting out a revamped receiver room. Rahne succeed-
ed in finding diamonds in the rough at receiver last offseason, and he’s
trying again with transfers JJ Harrell from Holmes (Miss.) Community
College and Zion Agnew from Div. II Lenoir-Rhyne.

DEFENSE ODU’s defense was lights-out last season. The Monarchs held

eight opponents to 10 points or fewer and allowed only 57 points across a
six-game winning streak to end the year. With starters back at every level
of the defense, that unit should again be among the Sun Belt’s best.

Safety and top tackler Mario Easterly returns along with run-stuffing
defensive lineman Chris Forbes, both of whom earned All-Sun Belt hon-
ors in 2025. Linebacker saw several departures, so the Monarchs brought
in transfer Montrell Chapman (Mississippi State) to man the middle.

Few teams created havoc at the rate ODU did in 2025, but some of the
most productive players from a unit that finished in the top 10 nationally
in sacks and turnovers are no longer there.

SPECIALISTS Nathanial Eichner took over kicking duties in November and

went /-for-10 on field goals and 19-for-19 on extra points the rest of the
way. ODU brought in transfer Andrew Nelms from FAU to compete. Ian
Brandt is back at punter, though his average of just under 41 yards was
on the low end in the Sun Belt. Roche led the conference in punt return
average last season, and he’s expanding his duties to kickoff returns, too.

FINALANALYSIS Rahne and the Monarchs desperately needed to put it

all together in 2025, and they delivered with a historic campaign. ODU
would be hard-pressed to repeat that success considering what it lost.
Still, the defense provides a high floor, and Henicle could be a reasonable
facsimile of Joseph under center. If that’s the case, the Monarchs should

ARKANSAS STATE RED WOLVES


===== Arkansas State =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 100 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 2 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-3 SBC

OFFENSE Head coach Butch Jones opted for a new look, hiring Garrett
Altman, formerly Vanderbilt’s quarterbacks coach, as offensive coordina-
tor. While the tempo is likely to be a little more deliberate, the outcome
of a four-man quarterback competition will also determine the approach.
Texas transfer Trey Owens is more suited to the pocket at 6-foot-5;
Ethan Crawford is a breakaway running threat; and Vanderbilt transfers
Drew Dickey and Jérémy St-Hilaire offer a mix of skills.

Running backs Devin Spencer and Kenyon Clay combined for 1,082
rushing yards last year and should find more room behind a transfer-bol-
stered line. Chauncy Cobb, the Red Wolves’ 5-foot-6 slot receiver, caught
73 passes in 2025, while Hunter Summers, a taller target at 6-foot-3, had
44 receptions.

DEFENSE The Red Wolves must rebuild their front after recording 39 sacks

last season, tying for the most in the Sun Belt. Six tackles and five ends on
the spring roster were first-year transfers. Sophomore Ryan Reese, the only
returning defensive tackle, emerged as part of the rotation last season.

Joe Drew, who started the last five games last year as a freshman,
switches from nickel safety to linebacker to play alongside Nigel Nelson,
who also played heavily as a freshman and finished with 54 tackles. Safety
AG McGhee is the top returning tackler (83 total tackles in 2025). Bran-
don Greil and Brandon Barnes were starters in last year’s secondary, and
Kennesaw State transfer Five Hamilton is in line for a key role. Arkansas

State struggled against the pass in 2025, allowing 245 yards per game.
SPECIALISTS Arkansas State has a trusted kicker in Clune Van Andel, who

made a game-winning field goal vs. South Alabama and was 21-of-27 on
the year. Cobb, who returned kickoffs for touchdowns against Arkansas
and Louisiana, was first-team All-Sun Belt as a return specialist after
averaging 26.9 yards per return. Fellow wideout Jaylen Bonelli takes over
on punt returns after breaking a 35-yarder in the Xbox Bowl. Daniel
Vuckovic, who averaged 42.3 yards per punt last year at Northern Illinois,
was the only punter on the spring roster.

FINALANALYSIS After losing four of its first five games in 2025, Arkansas
State took six of its last eight to finish 7-6 with a victory over Missouri
State in the Xbox Bowl. While the Red Wolves have a more manageable
schedule with one Power 4 opponent instead of two, they can no longer
lean on the two-minute mastery of former quarterback Jaylen Raynor, who
engineered six game-winning drives in the past two seasons. Arkansas
State’s hopes of playing in a fourth consecutive bowl game likely depend
on finding a capable successor to Raynor and rebuilding its defensive line.

LOUISIANA RAGIN CAJUNS


===== Louisiana =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 114 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 4 | 2025 RECORD: 6-7, 5-3 SBC

RAGIN:
CAJUNS

OFFENSE Quarterback Lunch Winfield came off the bench in late Sep-
tember to lead a wild, come-from-behind win over Marshall and went on
to take every snap from that point. A dual-threat type, Winfield typically
did more damage with his legs than his arm, but he still has plenty of
room to grow as a passer entering his first full year atop the depth chart.

When Lunch isn’t calling his own number, carries will be up for grabs
following the transfers of running backs Zylan Perry (Cincinnati) and Bill
Davis (Virginia Tech), a workmanlike combo who accounted for 3,264
yards and 28 touchdowns over the past two years. [he only remotely
proven playmaker still in the fold is junior wideout Shelton Sampson Jr.,
a former LSU signee on breakout watch after emerging as the Cajuns’ top
receiver in his first year in Lafayette.

Up front, the O-line projects as a strength after an injury-plagued
campaign that featured eight different starting combinations. Seven of the
10 linemen who served in that shuffle are back.

DEFENSE Nine of last year’s top 10 defenders in terms of snap counts
are gone. [he one holdover in that group, sophomore cornerback Brent
Gordon Jr., looks like a rising star after breaking up 11 passes as a true
freshman. The rest of the depth chart is murkier.

There are familiar faces along the front line, where veterans Fitzgerald
West Jr., Chase Edwards, Ashley Williams, Lance Williams and Maxie
Baudoin IV were all part of the regular rotation. Ditto for safeties Mau-
rion Eleam and Kody Jackson. Linebacker, however, could be in for a full
reset. [he top three candidates in the spring — Micah Johnson, Conor
McPherson and Ethan Veal — are all in their third or fourth year in the
program but have played almost exclusively on special teams to date.

SPECIALISTS Kicker Tony Sterner finished 20-of-28 on field-goal at-
tempts in 2025, including 3-of-6 from beyond 50 yards. The new punter,

Mason Golding, is the latest in a long line of Aussies to man the position.
JaCorian Norris took over kick return duties late last year and isn't likely
to give them up anytime soon after housing the opening kickoff against

rival ULM.
FINALANALYSIS Head coach Michael Desormeaux is a south Louisiana

lifer running a homegrown program: Only 15 players on the spring roster
arrived as transfers, among the fewest in the country. A 10-4 finish in
2024 stands as proof that it’s still possible to build a winner the (more or
less) old-fashioned way. The baseline, however, has been set by 6-7 records
in each of Desormeaux’s other three seasons. The challenge in Year 5 is
bucking the trend before it becomes the new normal.

LOUISIANA TECH BULLDOGS


===== Louisiana Tech =====
© NATIONAL FORECAST: 104 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 3 | 2025 RECORD: 8-5, 5-3 CUSA

OFFENSE ‘Three different quarterbacks saw significant reps in 2025, the
majority going to sophomore Blake Baker before a torn ACL ended his
season in November. Junior-college transfer Trey Kukuk presided over

a three-game winning streak to close the season. Baker went through
non-contact drills in the spring, but regardless of the pecking order, there
will be a role for Kukuk, who adds a dynamic element as a runner.

Last year’s top three running backs all moved on, leaving the position
without a player who has played a snap at the NCAA level. The top four
wideouts are gone, too, although senior tight end Eli Finley is a proven
target underneath. The revamped receiving corps includes 2025 injury
casualty Jay Wilkerson; FCS Eastern Kentucky transfer Marcus Calwise
Jr; and Oklahoma State transfer Talyn Shettron, a once-touted recruit.

Up front, left tackle Hayden Christman has logged 24 consecutive

starts for an oftensive line that allowed the fewest sacks in Conference

USA last season.

DEFENSE ‘The Bulldogs feasted on takeaways in 2025, tying for second na-
tionally with 22 interceptions. [he bad news: The majority of those picks
left with safeties Jakari Foster (NFL), Jacob Fields (TCU) and Michael
Richard (Michigan State). The entire starting five in the secondary will be
new, with last year’s backups vying for reps with an influx of transfers.
There are few concerns at linebacker, where sixth-year vet Kolbe Fields
headlines a deep unit. Fields, who came to Ruston from previous stops at
South Carolina and LSU, was on his way to living up to his billing as the
preseason CUSA Defensive Player of the Year last fall before a leg injury
cut his season short. His absence left plenty of reps for freshmen Jadon

Mayfield, Alonzo Jackson Jr. and Zheric Hill, who might be difficult to
keep off the field as sophomores.

SPECIALISTS Placekickers Drew Henderson and John Hoyet Chance fin-
ished just 10-of-18 on field-goal attempts in 2025. Their struggles opened
the door late in the year for true freshman Kaegan Kent, who will now
compete with Henderson. ‘The new punter, freshman Reuben Edwards,
arrived in the spring from Australia. There is no experience in the return
game, which means opportunities are there for the taking.

FINAL ANALYSIS After months of uncertainty and legal posturing, Confer-
ence USA agreed to a deal in April allowing Louisiana Tech to depart for

the Sun Belt for the 2026 season. ‘Thus ends a 13-year run in CUSA that

yielded mostly winning conference records but no titles. The move offers
the Bulldogs a fresh start in a more competitive league, but matching last
season’s 8-5 record in their Sun Belt debut figures to be an uphill battle.

===== PAGE 206 =====
SOUTH ALABAMA JAGUARS


===== South Alabama =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 115 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 5 | 2025 RECORD: 4-8, 3-5 SBC

OFFENSE South Alabama’s offense took a step back in head coach Major

Applewhite’s second season at the helm, but the Jaguars will have some
vital experience returning.

Quarterback Bishop Davenport (2,072 yards, 12 TDs, six INTs) started
all 12 games and finished second in the Sun Belt in completion percent-
age (68%). Running back Keenan Phillips is USA’s leading returning
rusher with 607 yards after the departure of All-Sun Belt first-teamer
Kentrel Bullock. PJ Martin (326 yards, two TDs) will share the load.

Leading wide receiver Devin Voisin is gone after seven seasons, and
Jeremy Scott transferred to TCU, leaving Anthony Eager and Brendan
Jenkins as the only two proven pass-catchers. The offensive line returns
one starter and welcomes some experienced transfer portal additions.

DEFENSE South Alabama lost a plethora of players from its defensive front
to the portal, leaving first-year defensive coordinator [odd Orlando with
a rebuild on his hands.

Emmanuel Waller and Stephen Johnson return after being rotational
pieces up front last year, while five defensive linemen transferred in, in-
cluding Mase Lewis (FCS McNeese) and Dylan Chiedo (FCS Furman).

The linebacking corps has to replace some key players, but Virrell John-
son (54 tackles, a sack, an INT) is back after ranking fifth on the team in
tackles, and Caleb Dozier returns from injury.

The secondary lost All-Sun Belt corner Nehemiah Chandler to Florida
State. Jayvon Henderson (14 tackles, five PBUs) returns to the other
corner spot after playing well opposite Chandler in his first season.

There is a lot of lost production at safety, but Cole Blaylock and Dallas

Young saw snaps in all 12 games and will compete for starting jobs.

SPECIALISTS Davis Little appeared in five games at placekicker, mainly
playing behind Hamilton DiBoyan. With DiBoyan gone, Little will com-
pete for the full-time job. Aleksi Pulkkinen averaged 40.4 yards per punt
and landed nine of them inside the 20. Phillips will likely take the kick

return job after returning three kicks last season.

FINALANALYSIS It’s an important Year 3 for Applewhite, who went 4-8

last season after leading South Alabama to a 7-6 finish and bowl win

in his first year. Count on the offense to improve. Davenport has the
potential to get back to where he was two years ago, and there might be
just enough experience back to comprise a good supporting cast. Staff
overhauls on defense, coupled with several new players at all three levels,
leave a lot more questions than answers, particularly on the defensive line
and in the secondary.
