SOUTHERN MISS GOLDEN EAGLES


===== Southern Miss =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 123 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 6 | 2025 RECORD: 7-6, 5-3 SBC

OFFENSE Southern Miss returns exactly one starter: seventh-year offensive
lineman Broderick Roman, who took every meaningful snap at center in

2025 in his first year on campus. Otherwise, “we're starting from scratch

on 99% of this roster,” says new head coach Blake Anderson, whose pro-

motion from offensive coordinator last December coincided with a mass

exodus via graduation and the portal.

At quarterback, a pair of new arrivals — senior transfer Ethan Hampton
and true freshman Hugh Price — split spring reps with last year’s backups,
Landry Lyddy and John White. If experience counts for anything, the
edge goes to Hampton, a sixth-year vet with 14 FBS starts under his belt
at Northern ILlinois. But Hampton barely saw the field in 2025 at Illinois,
and the 6-foot-5 Price is looming, literally and figuratively.

‘The biggest wild card among the new arrivals is Penn State transfer
receiver Kaden Saunders, a former blue-chip recruit more than two years
removed from his last live rep due to a nagging foot injury. At full speed,
he could be the most dynamic presence in the Southern Miss attack.

DEFENSE ‘The defense under first-year coordinator Joe Bolden will consist
almost entirely of transfers, none of whom has logged more than 500
snaps at the FBS level. [here is some untapped talent on hand, with
once-touted prospects who began their careers at Auburn (DB Laquan

Robinson), Ole Miss (DE Jeffery Rush), Ohio State (DB Jyaire Brown),
TCU (CB Champ Lewis), Texas (CB Ishmael Ibraheem) and Texas

A&M (DB Jordan Pride).
They’ll be competing for reps with an influx from the FCS and

junior-college ranks, among them 6-foot-6 edge rusher Amari Wilson
(Campbell), seasoned linebacker Andrew Martin (Stetson) and lanky
cornerback Hiroshi Carr (Morgan State).

SPECIALISTS Placekickers Creighton Wilbanks and Reed Harradine

return, but the pair was inconsistent, connecting on just 4-of-10 field-
goal attempts from beyond 40 yards. The new punter, Dusty Zimmer, is

a strapping Australian by way of Washington. FCS Chattanooga transfer
A.J. Little is most likely to succeed in the return game after housing a pair
of punt returns in 2025 for the Mocs.

FINAL ANALYSIS Few traces remain of last year’s team, which ran out of gas
in November after starting 5-0 in Sun Belt play. Anderson, who led his
teams to nine bowl games in 10 seasons as head coach at Arkansas State

(2014-20) and Utah State (2021-23), is no stranger to winning under

less-than-ideal circumstances. But trotting out what amounts to a brand-
new team in Year 1 is a new one even for him.

===== PAGE 208 =====
TROY TROJANS


===== Troy =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 81 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 1 | 2025 RECORD: 8-6, 6-2 SBC

OFFENSE When he’s healthy, sixth-year quarterback Will “Goose” Crowder
is a steady hand with all-conference potential. [he problem is keeping

him upright: Crowder has missed 14 games over the past two seasons to
assorted injuries. His understudy, fellow senior ‘Tucker Kilcrease, took the
majority of snaps in 2025 while presiding over a 5-2 record as a starter.

Troy needs more from the surrounding cast after finishing dead last in
the Sun Belt in both total offense and yards per play. A new coordinator,
Adam Austin, arrived in January from the highest-scoring team in the
FCS, Tarleton State. So did an influx of skill talent from the junior-col-
lege ranks, headlined by junior-college All-America running back Gavin
Griffin and former Ole Miss signee Noreel White, a wide receiver.

Up front, the ‘Trojans welcome back left tackle Elijah Prather, whose

sophomore campaign ended at midseason due to injury.

DEFENSE Nearly the entire starting lineup moved on, but there are familiar
faces. Juco product Donnie Smith broke out in 2025, earning a first-team
All-Sun Belt nod despite being limited to a part-time role in his first year
on campus. In Year 2, Smith is entrenched at “Bandit,” earning praise
from head coach Gerad Parker in the spring for his emergence as a vocal
leader. Former walk-on Justin Powe is entering his third year as a starter
at safety. Seniors Kam Curry, Luke Hodge, TJ Thompson, Jordan Perry
and Jabari Strait are all due for promotions to more prominent roles.
Newcomers include two SEC refugees via Arkansas (DE Kavion Hen-
derson) and Tennessee (S Marcus Goree Jr.), both of whom will compete
for reps, as well as a pair of injury-plagued transfers from Boston College,

DT Ty Clemons and LB Bryce Steele, looking to go out on a high note.
SPECIALISTS Punter Evan Crenshaw earned Sun Belt Special Teams Play-

er of the Year honors as a junior after dropping 29 punts inside opponents’

20-yard lines, most in the nation. Kicker is less settled, with four candi-
dates who have yet to attempt a field goal at the D-I level. The competi-
tion at the return spots is similarly wide open.

FINALANALYSIS Parker’s predecessor, Jon Sumrall, set a lofty bar by win-
ning back-to-back conference titles in 2022 and’23. Nobody was about to
confuse the 2025 Trojans with those teams, but they did take a big step in
the right direction, finishing atop the Sun Belt West. For all its losses, the
defense under third-year coordinator Dontae Wright can still be count-
ed on to hold up its end of the bargain. If the revamped offense follows
suit — a big if, largely hinging on Crowder’s health and consistency — a
fourth trip to the SBC Championship Game in five years is within reach.

UM

ULM WARHAWKS


===== ULM =====
NATIONAL FORECAST: 131 | SUN BELT WEST PREDICTION: 7 | 2025 RECORD: 3-9, 1-7 SBC

OFFENSE ULM ranked near the bottom of the Sun Belt in both total

offense and scoring offense in head coach Bryant Vincent's second
season, averaging only 323.5 yards and 16.6 points per game during a 3-9
campaign that represented a step back from a five-win season in 2024.
Returning starting quarterback Aidan Armenta (1,628 passing yards, 12
TDs, eight INTs) will try to improve a passing game that finished dead
last in the league in yards per game (160.4). Nic Trujillo (23 receptions,
368 yards, four IDs) returns at wide receiver, but much of the receiving
corps is largely unproven. Memphis transfer tight end Bryce Anderson
could help with the pass-catching duties.

ULM’s strength was running the ball, but the Warhawks must replace
leading rusher Zach Palmer-Smith. Former Louisville and Miami (Fla.)
running back Donald Chaney Jr. was one of a few transfer portal addi-
tions to the Warhawks’ backfield.

Returning starting tackle AJ Vinson will anchor an offensive line that
has to replace four starters.

DEFENSE ULM improved defensively last season, finishing sixth in the
Sun Belt in total defense (384.8 ypg). The Warhawks were more balanced
than they had been the previous season, allowing just 162.6 rushing

yards and 222.3 passing yards per game. But they do have some pieces to
replace on the defensive line and in the secondary.

The linebacker position has a few returning players who were part of
the rotation last season, including Noah Flemmings and Marcus Ross.
There are four new faces in the secondary, including corner Armoni Rue,
a former junior-college All-American who appeared in five games for

UTEP last season.

SPECIALISTS ULM might have a kicking competition on its hands. Max
Larson is back after missing 2025 with an injury. Jacob Chambless returns

for his second season, while Abram Murray transferred in from Cal.
Makenzie Ryan will hold down punting duties after averaging 38.8 yards

per punt as a sophomore.

FINAL ANALYSIS Vincent’s first season (2024) was promising, as ULM start-

ed 5-1 before losing its last six. Last season was similar, with the War-
hawks unable to capitalize on a 3-1 start, losing their last eight and going
1-7 in the Sun Belt. Vincent made several staff changes in the offseason,
particularly on offense, adding offensive coordinator Jesse Montalto in

addition to the portal pickups at receiver to try to jumpstart the passing
game. If the defense can continue to build on its 2025 gains, ULM could

get back in the bowl conversation.

THE BACK PAGE
Li : :

E evenge games come in all shapes and sizes. Typically, they are an op-

portunity for a team to avenge a loss from the prior season. But with the

proliferation of transfer portal player movement and coaching changes
— especially in the same conference — there are ample opportunities for
revenge on the 2026 college football calendar. BY KYLE WOOD

er

7 a .

| LSU AT OLE MISS (SEPT. 19) =

Games don't get much more spiteful than this. Lane Kiffin's SEC opener as
the Tigers’ new head coach — and as public enemy No. 1 in Oxford — is at
Vaught-Hemingway Stadium.

Kiffin left the Rebels amid the best season in program history to take the
top job at LSU. Then Ole Miss and head coach Pete Golding went on arun to
the College Football Playoff semifinals. Kiffin convinced several players and
coaches to follow him to Baton Rouge, upping the stakes for their return.

Remember the warm welcome Tennessee fans gave Kiffin in his return to
Knoxville more than a decade after he left? That might look tame compared
to how the Rebels receive him.

OHIO STATE AT INDIANA (OCT. 17)

Ahead of a highly anticipated bout between his alma mater and the Buckeyes in
2024, former Hoosiers quarterback Trent Green said: “It’s hard to say it’s a rivalry
when it’s been so one-sided.” Ohio State won that game to extend its win streak in
the series to 30.

Last fall, though, Indiana struck back. The Hoosiers toppled the reigning
champion Buckeyes in the Big Ten Championship Game en route to an undefeat-
ed season and their first national title. Indiana lost several stars from that storied
team, including Heisman Trophy-winning quarterback Fernando Mendoza. How-
ever, the Hoosiers stocked up in the transfer portal to make a run at a repeat.

The Buckeyes return a loaded roster of their own, and the folks in Columbus
have surely had enough of all the Hoosiers hype.

GEORGIA AT OLE MISS (NOV. 7)

‘The Rebels have quietly emerged as the Bulldogs’ kryptonite. Ole Miss is the only
SEC team other than Alabama to beat Georgia in the past five seasons. ‘That in-

210 | ATHLON SPORTS COLLEGE FOOTBALL PREVIEW

Ae A!

a IV

cludes when ‘Trinidad Chambliss and
the Rebels sent the Bulldogs packing
in the CFP quarterfinals.

Georgia has won back-to-back
SEC titles, and no FBS team has
more wins this decade. Still, the
Bulldogs have gone one-and-done in
their past two CFP trips and haven't
won a playoff game since the 2023

national championship.

TEXAS VS. OKLAHOMA (OCT. 10)
The Red River Rivalry doesn't need

any extra fuel. It got some anyway
when Longhorns receiver Parker
Livingstone — Arch Manning’s
roommate — transferred to play for
the Sooners. [exas has had the upper
hand in the series, winning three of
the past four, including a 23-6 win
in 2025. Still, Oklahoma got the last
laugh with a CFP appearance.

The hype for the Longhorns may
well have been a season early. After
opening 2025 as a title contender,
the 2026 team may have the goods
to deliver on those expectations. But
with quarterback John Mateer back,
and Livingstone having traded in
burnt orange for crimson and cream,
the Sooners should not be slept on.

DUKE AT MIAMI (FLA.) (NOV. 14)

There are layers to this budding
beef. The Blue Devils just won the
ACC, something the Hurricanes still
haven't done. Still, it was Miami that
represented the conference in the
CFP and advanced to the national
championship game. There’s also the
matter of Mario Cristobal replacing
former Canes head coach Manny
Diaz, who's in charge at Duke.

And here’s the kicker: Blue
Devils quarterback Darian Mensah
transferred to Miami, a move that led
to a lawsuit. Duke will surely have
revenge on its mind when it marches
into Miami, but the acquisition of
Mensah (and his top receiver Cooper
Barkate) ensures that the Canes have
a roster ready to run the ACC.
